2026-10-06  -  Displace: Quiet Velocity Bleed -- the gate no longer discards a gentle braking pulse (main.js v=229)
  - Data: Export All Logs 04:49:27Z (Native Sensor, after the decay/gate/curve fixes). Confirmed working: live decay 0/0, gate 8, curves start at 0,
    mode nativeSensor; position now HOLDS (04:49:18.4-20.0: PosPreCurve x 0.05 / y -0.18 / z -0.18 and Targets frozen, ZUPT ACTIVE, dwell up
    to 1333 ms), and the double-tap Displace Reset zeroed position at 04:49:15.6.
  - Remaining problem (measured): 04:49:22.6-23.4 a ~0.9 m/s^2 X push (ProcAccel x -0.79 -0.99 -0.98 -0.87 -0.86) built Vel X -0.55 m/s; the
    stop never cancelled it -- Vel X stayed -0.57 .. -0.50 .. -0.37 .. -0.33 with ProcAccel 0.00 while StationaryGate flickered (dwell 67 / 17 /
    67 ms, never the 150 ms needed), so PosPreCurve X ran 0.05 -> -0.74 m in ~1.6 s and the Target clamped at 29 u (Boundary X CLAMPED).
  - CAUSE (code, found by reading): the gate (gyro < 8 deg/s AND |a| < 0.6 m/s^2) zeroed ALL input while "quiet" -- including a gentle braking
    pulse below 0.6 m/s^2. A strong push integrates, a soft stop is discarded, the leftover velocity is never cancelled; the low-speed snap
    only catches < 0.15 m/s and the ZUPT dwell is reset by wrist wobble.
  - FIX: while quiet AND the speed is still >= the Low-Speed Velocity Snap, the input is kept (so the stop can cancel the velocity) and the
    velocity is bled with time constant "Quiet Velocity Bleed" (new slider, default 400 ms, 0 = old behaviour); below the snap speed nothing
    changes. Same rule in both A/B shadow pipelines. Position is never touched (hold rule unchanged).
  - TEST (Node, real three, mocked clock): a 0.9 m/s^2 push then a 0.45 m/s^2 stop with the gyro flickering 3 quiet : 1 noisy -- OLD leaves
    -0.527 m/s and -2.332 m of drift over the 3.5 s replay (reproduces the log); NEW ends at 0.000 m/s and -0.460 m (the real push+stop);
    a symmetric moving-gyro push/brake and a still phone are unchanged (0.5551 m both; 0.0000 m).
  - Not device-tested. Other observation, NOT changed: Y/Z gain is ~56-74 u/m (22 cm = 12.8 u), so any real centimetres read large; raise the
    Reference Distances if wanted.

2026-10-05  -  Fix: camera slider value boxes could not be clicked into / typed in (main.js v=226)
  - Report: "with lock camera pan unchecked, I still can't click into camera position slider text box to type in the value",
    then "make sure all camera side text boxes are fixed as well".
  - Cause: devPanel.js's click-to-type puts an <input> INSIDE the value readout (valueEl.textContent = ''; appendChild(input)).
    syncValue() -- called every frame by syncCameraPanelFromLive() for Camera X/Y/Z, FOV, Zoom, Yaw and Pitch -- only skipped
    the readout when `document.activeElement === vEl`, but focus is on the child input, so the test never matched and the sync
    rewrote vEl.textContent every frame, deleting the box the instant it was clicked.
  - Fix: syncValue() now also skips when the readout contains the active element or any <input>; syncControlDom() leaves an
    open click-to-type box alone too. One change covers all 7 camera sliders (they share syncValue); no per-slider code.
  - Note: the earlier 2026-09-27 "skip a slider that has focus" guard was written against the slider/readout themselves, never
    the child input -- any FUTURE per-frame writer of a slider readout must check the readout's children too.
  - Not run in a browser (project rule); checked by reading devPanel.js makeDevValuesEditable() and every writer of 'value*' readouts.

2026-10-05  -  Camera group: "Center World Origin" button (main.js v=225)
  - Request: "The camera controls group add a button ... to center the world origin in the browser screen space in regards
    to X and Y axes."
  - centerWorldOriginOnScreen(): a pure pan. The camera and its orbit target move by the same offset until the target is
    world (0,0,0), so the origin lies on the camera's optical axis = the centre of the browser view in screen X and Y.
    Viewing direction, zoom distance and FOV are unchanged. Updates cfg camera/target, the camera sliders, and pushes an
    undo state. Works even if Lock Camera Pan is on (explicit button action). Button sits under the Zoom slider.
  - Not run in a browser (project rule); the maths is a single translation, checked by reading.

2026-10-04  -  IK brought up to date with HANDO's CURRENT IK system (main.js v=224)
  - Request: "look at the ik system in HANDO. then implement it in the same way". HANDO's IK had moved on a lot since the
    first port (HANDO changelog 2026-10-04 10:37 AM - 5:27 PM), so HANDO's real source (src/main.js IK HAND POSE block +
    its IK Hand Pose DEV_GROUPS entry) was read directly and diffed against this file's IK block.
  - Ported: (1) HIERARCHICAL two-stage solver (stage 1 = best pair error only, stage 2 = influence-weighted joint choice,
    accepted by a line search only if the pair error stays within tolerance) -- replaces the single blended objective, so
    the All slider no longer changes whether a target is reached. (2) IK Influence rewritten: one % slider per EXISTING
    pose slider (Fingers, Pose Offset, Whole-Hand Rotation, Wrist Rotation / Splay / Bend, Elbow Bend, Elbow Side Bend,
    Forearm Twist) + All, each with a Min / Max RANGE BAR = hard window (typed numbers unrestricted; the pose slider's own
    range is only the fallback). (3) Pair Priority (%) slider on every pair (scales that pair's errors). (4) Overlap
    highlight: a pair title turns red when another ON pair uses the same Target or Source node. (5) Saved IK Influence
    preset picker (Save/Overwrite/Use/Rename/Delete/Export/Import; localStorage + Sync key listPicker_ikInfluence).
    (6) Fix: switching IK on/off now clears the "last value pushed to the sliders" memory so the sliders cannot keep
    showing the baseline while the hand is posed.
  - Removed from the solver (as in HANDO): the 14-slider influence set incl. Forearm Bend/Twist (+Tail) Rotation/Offset,
    Wrist (%) and Arm (%), and the 9 bone-offset DOFs. The bone-offset POSE sliders themselves are still in the Pose group
    (HANDO deleted them; not removed here -- user-facing pose controls) but IK never drives them.
  - Defaults as HANDO: Off 0, Rot 0, elbow/side/twist 0, fingers + wrist 100. Saved Sync values still win, so a synced
    sliderIkInfluenceRot / sliderIkInfluenceOff of 100 keeps the whole hand free. Old saved keys (Wrist, Arm, Bend*, Twist*)
    are ignored.
  - TESTED (Node, real three r169 + the real Draco-decoded hand GLB, run6.mjs, 14 checks pass): arm/whole-hand at 0 stay
    untouched; master 0 changes nothing; All 100 -> 30 keeps accuracy (2.390 -> 2.427 u); arm unlocked reaches closer
    (7.70 -> 5.33 u); typed windows are hard limits (-5..5 held; 400..500 beyond the slider honoured); priority 0 adds no
    pull; whole-hand unlocked lands the tip at 0.000 u. With whole-hand locked a 2 u move only gets to 1.07 u -- the same
    consequence HANDO documents. NOT tested in a browser (project rule): the range-bar rows, preset picker, red overlap
    label and priority slider UI. Range bars are watched by a 250 ms string-compare timer because the generic range-bar
    fires no event and the render loop stops when idle.

2026-10-04  -  Fix: Absolute rotation mode had the Y and Z axes switched (iPhone 17 Max Pro, Samsung Galaxy S26)
  - Report: "for iphone max pro and samsung galaxy 26 models, the y and z rotation axes got switched", then
    "that probably also applies to displacement for those models", then "for absolute mode, switch the y and z axes".
  - Cause found: those two models are the only ones whose saved per-model Rotation Mode is 'absolute' (all others
    are 'gyro'). Gyro mode routes the Y/Z controls one way; Absolute mode (computePhoneAbsoluteOrientationRawQuat)
    routed alpha (spin) to the Z controls and gamma (roll) to the Y controls, i.e. the other way round.
  - FIX (main.js v=223): in Absolute mode alpha is now gated/scaled by the Y On/Off + Scale controls and gamma by the
    Z ones. Displacement: in Absolute mode computePhoneResponsiveDisplacement() swaps the Y and Z controls (On/Off,
    Scale, Invert, Range, Curve, Reference) so Y's drive the Z displacement and vice versa. Gyro/other modes and the
    A/B shadow diagnostics are untouched. Not tested on a real phone (no device, no sandbox verification).
  - Consequence: the saved per-model Y/Z values for those two models now apply to the other axis (e.g. S26 rotation
    scale Z -1 now acts as the Y one) -- re-tune by hand if a sign looks wrong.
  - Noted, NOT changed: worldPosition displacement rotates the already Y/Z-swapped acceleration by the raw W3C
    orientation quaternion, which is a frame mismatch for any model using it; left alone because only these two
    models were reported.

  - Report: "phone responsive displace doesn't work. Even the y axis displacement we fixed earlier doesn't work"

    devicemotion/deviceorientation listeners attach, driven with synthetic events + a busy-wait clock): velocity/

    default camera is TOP-DOWN (y=114, fov 15), so the vertical (Y) displacement only changes the phone's size
    (11 of 112 units toward the camera); X/Z are the visible axes.
  - REAL GAP FOUND: the dev panel keeps INDEPENDENT per-device values for these controls (the user's tuned phone
    values live in the Mobile tab: damping 0.16, vel/pos decay 0/0, stationary gate 8 deg/s, axes ON, Z
    reference 1.15, rotation scale X -2), but every wired callback only listens to the DESKTOP element, so a phone
    silently ran on the Desktop values (damping 1, decay 3/0.08, gate 2) -- the same class of bug
    getRuntimeDeviceSuffix() fixed for the texture offsets on 2026-10-01 ("behavior is still dictated by desktop
    tab"). Heavy decay + a gate that flickers on a real phone + damping 1 = a short, snappy, cut-off motion.
  - FIX: PHONE_DEVICE_AWARE_ID bridge (main.js): on a TOUCH device the Phone Displace + Phone Rotation checkbox/
    slider/select values are read from the Mobile (portrait) / Landscape row (that row mirrors Desktop unless the

    (no touch) is unchanged. Range/curve widgets and the FineTune sliders are not included (own paths).

    gate 8, axes true/true/true, rotation scale X -2 (the Mobile tab's values); on a normal desktop load: damping
    1, decay 3, gate 2 (unchanged). NOT tested on a real phone.
  - Consequence to know: on a phone, a Desktop-tab edit of a control whose Mobile row is marked "Independent from
    Desktop" no longer changes behavior (that is what independent means) -- edit the Mobile tab, or untick
    Independent on that row to follow Desktop.

2026-10-04  -  Fix: Phone Responsive Rotation did nothing (2 independent causes)
  - Report: "phone responsive rotation doesnt work regardless of ik on off". Not IK -- found by reading the
    gates and the synced settings (data/processed/dev-panel-settings.json), then probing the live site.
  - CAUSE 1: it was gated by the HAND's "Tracking Enabled" master (Palm Rotation / Wrist Splay / Crop), which the
    synced settings had OFF. The gate sat in 5 places: computePhoneCombinedQuat() (desktop branch),
    computePhoneResponsiveAxisDeg(), integratePhoneGyroRotation(), initMotionInput() (sensor listeners were
    never attached unless tracking or Displace was on) and the render loop's continuousRenderNeeded (the loop
    went idle, so the per-frame sensor read/slerp never ran). Phone Responsive Rotation now depends only on its
    own On/Off + the Debug global override, exactly like Responsive Displace ("fully independent"); it also forces
    continuous rendering and requests the motion sensors at load / when its checkbox or the global override is
    turned on. Its On/Off now also gates 'absolute' mode (that branch never checked it).
  - CAUSE 2: the Phone Model Item Selector's startup-restore handler treated the settings restore as a model
    SWITCH: it captured the current control values under the wrong model's key and then applied the restored
    model's stored per-model snapshot over the just-restored values. The iPhone17MaxPro snapshot still said
    Responsive Rotation OFF, so a saved ON came back OFF on every refresh. The handler now skips both steps while
    the startup restore is running (phoneModelRestoreInProgress / first 8 s).
  - Verified on the LIVE site (v=221): after a plain reload cfg shows tracking=false, respRot=true; a mouse move
    changes the phone wrapper quaternion (0,0,0,1) -> (0.879,0.137,0.453,0.058); no console errors. NOT tested: a
    real phone's gyro/orientation stream (no device here). The stale iPhone17MaxPro per-model entry in the settings
    file is left alone (it self-corrects the next time that model is switched away from).

2026-10-04  -  IK: ported HANDO's IK Hand Pose; camera anchor = world origin
  - Direct request: "reference project Hando and implement the IK implementation just as that project
    does". Read HANDO's real source (IK NODES + IK HAND POSE blocks) and replaced this project's bone-
    level CCD / 6-DoF solver (+ bone stretching, single IK Influence slider, whole-model RootNode slide).
  - Solver (solveIkHandPose, ported in structure): the existing POSE SLIDERS are the unknowns --
    whole-hand rotation/offset, wrist bend/splay/rotation, the arm (elbow bend / side bend / forearm
    twist + forearm-bend/forearm-twist/wrist bone OFFSETS) and the curl/splay of only the fingers
    involved. Damped least squares (LM, 14 iterations, warm-started) over position (if "Displace" is on)
    + orientation (source axes or world axes + the pair's local XYZ) residuals, with a small penalty for
    leaving the BASELINE pose. Turning IK on captures the baseline (lazily, once startup has settled:
    3 s after the hand loads); turning it off restores it. Writing real sliders means the result is
    visible, saveable and tweenable. Re-solves only when a pair, a source node's pose or an influence
    changed (signature).
  - IK Influence: HANDO's 14 sliders (All / Fingers / Wrist / Whole-Hand Rotation / Whole-Hand Offset /
    Arm / Forearm Bend + Twist x Rotation + Offset + Tail), same defaults (Arm and Bend/Twist = 0 so the
    arm stays put unless asked). Each DOF may move (master % x its part %) of its slider range from the baseline.
  - New ARM pose group (HANDO's applyArmPoseToSkeleton, axes MEASURED by HANDO on this same rig):
    Elbow Bend (rForearmBend local Z), Elbow Side Bend (X), Forearm Twist (rForearmTwist local Y) and 9 bone
    offsets; 12 new pose keys (saved poses/tween/capture include them; older saved poses reset them to 0).
    HANDO's 3 shoulder keys are NOT ported (this rig has no shoulder bone). computeCurlAxisRefQuat() now
    follows the whole arm chain (elbow x twist x wrist net change since rest), measured at <= 0.04 deg finger-
    orientation drift for every arm axis (and unchanged ~0 for wrist / whole-hand rotation).
  - IK nodes now ride the SKIN like HANDO's: rigid nearest-bone + inverse-distance skin-weight blend of the 4
    nearest vertices + nearest-triangle (barycentric, each vertex skinned) position, all in the glTF's file
    space; plus the two forearm TAIL nodes (rForearmBend_Tail / rForearmTwist_Tail). Unposed they sit within
    0.13 u of the authored empties; with a curled fist 38 nodes follow the fingers. The expensive vertex/triangle
    scans are deferred until a node's matrix is first needed.
  - Pairs: "Pair On" checkbox, "Displace Target To Overlap Source", axes labels as HANDO, max 8; Debug > IK
    Nodes got "Pause IK Solver"; the solver status line (per-pair pos/rot error, iterations, ms) shows under
    IK POSING On/Off.
  - IK OFF = nothing runs (direct request): no solve, no baseline, no signature, no node following unless
    the node display (or an armed pick) is on; the skin binding itself is lazy.
  - CAMERA ANCHOR (direct request, AskUserQuestion: "pivot at (0,0,0), pan stays free"): controls.target now
    starts at world (0,0,0); applyCameraPreset() ignores a saved camera's stored target (tx/ty/tz) and sets
    (0,0,0) -- position/FOV/zoom still restore literally. Orbit/rotate, Zoom (distance) and Yaw/Pitch all
    pivot on it. Side effect to expect: with the camera aimed at the origin the hand (which sits ~Y33 up) is no
    longer centered; saved cameras need re-saving to frame it.
  - TESTED (Node, REAL three r169 + the REAL Draco-decoded HandiBonesB-IK.glb + the real functions
    extracted from main.js): arm-axis drift, node binding vs authored position, node motion under curl, solver
    convergence (default influence 0.095 u, far target with arm influence 0.19 u, arm DOFs only touched when
    their influence > 0, master 0% freezes everything); then on the LIVE deployed site: no console errors, 52
    hand nodes bound, IK on -> baseline captured + "P1: pos 11.11 u, rot 69.3 deg | 14 it, 38 ms", IK off ->
    baseline restored. NOT tested: the pair-card UI clicks / on-screen picking (project rule), and real
    gyro-driven source motion.
  - Differences from HANDO, on purpose: pairs are HANDYSET's own dynamic list (dropdown + PICK) instead of 8
    pre-declared slots + datalists; no hand2; thumb DOFs are curl+splay only (this project has no
    thumbBaseSplay key); bone stretching and the CCD solver are gone (HANDO has neither). main.js v=219.

2026-10-04  -  IK POSING: "IK Influence (%)" slider (how much of the model IK may move)
  - New slider in Hand Model > IK POSING with a live caption ("Fingers: free . Wrist: 100% .
    Forearm: 98% . Whole model: 0%"). Four tiers, each ramping 0 -> 100% in order as the slider
    rises: fingers are ALWAYS free; 0-33% wrist (rHand + rCarpal*: bend / splay / rotate);
    33-67% forearm (rForearmTwist/Bend); 67-100% the WHOLE model (RootNode). Default 66 (wrist +
    forearm free, whole model locked = the previous behaviour). Set low and the arm stays still;
    at the top everything can move.
  - A tier's freedom caps how far its bones may rotate from the pose they had before IK
    (freedom x 180 deg, ikClampToLimit, applied inside CCD and the 6-DoF refinement, so a
    saturated joint simply stops responding). ikActiveChain() keeps the leaf-first prefix of the
    chain that has any freedom, so a locked wrist means fingers-only solving.
  - Whole-model tier: the chain now ends at RootNode (it rotates within its limit) and
    ikTranslateModel() slides the RootNode BONE by `freedom` x the remaining gap (1 = lands on the
    goal). Translating a bone is safe here: no other system translates bones, so it doesn't
    fight the wrapper/clone placement code. ikBoneState now also tracks bone POSITION (restored
    exactly when IK is turned off).
  - Node tests (real three.js, real hand skeleton, a far target): 0% -> fingers move, wrist /
    forearm / whole model exactly still; 15% -> wrist free to <= 81 deg, forearm locked;
    50% -> forearm <= 90 deg, whole model locked; 100% -> lands on the far target (0.00 mm);
    reach never decreases as influence rises (525>516>516>493>493>296>0 mm); exact release incl.
    position; 170 random cases, no NaN. Live site v=217 with the user's pair: gap 27.92 @0% ->
    27.47 @40% (wrist +44 deg, forearm +6 deg) -> 0.00 @100% (RootNode slid).
  - Cost note: the whole-model tier puts RootNode (the entire skeleton subtree) in the chain, so
    its numeric Jacobian is the most expensive; only active above ~67%.
  - Several pairs with the whole-model tier on share the one RootNode translation: the last pair
    solved wins (the earlier ones can be pulled off).

2026-10-04  -  IK POSING: the whole hand/arm now swings toward out-of-reach targets
  - Report: "when I turn on IK posing, the rigged hand should change its pose accordingly".
    Probed the live site with the user's own pair (thumb pad -> phone side point, IK on, stretch
    off): the solver ran, but the chain stopped at the finger (<= 3 bones) while the target was
    ~30 world units away (hand ~8 units long) -> IK on only nudged the thumb ~3 units, i.e. the
    hand looked unchanged.
  - ikChainFor() now runs from the target's bone UP through finger bones, carpal, wrist (rHand) and
    forearm to rForearmBend (<= 8 bones) -- "move the target node as far as you can, and all the
    resultant other bones". Solving is STAGED: the finger bones solve first; carpal/wrist/forearm
    join only if the finger alone can't put the node on the goal (otherwise CCD nudges every bone
    each pass and two pairs on one hand disturbed each other: 61 mm error -> 0.000 mm with staging).
    Orientation refinement likewise tries the finger-only sub-chain first, then the full chain.
  - Bone stretching lengthens only the FINGER part of the chain (its topmost finger bone), never
    the arm (scaling the arm root would stretch the whole hand mesh); palm/wrist targets have no
    finger bone -> no stretch. The stretch factor is now (reach+gap)/reach and only applies once the
    chain is extended straight at the goal.
  - Node test (real three.js, real hand skeleton, thumb pad): goal at 0.5x / 1x the pad's distance
    from the arm base -> lands exactly (0.0 mm) with the wrist rotating 16 / 32 deg; goal at 2x -> the
    arm swings ~45 deg and closes 88 mm of a 402 mm gap (173 mm left with stretching on).
    Live site v=216 with the user's pair: pad 30.74 -> 27.47 units from the node, wrist +44 deg,
    thumb joints +56..66 deg. The remaining gap is the arm's genuine reach limit.
  - Measurement lesson: in the browser pane an unfocused tab pauses requestAnimationFrame, so a
    state read right after a toggle can show NO change; pump a frame (screenshot) between steps.

2026-10-04  -  Hand Model selector reorder, Debug > IK Nodes, Hand Model > IK POSING, X/Z displace match Y
  1. Hand Model selector: each row has a ⠿ drag icon (drag starts ONLY from the icon;
     pointer events so it works by touch). Order is saved through a hidden Sync-participating
     control (hiddenHandModelOrder) and applied to HAND_MODEL_OPTIONS in place.
  2. Debug > IK Nodes: "IK Nodes + Axes On/Off", Node Radius, Axes Length, Axes Thickness (Px),
     Render In Front. One InstancedMesh (spheres) + one LineSegments2 (axes) for ALL nodes.
     Phone: the model's own "IK Target Nodes" empties (6 of 8 phone GLBs have ~50; Galaxy S2 and
     Motorola Razr have none). Hand: HandiBonesB-IK.glb has 40 empties under "IK Target Points"
     -- but they are children of the SKINNED MESH node, not of any bone, so they would never
     follow the fingers. measureAndSetHandModel() binds each to its nearest hand bone (distance
     to the bone's head->tail segment at bind pose; checked offline on the real GLB: 40/40 land
     on the right finger/palm bone, tips on the last finger bone) and every hand clone gets a
     proxy Object3D under that bone. Hand2.glb has no IK layer -> zero nodes.
  3. Hand Model > IK POSING: On/Off, Allow Bone Stretching, Max Bone Stretch (x), then a list of
     PAIRS (ADD IK PAIR / DELETE PAIR). Per pair: Target (a hand node) and Source (any node) set
     by pressing PICK then clicking the node on screen (nodes auto-show while picking; nearest
     node within 36 px of the click; a drag is not a click); Axes = Match Source Node Axes or
     World Axes; Rotate X/Y/Z (Deg) = extra rotation in the target node's own frame;
     "Overlap Position (Bend Finger To Reach)". Target nodes show green, sources magenta.
     Pairs persist through Sync (hiddenIkPairs). Pair node ids are name-based, so pairs survive
     phone-model swaps.
     Solver (per frame, after the phone moved): HANDO's CCD (8-12 iterations, tip->root) bends the
     finger chain (<= 3 bones) until the target node reaches the source; out of reach -> the
     chain ends fully extended toward it ("as far as it can go"); with stretching on the chain's
     ROOT bone lengthens (up to Max Bone Stretch) -- root only, because scaling every bone
     compounds through the hierarchy and overshoots. Then a prioritized damped-least-squares
     refinement (position = primary task, orientation = secondary task in the position Jacobian's
     null space; kept only if position doesn't end worse and orientation improves). Overlap off
     = orientation only, matched exactly by rotating the node's own bone. Each frame the chain
     bones are first restored to the pose system's value, so IK never drifts; a bone rewritten
     by the pose system is adopted as the new base; turning IK off / deleting a pair releases
     the default pose exactly.
     Verified with real three.js r169 on the real GLB skeleton (node scripts): reach 0.00 mm,
     point + 20 deg tilt together 0.00 mm / 0.0 deg, no drift, exact release, stretch clamp,
     160-case sweep (all 40 nodes x overlap x stretch) with 0 NaN and 0 position regressions,
     two pairs at once 0.01 mm. NOT verified in a browser/on a device: the dev-panel UI,
     click-picking and rendering of the node spheres/axes.
     Interpretation made (the answer to "what does unchecked do?" was cut off mid-sentence):
     orientation ALWAYS follows the source; position is forced only when the box is checked.
     Easy to change if that is not what was meant.
  4. Displace X/Z now match Y in the synced settings: both axes enabled (they were OFF, which is
     why X/Z read 0.00 in every log), Range max 80, Reference Distance 1.15, Scale 0.6 (X keeps
     its inverted sign: -0.6). All displacement code fixes were already axis-generic. Other
     phone models keep their own stored copies (hiddenPhoneModelPerModelSettings).

2026-10-04  -  Bias-tracker learning clamp: the real cause of the stop "bounce back"
  - Full up/pause/down/pause log: every stop left residual velocity opposite to the
    motion (+0.11 m/s cycle 1, -0.21 m/s cycle 2, ~25-37% of peak speed) that nothing
    removed (gate inactive 1.2 s in cycle 2 because wrist wobble read ~10 deg/s, over
    the 8 deg/s threshold), sliding Y 38.56 -> 31.00 after the pause.
  - Root cause: the bias high-pass learned the push itself. Logged Bias swung up to
    0.24 m/s^2 during a push (Z column), shrinking the push, then relaxed during the
    brake, inflating it. A 1.5 m/s^2 / 0.5 s push + equal brake integrated to
    -0.08 m/s instead of 0.
  - CORRECTION: the previous entry ("Velocity snap default 0.03 -> 0.15") said the
    bias tracker was ruled out (~0.02-0.05 m/s). That synthetic sim used pushes about
    half as long as real ones; redone with realistic pushes it reproduces the log.
  - Fix: stepDisplaceBias() clamps the per-sample correction to
    cfg.phoneDisplaceBiasInnovationClampMps2 (default 0.10 m/s^2, slider "Bias
    Learning Limit (M/S^2)", 0 = old unclamped tracker); used by the real pipeline
    and both A/B shadows. Balanced push/brake residual: -0.08 -> -0.01/-0.02 m/s
    (extracted real function, node). Slow drift (<clamp) tracks identically. Cost: a
    static offset > 0.1 m/s^2 is learned slower (0.3 m/s^2 reaches 0.287 in 10 s).
    Not device-verified. Velocity snap stays at 0.15.

2026-10-04  -  Velocity snap default 0.03 -> 0.15 m/s (stop bounce)
  - Up/pause/down/pause test: Y bottomed at 21.80 then bounced to 24.27 during the
    pause. Displace Log: velocity flipped -0.44 -> +0.11 m/s across the stop (braking
    pulses integrate to more than the push), and the +0.11 persisted ~0.4 s (gate
    flicker + 150 ms dwell) -- the 0.03 snap ignored it. Default now 0.15; slider
    max 0.5. Estimated to remove the last ~1.2 of the ~2.5-unit bounce; the first
    ~1.3 (braking overshoot while the gate is still inactive) is not addressed.
  - Bias-tracker absorption evaluated (synthetic sim) and ruled out as the main
    cause (~0.02-0.05 m/s of the 0.11); not changed. Not device-verified.

  - Sensor Log + Displace Log now store EVERY field per line ({ts, parts:[{k,t}]})

    Unticking "Log A/B Compare" (or Target vs Rendered, ZUPT, etc.) drops that

    discarded). Standing rule: any new toggleable log field must be a part with

    flickers so the 150 ms dwell rarely completes and leftover velocity kept
    integrating. New cfg.phoneDisplaceVelSnapMps (default 0.03 m/s, slider
    "Low-Speed Velocity Snap (M/S)", 0 disables): while the Stationary Gate reads

    0.02 deadzone, then jumped by curve(0.02)*range. computePhoneDisplaceAxisUnits
    now feeds the curve the ratio remapped so the deadzone edge -> 0 and the
    Reference Distance -> 1 (edge step 1.04 -> 0.00005 units in a linear-curve
    model). Slight (~2%) slope change everywhere else.

    the real formatLogEntry source; snap and deadzone as replicas). NOT verified

2026-10-04  -  Stationary Gate threshold 2 -> 8 deg/s (the actual fix for the drift)

Direct instruction: "raise it. just fix this so i get what i want."
Follow-up to the entry below, which showed the wander on a motionless
phone is velocity-driven (gate active ~9% of ticks, so ZUPT almost never
zeroes velocity, and velocity decay is 0). Replayed the user's FULL pasted
log (handling 20.7-26.0s + still 26.1-43.3s) at the live decays (0/0):
  gate  2: first ZUPT 1.2s after stopping, drift after it ~15.5 Y units
  gate  6: 0.6s, ~3.9        gate  7: 0.6s, ~1.3
  gate  8: 0.4s, ~1.0, 7% of moving ticks mis-flagged stationary
  gate 10: 0.4s, ~2.7, 26% of moving ticks mis-flagged (eats real motion)
Chose 8. Applied in the cfg code default (2.0 -> 8.0), the synced Desktop
value (what feeds cfg on the phone) and the Mobile-tab value (display
consistency only -- Mobile values for this slider do not reach cfg).
Accel threshold (0.6), dwell (150ms), decays, curve, boundary untouched.
Caveats: replay holds 200ms samples for 12 ticks and starts the bias
filter cold, so the numbers are approximate; the 8 vs 6-7 difference is
small and 8 was picked for fastest first ZUPT with mis-flagging still
low. If real slow translations ever get swallowed, lower it toward 6.
Not tested on the device.

2026-10-04  -  Position held through stationary ticks; "return to center" traced

Direct report: after reaching Y ~38.5 the phone drifts back to Y=31
(38.48 -> 38.03 -> 37.16 -> 36.34 -> ... -> 32.20 -> 31.00) while held
still; diagnosed by the user as position decay pulling internal position
to zero, not ZUPT. Requested: ZUPT zeroes velocity but never position,
stationary gating never changes position, position decay must not
recenter the phone while stationary, internal position stays where the
physical movement left it, boundary fix / Native mode / A/B untouched.

WHAT WAS FOUND (code + the user's own sensor data):
- The ONLY line that modifies phoneDisplacePosX/Y/Z (besides the Reset
  gesture) is applyPhoneDisplaceSample(), `pos = pos*posDecay + vel*dt`
  (3 lines, X/Y/Z), plus the identical shadow-pipeline copy. posDecay =
  exp(-cfg.phoneDisplacePosDecayRate*dt); code default 0.08/s.
  `pos*posDecay` ran on EVERY tick including ZUPT/stationary ones, so
  ZUPT zeroing velocity did NOT protect position whenever rate > 0.
- BUT the synced settings now read Position Decay 0 AND Velocity Decay 0
  (Desktop and Mobile). At posDecay 0 that term is a literal no-op, so
  it cannot have produced the return. Whether the phone had a non-zero
  rate during the test cannot be determined from the data available
  (and Mobile-tab slider values never reach cfg for these controls, so
  the panel and the running app can disagree).
- Replaying the user's real still-phone Sensor Log (17 s motionless)
  through the exact Y-path math with decay 0/0 and ZUPT on: position
  still wandered to 0.14 m (max 0.24 m, ~6-10 output units on Y, the
  size of the reported move), final velocity 0.11 m/s. Gate active only
  9.2% of ticks, ZUPT 3.6%. With velocity decay 0 an unzeroed velocity
  integrates forever; this is a velocity-driven path (`+ vel*dt`), not
  position decay. With velDecay 3 the same data drifts only 0.03 m.
  Gate sweep (decay 0/0): at 6 deg/s ZUPT engages 72% of ticks and final
  velocity is exactly 0; at 8 deg/s 87%. At the current 2 deg/s, 3.6%.
  Replay caveat: 200ms samples held for 12 ticks (correlated noise) and
  the bias filter starts cold, so magnitudes are approximate; the
  mechanism, not the exact number, is the finding.

CHANGE: applyPhoneDisplaceSample(ax,ay,az,dt,holdPosition=false). When
the Stationary Gate classifies the tick as quiet the position-decay
factor is exactly 1, so gating/ZUPT cannot shrink position; velocity
decay and ZUPT's velocity zeroing are unchanged. Real pipeline passes
isStationary; the desktop cursor path is untouched (default false). The
A/B shadow pipelines apply the identical rule through a local holdPos
flag (not state.isStationary, which goes stale on a null sample) so
Existing vs Native still differ only in acceleration source. Internal
position is still never clamped. Verified (all checks): under the
default 0.08 decay a -0.166 m displacement decayed to -0.015 m in 30 s
of stillness before and holds at exactly -0.1660 now; ZUPT leaves
position untouched; decay still acts on non-stationary ticks; moving
back moves position back from the held value.
LIMITATION (stated, not hidden): only ticks the GATE calls stationary
are held, and the gate is the weak link on this sensor, so with a
non-zero decay rate most ticks on this phone still decay. The change
does not by itself stop the velocity-driven wander above.
DIAGNOSTIC: the Displace Log's PosPreCurve field now ends with
`(decay vel:<rate> pos:<rate>)` -- the rates actually in cfg on the
device -- so the next capture states which regime applied.
Not changed: gate threshold, accel threshold, decays, curve, boundary,
rotation, sensor input, ZUPTDwell logging. Not tested on a real device.

2026-10-04  -  ZUPT dwell investigation: stale gate flags fixed, dwell diagnostics

Direct report, with a Displace Log: "StationaryGate:ACTIVE persists for
well over the 150ms dwell but ZUPT stays inactive the entire time."
Instruction: inspect integratePhoneDisplacement()'s ZUPT, find why
phoneDisplaceZuptDwellStart isn't producing an active ZUPT, fix the
state handling, add a dwell-duration field to the existing Displace
Log, change no thresholds/decay/curve/rendering.

FINDINGS (read the code, then tested against the user's own data):
1. The dwell/ZUPT state machine is CORRECT as written. The dwell start
   is set exactly once on the first stationary tick, nothing else in the
   file writes it (grep: all 6 writes are inside the one gate block), and
   ZUPT goes active on any later tick where now-start >= dwellMs. A
   replica of the block engages ZUPT at 150-167ms of continuous quiet,
   zeroes velocity, and leaves position untouched (requirements 1-8 all
   verified). Nothing about timers, rendering, or logging resets it.
2. REAL BUG FOUND: three paths never WRITE the flags at all -- Displace
   disabled, Tilt mode, and any tick where the acceleration source
   returns null (e.g. 'nativeSensor' with an empty e.acceleration, which
   this device has been suspected of). The module variables simply kept
   their last values, so a StationaryGate:ACTIVE / ZUPT:inactive pair
   captured in the first <150ms of a stationary run stayed in the log
   forever -- indistinguishable from a dwell-timer failure. Fixed with
   clearPhoneDisplaceGateState() (clears dwell start/active/stationary/
   elapsed only -- never velocity or position), called at all 3 paths.
3. LIKELY EXPLANATION for the rest: the log samples every 200ms but
   devicemotion ticks far faster, so "ACTIVE" on consecutive log lines
   does not mean the gate held continuously in between. Measured on the
   user's pasted still-phone gyro stretch (41 samples, 29.0-37.1s):
   median |w| = 3.8 deg/s, min 1.75, only 12% of samples under the 2
   deg/s gate, longest run of consecutive 200ms samples under it = 2.
   A 150ms continuous window (9+ ticks at ~60Hz) essentially never
   occurs on this sensor at that threshold, so the timer correctly keeps
   resetting. NOT claimed as proven for the user's specific log (the
   Displace Log itself was cut off in the paste) -- the new fields below
   settle it on the next test.

DIAGNOSTICS (existing Displace Log, no new mechanism): the line now
reads  StationaryGate:<state> | ZUPTDwell:<now>ms (peak <p>ms / need
<n>ms) | ZUPT:<state>  (order changed so these 3 sit together). "peak"
is the longest continuous quiet run since the previous log line (reset
after each print) -- the field that exposes flicker between samples.
Also window.__debug.phoneDisplaceZuptDwell {elapsedMs, peakMs,
neededMs}.

SEPARATE FINDING, NOT CHANGED: every Displace slider (decay rates,
damping, gate threshold, ZUPT, reference) is wired with plain
wireSlider(), which only binds the Desktop element. Mobile-tab
"Independent" values for them (e.g. gate 2.7, PosDecay, Zref 1.15)
live only in devDeviceValues and never reach cfg -- only Rotation
Fine-Tune and Texture offset/scale have a Mobile bridge
(wireDeviceSliderMirror). The phone runs on the Desktop values (gate 2.0).

Verified via a standalone replica (all checks): continuous quiet engages
ZUPT at >=150ms with vel=0 and pos preserved, motion resets everything,
a gate breaking every ~150ms never engages ZUPT while peak records the
<150ms run, 200ms-sampled log lines read ACTIVE on most lines yet peak
stays under need, and tilt/disabled/no-sample ticks no longer leave the
flags frozen. Not verified on a real device.

2026-10-02  -  Decay rate mins to 0, scale-slider step:any, 4-decimal cap

Several direct requests in one round:

1. "set Position decay min to 0, and the velocity decay min to 0, set
   Position Decay input to 0" -- sliderPhoneDisplacePosDecayRate's and
   sliderPhoneDisplaceVelDecayRate's own min bound changed from 0.02/0.5
   to 0 in main.js. The Position Decay Rate's actual VALUE was also set
   to 0, both at the Desktop level (already 0, confirmed) and on an
   independent Mobile-tab override that was still at 12 -- found by
   checking the live settings file per CLAUDE.md S12s's own "check git
   state first" discipline, same per-device-override pattern as the
   earlier Z Reference Distance fix. Without catching this, the user's
   actual real-device experience would have kept using the old,
   non-zero Mobile value despite the Desktop default already being
   correct.

2. "for scale sliders such as texture scale or min max scale, dont ever
   cap me at increments. allow me to type whatever number i want,
   including small decimals" -- audited every "Scale"-labeled slider in
   main.js. Found most already had step:'any' (Phone Model/Rotation
   X/Y/Z/Displace X/Y/Z/Texture/Texture X/Y Scale); 3 did not (Pose
   Scale, Hand Scale, Ground Scale) -- added step:'any' to those 3 for
   consistency, after confirming via AskUserQuestion which specific
   slider the user's own "no." was about (a momentary misread on this
   round's part -- the "no" turned out to be about a DIFFERENT, deeper
   issue, below, not about Ground Scale).

3. Direct follow-up report: "for example texture scale curve then
   slider snaps me to whole numbers" -- despite sliderScreenTextureScale
   already having step:'any' throughout its FULL chain (main.js's own
   control definition, devPanel.js's buildSliderRow() DOM attribute
   assignment, wireSlider()'s parseFloat read, and the click-to-edit
   popup's own step-inheritance, confirmed line by line). Traced every
   step-propagation code path and found no code-level cause for THIS
   specific, already-step:'any' slider -- flagged to the user as likely
   a native mobile touch-drag precision limitation (a well-known,
   largely inherent constraint of <input type=range> on touchscreens)
   rather than a bug in this project's own code, pending a more specific
   repro (drag vs. click-to-type, which exact control) to investigate
   further if it turns out to be something else.

4. "but for all sliders, show and use only max 4 decimal places... dont
   show all decimal places if theyre just 0s" -- added a shared
   formatDevNumericValue(raw) formatter to devPanel.js (the engine file,
   genuinely generic capability -- not project-specific), exposed on
   `window` so main.js can reuse it. Rounds to <= 4 decimals and relies
   on Number's own string conversion to drop trailing zeros/unnecessary
   decimal points (no manual regex trimming needed): (1.5000).toFixed(4)
   -> Number -> String gives "1.5", not "1.5000"; (2).toFixed(4) ->
   "2", not "2.0000".

   Applied in buildSliderRow()'s own numeric readout (initial paint AND
   the live drag-update listener) and makeDevSliderBoundsEditable()'s
   min/max bound-label commit. ALSO applied in main.js's
   wireSlider()/wireDeviceSlider()/wireDeviceSliderMirror() -- these
   project-level listeners are attached AFTER devPanel.js's own, so they
   run SECOND and have the final say on what's actually displayed;
   leaving them unrounded would have silently overwritten the engine's
   own formatted text with the raw value on every single slider in the
   project. A new roundSliderValue() helper in main.js rounds the ACTUAL
   value fed into cfg (not just the display text) through the same
   formatter, satisfying the explicit "show AND USE" requirement -- the
   stored cfg value itself is now capped at 4 decimals, not just its
   on-screen text.

Verified via 2 standalone scripts: the decimal formatter (11/11 checks:
whole numbers unpadded, trailing zeros stripped, exactly-4-decimal
values preserved, 5+ decimal values rounded to 4, negatives and small
decimals like 0.001 handled correctly, non-numeric input passed through
unchanged) and confirmed via git diff that the 2 settings-file edits
touched exactly their 1 intended line each, with a remote-drift check
immediately before and after both. NOT independently verified against a
real device's actual touch-drag behavior -- per this project's own
standing sandbox-verification limitation, and specifically relevant
here since item 3's own open question is precisely about real-device
touch precision.

2026-10-02  -  Investigated "X/Z still stuck at boundary" -- config, not code

Direct, detailed instruction: port the Y-axis boundary-clamp fix (from
the previous 2026-10-01 change) to X and Z, treating Y as the reference
implementation, since X/Z supposedly still exhibited the old "stuck at
boundary" behavior.

INVESTIGATED BEFORE IMPLEMENTING, per standing discipline (never
execute a request built on an unverified premise): read
computePhoneDisplaceAxisUnits() (the shared curve/range/reference/
boundary-clamp function), computePhoneResponsiveDisplacement() (its 3
call sites), parsePhoneResponsiveDisplaceConfig() (the curve/range
parser), and applyPhoneDisplaceSample() (the integrator). Confirmed:
ALL THREE axes already run through the exact SAME formula, called
identically for X/Y/Z with zero per-axis branching anywhere in the
pipeline. The 2026-10-01 boundary fix was never axis-specific -- it was
baked directly into the one shared function, so it applied to X, Y, and
Z simultaneously from the moment it shipped. There was no "Y-only fix"
to port.

REAL EXPLANATION, found by checking the live git-tracked
dev-panel-settings.json (per CLAUDE.md S12s's own "check git state
first" discipline): Y's Reference Distance (1.15m) and Range max (52)
were far larger than X/Z's values, which were still sitting at the
CODE's own shared default (0.35m / max 20 -- confirmed identical across
X/Y/Z in src/main.js's own cfg declaration, ruling out even a
default-level asymmetry). Since the boundary clamp's release threshold
is mathematically always "internal position returns inside the
Reference Distance" (proven in the prior fix -- inherent to any bounded
clamp, not a remaining bug), a short 0.35m Reference Distance saturates
far more readily during ordinary real-world phone movement than Y's
generous 1.15m, making the IDENTICAL underlying mapping look broken on
the more tightly-tuned axes. This is a tuning/UX difference, not an
architecture difference.

Presented the findings directly to the user via AskUserQuestion rather
than silently either (a) implementing a fake "fix" to satisfy the
request's literal wording, or (b) silently doing nothing -- per this
project's own standing instruction to flag when a requested fix looks
unnecessary and let the user decide. Chose: match Z to Y.

FIX APPLIED (settings only, zero code changes): while investigating,
found the user had ALREADY live-tuned X themselves (to 1.3m / max 41)
during this same session, apparently in direct response to seeing this
conversation's own explanation -- left X completely untouched, not this
fix's business to override in-progress live tuning. Z was still at the
original defaults on both the Desktop-level control (0.35m / max 20)
AND its own independent Mobile-tab override (0.68m, found in
devDeviceValues.mobile) -- bumped both to match Y (1.15m / max 52) via
a small, targeted string-replacement script (not a full JSON.parse/
stringify rewrite, to avoid reformatting the entire 470KB+ file for a
3-value change). Verified via `git diff` that exactly 3 lines changed
and nothing else; checked for newer remote Sync commits both
immediately before and immediately after the edit (none landed either
time).

NO main.js CHANGES -- computePhoneDisplaceAxisUnits(), the integrator,
and every other Displace mechanism were already correct and
symmetric across all 3 axes; making a code change here would have
meant inventing special-case per-axis logic where none was needed,
directly contradicting the request's own "do not create separate
special-case behavior per axis" instruction.

2026-10-02  -  A/B diagnostic pipelines: Existing vs. Native Displace

Direct request (a detailed, pre-drafted spec): compare the Existing
(manual gravity-subtraction) and Native (raw e.acceleration) Displace
acceleration sources against the SAME physical phone movement, since
the 2 cannot be tested sequentially with a reproducible real-world
motion. Explicit requirement: both pipelines must process the identical
incoming sensor sample simultaneously, with completely separate
internal state, and neither may feed its processed output into the
other. Explicit non-goal: this is a diagnostic comparison only -- the
currently selected Displace Mode continues to drive the actual rendered
phone; nothing here competes with or replaces that.

IMPLEMENTATION: 2 new shadow pipeline state bundles
(displaceShadowExisting, displaceShadowNative), each a plain object
owning its own bias/velocity/position/timestamp/ZUPT-dwell state.
updateDisplaceAbComparison(e), called from handleDeviceMotion() right
alongside (never instead of, never chained after) the real pipeline's
own integratePhoneDisplacement(e) call, feeds the SAME `e` (and
therefore the same accelerationIncludingGravity/acceleration/
rotationRate W3C fields -- the same physical sample) to both:
stepDisplaceShadowPipeline() runs once per shadow state, with
computePhoneLinearAccelDeviceLocal(e) feeding the Existing state and
computePhoneLinearAccelNative(e) feeding the Native state.

Deliberately DUPLICATES (does not refactor into a shared function call
from) the real pipeline's own bias high-pass, raw deadzone/clamp
filter, Y/Z axis swap, Stationary Gate/ZUPT dwell logic, and leaky
accel->velocity->position integrator -- same PHONE_DISPLACE_BIAS_TRACK_RATE
constant, same filterPhoneDisplaceRawComponent() filter, same
cfg.phoneDisplaceStationaryGateDegPerSec/cfg.phoneDisplaceZuptAccelThresholdMps2/
cfg.phoneDisplaceZuptDwellMs thresholds, same cfg.phoneDisplaceVelDecayRate/
cfg.phoneDisplacePosDecayRate decay rates throughout, so the comparison
reflects a genuine difference in acceleration SOURCE only, never a
drifted-apart copy of the math. This was a deliberate choice over a
single parameterized stepping function used by all 3 call sites (real +
2 shadow): duplication keeps the real, already-shipped, already-
verified pipeline's own code path completely untouched, matching the
explicit "do not redesign the displacement system" instruction.

computePhoneDisplaceAxisUnits() (the curve/range/reference/boundary-
clamp mapping added in the previous fix) gained ONE new, OPTIONAL 6th
parameter: a boundary-flag write callback, defaulting to exactly the
original inline phoneDisplaceBoundaryX/Y/Z assignment when omitted. All
3 pre-existing call sites (computePhoneResponsiveDisplacement(), the
real pipeline) pass nothing new and are byte-identical to before this
change. The 2 shadow pipelines pass their own callback
((v) => { state.boundaryX = v }, etc.) so their own boundary state can
never contaminate the real pipeline's flags -- this is the ONE
genuinely shared piece of logic reused (not duplicated) between the
real and shadow pipelines, since it is already a pure function taking
its subject as an explicit parameter.

"Rendered" for each shadow pipeline is a diagnostic-only damped-lerp
value (state.currentX/Y/Z, same cfg.phoneDisplaceDamping formula the
real pipeline applies to _phoneDisplaceCurrentVec) -- computed every
tick but never written to phoneModelWrapper.position or any other
Object3D.

RESET: resetPhoneDisplaceBaseline() (the existing Displace Reset
double-tap gesture) now also calls resetDisplaceShadowPipelines(),
zeroing both shadow pipelines' velocity/position/rendered state --
matching the real pipeline's own already-established, hard-won
convention of deliberately PRESERVING bias across a reset (a 2026-10-01
fix found that zeroing a still-valid bias estimate CAUSES post-reset
drift rather than preventing it; the identical reasoning applies
independently to each shadow pipeline's own bias state).

DIAGNOSTICS: one new Displace Log checkbox, "Log A/B Compare: Existing
vs. Native (Diagnostic Only)" -- gates the WHOLE comparison block as a
single unit (not one checkbox per sub-field), per direct instruction to
avoid a new UI system and keep field names concise. Logs the one shared
raw native accel sample once (since both pipelines received the
identical reading), then each shadow pipeline's Pos/Vel/Target/Rendered/
Clamp state side by side via a new fmtDisplaceShadowLog() helper (kept
as one shared formatter specifically so the 2 pipelines' log lines stay
structurally identical -- any formatting drift between them would
undermine the whole point of a side-by-side comparison). Also added
window.__debug.displaceShadowExisting/displaceShadowNative (plain
snapshot copies).

PRESERVED, confirmed unchanged by this feature: native acceleration
input, the acceleration -> velocity -> position integration, bias
correction, the Stationary Gate/ZUPT, damping/decay, the range/
reference/curve mapping, the boundary fix from the previous change, all
5 Displace modes, and every existing control. The real pipeline's own
code path (integratePhoneDisplacement(), applyPhoneDisplaceSample(),
computePhoneResponsiveDisplacement()) has zero behavioral changes --
only the new, additive 6th parameter on computePhoneDisplaceAxisUnits(),
which defaults to the prior behavior exactly.

Verified via a standalone script (9/9 checks): confirms
computePhoneDisplaceAxisUnits()'s new 6th parameter preserves the
real pipeline's exact prior behavior when omitted; confirms the 2
shadow pipelines produce genuinely different Pos given different
(simulated) acceleration sources under identical timing; confirms
shadow boundary writes never touch the real pipeline's own flags;
confirms reset zeroes velocity/position/rendered while preserving bias,
matching the real pipeline's own convention. NOT verified against a
real device's actual dual-source sensor stream -- per this project's
own standing instruction, sandbox browser-pane verification is skipped
for this project entirely.

2026-10-01  -  Fixed Responsive Displace boundary stuck/hysteresis bug

Direct request (a detailed, pre-drafted spec): the virtual phone
appeared stuck at the Displace movement boundary -- once displacement
reached the clamp limit, reversing the real phone didn't make it move
back until much later. Explicit architecture requested: clamp the
FINAL rendered output only, never the internal displacement state
(phoneDisplacePosX/Y/Z, phoneDisplaceVelX/Y/Z) -- keep the existing
range/reference/curve system, keep the boundary (useful, prevents
off-screen drift), fix only the mapping if it's the thing causing
hysteresis.

ROOT CAUSE, found by re-reading computePhoneDisplaceAxisUnits(): `t =
clamp(|rawMeters|/reference, 0, 1)` clamped the curve's own INPUT
before the curve ever ran -- not the final output. Once |rawMeters|
(the raw internal displacement in meters) crossed the Reference
Distance, t was pinned at EXACTLY 1 forever, making `magnitude` a flat,
zero-derivative CONSTANT for the entire range |rawMeters| >= reference,
however large. Any push or accumulated drift (a known, separately-
documented limitation of this same integrator) that carried
displacement past the Reference Distance froze the mapping completely
-- the curve gave zero response to anything until rawMeters retraced
the WHOLE overshoot back under the Reference Distance. This matches
the reported symptom exactly, and is a genuinely different bug from
the already-fixed ZUPT/drift issues (those are about the INTEGRATOR;
this is purely about the MAPPING from integrator state to rendered
output).

FIX: t is no longer pre-clamped. The curve lookup itself still only
ever receives a value in [0,1] (curves aren't defined outside their own
authored domain), but a linear extension now continues magnitude past
the curve's own ceiling at the curve's own average per-unit-t rate, so
the underlying "ideal magnitude" is a STRICTLY INCREASING function of
|rawMeters| across its ENTIRE domain -- never flat anywhere. The actual
boundary clamp (to [0, max], preserving the pre-existing negative-min
floor-at-0 fix) is applied exactly once, as the explicit LAST step, to
this function's own local return value -- it was NEVER applied to
phoneDisplacePosX/Y/Z or the velocity state, and still isn't; those
were already correct (the integrator never clamped anything) and
needed no change.

MATHEMATICAL HONESTY, proven via a standalone script rather than
assumed or glossed over: the recovery THRESHOLD -- the exact rawMeters
value at which the clamped output starts visibly moving again -- is
unavoidably "rawMeters returns inside the Reference Distance," for
BOTH the old and new formula. This is an inherent, provable consequence
of what "Reference Distance = the real-world distance that maps to the
full output range" means, combined with wanting a useful clamp at all
-- it is NOT something either version of this fix can or needs to
change, and no further code change can make it otherwise without
redefining what Reference Distance means (out of scope; the user
explicitly asked to keep the existing range/reference system). What
this fix actually changes, and the real reason it resolves the
reported symptom: the mapping no longer has a SEPARATE, EARLIER,
completely flat dead zone baked into its own arithmetic BEFORE that
threshold is reached -- the underlying state keeps measurably
responding to a reversal throughout the whole overshoot (visible now in
the new Boundary/clamp-state diagnostic and the existing Target/
Rendered log fields), instead of giving zero signal of any kind until
the exact instant of release, which is what made it feel like a dead,
broken freeze rather than an expected boundary hold.

Added a new per-axis "at boundary" flag (phoneDisplaceBoundaryX/Y/Z),
set inside computePhoneDisplaceAxisUnits() itself (true exactly when
idealMagnitude has run past the ceiling and the clamp is actively
pinning that axis's output), exposed on window.__debug
(phoneDisplaceBoundary) and as a new Displace Log checkbox ("Log
Boundary/Clamp State (XYZ)", default on) -- satisfies the requested
"Boundary/clamp state" diagnostic. The existing "Internal Pos"
("Log Integrated Position..."), "Internal Vel" ("Log Velocity..."),
and "Output/Rendered Pos" + "Target/mapped position" (the existing
Target-vs-Rendered field) diagnostics from the prior Displace Log
turn already cover the rest of the requested list -- no change needed
there, just confirmed the mapping.

Preserved, confirmed unchanged by this fix: the acceleration ->
velocity -> position integration, the native LinearAccel input added
earlier the same day, bias correction, the Stationary Gate/ZUPT, all 5
Displace modes, per-axis enable/disable, the range/reference/curve
controls and their dev-panel UI, and every coordinate convention. Tilt
mode is untouched -- it bypasses this entire curve/range/reference
system by its own design (already documented) and was never affected
by this bug.

Verified via a standalone script (13/13 checks): confirms the OLD
formula is bit-identical (flat) across a 50x range of overshoot past
the Reference Distance while the NEW formula's underlying ideal
magnitude strictly increases there; confirms both versions release at
the identical threshold (proving that part is inherent, not a
regression introduced by this fix); confirms the boundary flag
transitions correctly; confirms the pre-existing negative-min
floor-at-0 behavior survives unchanged. NOT verified against a real
device's actual accelerometer stream -- per this project's own standing
instruction, sandbox browser-pane verification is skipped for this
project entirely.

Direct request: add on/off checkboxes to log processed accel into the

the range/curve mapping, ZUPT active/inactive, Stationary Gate active/
inactive, bias being subtracted, and displacement target vs. rendered

path, so gating it like real sensor hardware would silently disable it
in this sandbox.

2 of the 7 fields needed new module-level state, since neither value
existed outside a single function call before this:
- phoneDisplaceLastAx/Ay/Az ("processed accel into the integrator") --

- phoneDisplaceIsStationary ("Stationary Gate active/inactive") --
  previously only a local `isStationary` variable inside

verdict flips true the instant gyro+accel both read quiet, while ZUPT
(phoneDisplaceZuptActive) only engages once that quiet state has held
for the full phoneDisplaceZuptDwellMs window. The two can legitimately

excludes cfg.phoneModelOffsetX/Y/Z (the manual offset sliders) from
both sides of the comparison -- including it on only one side would
measure offset magnitude instead of damping lag, which is the actual
point of this field.

Also added 2 new window.__debug getters (phoneDisplaceIsStationary,
phoneDisplaceLastProcessedAccel), matching this project's own
established debug-exposure convention, alongside the dev-panel log
itself rather than instead of it.

present in the log line when their checkboxes are on, no empty log
line produced when all are off, the ZUPT/Stationary-Gate-disagreement

2026-10-01  -  Native Sensor Displace mode + per-mode dev-panel visibility

A pasted ChatGPT prompt asked to replace Displace's acceleration source
with DeviceMotionEvent.acceleration directly (the browser's own
gravity-excluded "linear acceleration") and the orientation source with
the closest available fused, non-magnetometer rotation, reasoning this
would be better-quality native sensor data than this project's own
manual gravity-subtraction pipeline. Mid-implementation, direct
correction: "well actually... make it a new displacement mode so we
dont erase anything" -- the request became additive, not a replacement.

Added a 5th Displace Mode option, 'nativeSensor' ("Native Sensor
(Browser e.acceleration)"), which reuses the ENTIRE existing
acceleration -> velocity -> position pipeline (bias tracking, raw
deadzone/clamp filter, the Stationary Gate, the ZUPT dwell/hard-zero
logic, per-axis Range/Curve/Reference) completely unchanged -- the only
difference is which raw acceleration reading feeds in. A new
computePhoneLinearAccelNative(e) function returns e.acceleration
directly (null-safe); integratePhoneDisplacement() picks it instead of
the existing computePhoneLinearAccelDeviceLocal() only when this mode is
selected.

Deliberately NOT implemented: replacing the orientation source used for
gravity removal. This mode bypasses gravity-subtraction entirely (reads
e.acceleration raw), so there's no orientation-quality dependency left
to fix for it, and the user's own revised, narrower request didn't call
for a separate fused-orientation system on top. The Generic Sensor
API's RelativeOrientationSensor (the actual closest browser equivalent
to Android's Game Rotation Vector) was identified as the right answer
IF that had still been in scope, but wasn't pursued given the scope
pulled back to "just a new mode."

Important context for why this is additive rather than a default swap:
the existing default (computePhoneLinearAccelDeviceLocal(), manual
gravity subtraction from accelerationIncludingGravity) was switched TO
specifically because a real controlled test on 2026-09-30 found
accelerationIncludingGravity rich/continuous/reliable while
e.acceleration had never actually been observed to perform well on this
project's own test device (see that function's own 2026-09-30 comment).
Reverting to e.acceleration as the default would have undone a real,
evidence-based fix. As an opt-in mode instead, it can be A/B tested for
real on a device without risking the already-improved default, and
reverting away from it (if it performs worse) is as simple as not
selecting it in the dropdown -- no code change needed.

Added a new window.__debug.phoneDisplaceAccelSource getter, per the
original prompt's own "add minimal diagnostic output showing which
source is being used" ask, scoped down to the one axis (acceleration
source) that's actually configurable now.

Separately, direct follow-up request: "and for displacement modes dont
show irrelevant sliders and inputs if the mode doesnt need it." Added
updateDisplaceModeRowVisibility(), hiding 18 Displace rows that Tilt
mode's own code path never reads at all (confirmed by tracing
computePhoneResponsiveDisplacement()'s tilt branch and
integratePhoneDisplacement()'s own early return for tilt mode): Z axis
On/Off+Scale+Invert, Velocity/Position Decay Rate, the Stationary Gate
checkbox+threshold, both ZUPT controls, and all 9 per-axis Range-bar/
Curve-editor/Reference-distance rows. Every other mode ('acceleration',
'worldPosition', 'freeze', 'nativeSensor') shares the exact same full
control set -- confirmed by reading each mode's own code path, not
assumed -- so the split is simply "Tilt vs. everything else," applied
once at dev-panel build time and again on every mode change.

Verified via a standalone Node script (not the real browser -- per this
project's own standing "skip sandbox verification" instruction):
confirmed the null-safety of the new acceleration-source function,
confirmed the mode-branch picks the right source function for all 5
modes, and confirmed the row-visibility split hides exactly the
expected 18 rows in Tilt mode while showing all of them for every other
mode (11/11 checks passed). Not verified against a real device's actual
e.acceleration data quality -- that comparison is the whole point of
exposing this as a testable mode, not something this change claims to
have already answered.

A concurrent Claude Code session, sharing this exact same working
directory (not a separate worktree), landed a complete, unrelated

2026-10-01  -  True ZUPT (zero-velocity-update) for the Displace stationary gate

User-reported bug, with 2 real device logs attached: "I move the phone
forward and backward in a single axis. I pause in between the different
movements. When I move the phone, the phone in the app moves accordingly,
but once I stop, it drifts back to it's default position without me
telling it to." This affects the existing phoneDisplaceMode:'acceleration'/
'worldPosition' modes specifically (the 2 new 'freeze'/'tilt' modes added
earlier the same day, see the entry below, are a different, independent
mitigation strategy for the same underlying dead-reckoning limitation).

User then sent a detailed, structured investigation request (explicitly:
"Do not redesign the entire system or replace the existing implementation
blindly... Do NOT use position decay as a substitute for stationary
detection. Do NOT simply increase velocity decay to hide the problem. Do
NOT make the virtual phone snap back toward the origin... Do NOT
immediately make a large architectural rewrite"), asking for a report
first (A: what the current code does, B: why it still drifts, C: whether
the existing gate/bias tracker are working as intended, D: the smallest
fix for proper stationary velocity correction), then implementation.

REPORT:
A. integratePhoneDisplacement()'s existing stationary gate checked ONLY
   gyro magnitude (Math.hypot(alpha,beta,gamma) < phoneDisplaceStationaryGateDegPerSec,
   default 2.0 deg/s). When gyro was quiet, that single tick's
   acceleration INPUT was zeroed before being fed into the integrator --
   nothing else. The separate bias high-pass filter
   (phoneDisplaceBiasX/Y/Z, PHONE_DISPLACE_BIAS_TRACK_RATE=0.4, ~2.5s time
   constant) runs unconditionally every tick, independent of the gate.
B. Velocity is a leaky integrator: vel = vel*exp(-velDecayRate*dt) +
   accel*dt. Zeroing the ACCEL INPUT on a quiet tick stops NEW velocity
   from accumulating, but any residual velocity already present (e.g.
   from the deceleration phase right before the phone is judged
   "stationary") is only ever multiplied by exp(-velDecayRate*dt) each
   tick -- it asymptotically approaches 0 but mathematically never
   reaches it. With phoneDisplacePosDecayRate=0 (the user's own test
   config, since position decay pulls virtual position back toward the
   origin independent of real motion -- undesirable in general), nothing
   ever corrects what that slow, never-finished velocity decay keeps
   feeding into position every tick (pos += vel*dt). Over a long
   stationary hold this creeps toward the full theoretical v0/velDecayRate
   limit -- small per-tick, but non-zero for a long time, which is
   exactly the "drifts back toward default" symptom.
C. The gyro gate was working as intended for its own narrow job (stop
   feeding in new noisy accel samples while visibly still) -- it was
   never designed to address EXISTING residual velocity. The bias
   tracker was also working as intended (tracking a slow-moving DC
   offset in the accel signal) -- it's a separate mechanism that doesn't
   address this residual-velocity-after-motion-stops case either.
D. Smallest fix: keep the existing gyro gate, add an acceleration-
   magnitude check alongside it (so a vigorous motion with
   coincidentally-low gyro, e.g. pure translation with no rotation,
   doesn't falsely gate), and add a short dwell timer (a single quiet
   sample doesn't instantly trigger anything -- avoids false triggers
   mid-motion). Once BOTH conditions hold continuously for the dwell
   window, force velocity to EXACTLY 0 (a true Zero-Velocity-Update, the
   standard INS/IMU technique for this exact problem) rather than
   leaving it to decay. Position is never touched directly by this fix --
   it holds wherever it already was the instant velocity is zeroed,
   preserving real accumulated displacement exactly as specified.

IMPLEMENTATION: 2 new cfg fields (phoneDisplaceZuptAccelThresholdMps2,
default 0.6 m/s^2; phoneDisplaceZuptDwellMs, default 150ms) and 2 new
module-level state vars (phoneDisplaceZuptDwellStart,
phoneDisplaceZuptActive). The stationary-gate block in
integratePhoneDisplacement() now computes accelMag from the already
bias-corrected/filtered ax/ay/az, requires it under the new threshold
IN ADDITION to the existing gyro check, tracks how long both conditions
have held continuously, and only force-zeroes velocity once the dwell
window elapses -- the dwell timer (and the "stationary" flag itself)
resets the instant either condition is no longer met. 2 new dev-panel
sliders (ZUPT Accel Threshold (m/s^2), ZUPT Dwell Time (Ms)) sit right
after the existing Stationary Gate Threshold slider. 4 new window.__debug
getters (phoneDisplaceZuptActive, phoneDisplaceVel, phoneDisplacePos,
phoneDisplaceBias) expose the new state via the project's existing
diagnostic-exposure convention rather than a separate mechanism, per
direct instruction.

VERIFIED via a standalone Node simulation (not the real browser --
accelerometer/gyroscope hardware isn't available in this sandbox, same
standing limitation as every other device-sensor feature in this
project), comparing the OLD (decay-only) and NEW (dwell-gated hard-zero)
behavior against closed-form predictions:
- Long stationary hold starting from residual velocity 0.5: OLD converges
  to the full v0/velDecayRate limit (0.16172 measured vs 0.16667
  theoretical, small numerical-integration error); NEW converges to the
  much smaller, dwell-window-bounded limit (0.06165 measured vs 0.06040
  theoretical) and then holds there PERMANENTLY regardless of how much
  longer the hold continues -- this is the actual fix, a hard, duration-
  independent cap instead of an ever-shrinking-but-never-zero tail.
- Push-then-hold-still: position keeps settling naturally during the
  dwell window (correct, not a bug -- the dwell exists so a single quiet
  sample mid-motion doesn't instantly freeze things), then holds EXACTLY
  fixed (bit-identical, not just close) from the instant ZUPT engages
  onward, and was never reset to 0 -- real accumulated displacement from
  the push is preserved.
- A single noisy quiet-looking tick mid-motion does not falsely engage
  the ZUPT (dwell not yet satisfied), and the dwell timer correctly
  resets the moment real motion resumes.

NOT independently verified against a real phone's actual sensor stream
(standing limitation, see the project's own "skip sandbox verification"
instruction) -- the fix's correctness rests on the closed-form-matched
standalone simulation above, not a live device test.

  verbatim from HANDY DANDIES (`FINGER_JOINTS`/`FINGER_MAX_DEG`/`FINGER_SIGN`/
  `FINGER_CURL_AXIS`/etc, `rotateOnTrueWorldAxis`, `applyCurlToSkeleton`).
- Phone Tilt mechanic: real-device `deviceorientation` (with iOS
  permission-request handling) and a desktop mouse-distance/angle
  fallback, both reduced to one shared normalized tilt-vector
  abstraction, feeding the same lookAt+slerp rotation pattern HANDY
  DANDIES uses for cursor tracking.
- Dev panel: all 9 requested groups (Tween, Field Layout, Pose w/ 6
  subgroups, Camera, Phone Tilt w/ 3 subgroups -- renamed from Cursor
  Tracking, off by default, Lighting, Toon Shading w/ Outline subgroup,
  Background, Debug) built against the ACTUAL `.claude/TEMPLATE_DEV_PANEL.html`
  engine (copied verbatim into `src/devpanel/devPanel.js`), not HANDY
  DANDIES' own divergent copy -- a genuine mid-task architecture pivot,
  requiring every setting to be translated into the template's real
  (narrower) control-type API rather than 1:1 ported.
- Debug group's own Sensors console: raw accelerometer/gyroscope/compass
  readout, opt-in streaming toggle + sample-interval slider, gated to
  show "No sensor data on Desktop." and never stream on a non-touch
  device.
- Seeded SAVED_POSES (4)/SAVED_CAMERAS (7)/SAVED_LIGHTING (7) from the
  user's own exported HANDY DANDIES data; defaults applied on load
  (Fist / FRONTOS / FLABOVE, per direct instruction -- "Fronto"/"Flabove"
  matched to the closest-named entries, flagged to the user rather than
  guessed silently).

Fixed (found live during this session's own verification, not assumed
working):
- Zero-size-framebuffer render (documented HANDY DANDIES sandbox gotcha,
  `window.innerWidth`/`innerHeight` reading 0 at script-parse time) --
  `animate()` now self-heals every frame, same fix shape as that project.
- Wrist-crop clipping plane was defined in the raw bind-pose local frame
  while the mesh renders fully transformed (world-space clipping planes)
  -- fixed to use the clone's actual `matrixWorld`.
- Wrist-crop plane normal was inverted (discarding the fingers, keeping
  the forearm) -- fixed via an explicit `distanceToPoint()` check at the
  wrist vs. the hand's own visible center.
- Wrist-crop reach: the visible forearm/sleeve mesh extends far past the
  `rForearmBend` bone's own position (confirmed via a wide diagnostic
  screenshot -- a long dark sleeve with only a small fist-colored patch
  at one end) -- switched from a wrist-to-forearm-bone lerp to a fixed
  multiple of hand length, tuned by eye.
- Camera presets retargeting: HANDY DANDIES' saved cameras were captured
  against its own much larger multi-hand field (default field radius
  ~138 world units vs. this project's ~15-20) -- preserving their raw
  offset distance put the camera absurdly far from a single hand;
  `applyCameraPreset()` now preserves each preset's direction/FOV but
  recomputes distance from a "fit the hand in this FOV" formula.
- The dev panel never showed this project's own groups on first load --
  `main.js` (a deferred module script) executed AFTER `devPanel.js`'s own
  eager `ensureDevPanelBuilt()` call (auto-open on localhost), so
  `window.renderHandysetDevGroups` didn't exist yet when needed. Fixed by
  adding `defer` to devPanel.js's script tag and loading it after main.js
  (both then execute in that relative document order).
- The DEV toggle button/panel were permanently `display:none` -- missed
  copying the template's own early `<head>` script that sets
  `html.dev-mode` (a separate, tiny script block before the main
  `<style>`/`<script>`, easy to miss when assembling index.html by hand).
- Stale script cache: bumping the top-level navigation URL's query string
  didn't pick up main.js edits -- the actual `<script src="...?v=N">` tags
  need their own version bump (documented HANDY DANDIES gotcha, re-hit
  here).

Verified:
- Live UI interaction (not just JS state inspection): clicked Debug's
  "Show Wireframe" checkbox via a real `computer` click on the rendered
  page -- confirmed both `cfg.showWireframe` and the live material's
  `wireframe` property flipped, and the rendered mesh visibly changed to
  a wireframe view (which also confirmed the underlying hand geometry
  itself is correctly shaped/posed/cropped).
- Scrolled through the built dev panel confirming Debug (+ Sensors
  subgroup with the Desktop-silence note), Tween, Field Layout, and Pose
  (+ its Whole-Hand Rotation & Thumb subgroup) all render with working
  controls; Camera/Phone Tilt/Lighting/Toon Shading/Background use the
  identical shared rendering/wiring helpers so were not each individually
  scrolled-to and clicked, given session time constraints.
- NOT verified: real device motion data (no physical phone available this
  session -- only that the permission-request/gating code runs without
  throwing).

2026-09-21  -  1:18 AM - 1:25 AM EDT

Fixed 2 dev panel checkbox bugs reported directly by the user after
comparing the real behavior against TEMPLATE_DEV_PANEL.html's own
documented functionality.

Fixed:
- Every setting row on the Desktop tab showed "Independent from Desktop"
  instead of "Show in Mobile/Landscape" -- root cause: `addRow()`
  (main.js) never set `ctrl.tab = 'desktop'` on any control object, and
  `buildUniformControlRow()` (devPanel.js) checks `ctrl.tab === 'desktop'`
  explicitly to decide which checkbox to attach, silently falling through
  to the wrong one for every one of this project's ~150 controls. Fixed
  centrally in `addRow()`.
- No group-level "show in mobile/landscape" checkbox ever appeared next
  to any group's undock icon -- root cause: `buildGroupCascadeCheckbox()`
  in the RAW TEMPLATE_DEV_PANEL.html file itself sets `cb.style.display =
  'none'` unconditionally at creation, and nothing anywhere in that
  ~5700-line file ever sets it back to visible (confirmed by exhaustive
  grep of both this project's copy and the source template). This is a
  genuine latent bug in the template as shipped, not a simplification
  made in this project. Fixed locally by removing that line.

Verified:
- Direct DOM/computed-style inspection (getBoundingClientRect,
  getComputedStyle) on a live-reloaded page confirmed the group checkbox
  now has real dimensions and `display: block`, and a Camera-group row's

  Landscape" (was `.dev-independence-checkbox`).
- Visual screenshot confirmation: every group title now shows its own
  checkbox left of the lock/undock icons; Debug's individual rows
  (Show Grid Helper, Show Wireframe, etc.) each show their own row-level
  checkbox on the right.
- Along the way, hit the documented sandbox-networking gotcha (devPanel.js,
  4557 lines, intermittently failing to fully load under this sandbox's
  local static server -- `200 OK` + `net::ERR_CONNECTION_RESET` on the
  same request) which produced a completely empty dev panel and looked
  like a real regression until traced to a plain failed network request,
  resolved by retrying the navigation. Logged as its own gotcha since it
  cost real time before being correctly identified.

2026-09-21  -  3:48 AM - 4:08 AM EDT

Fixed 5 issues reported directly after live testing on the real Vercel
deployment, plus 2 requests added mid-session (Phone Tilt real-device
