Fixed:
- Pose Offset/Rotation/Thumb not appearing in their correct subgroups --
  root cause: the git-tracked dev-panel-settings.json AND the browser's
  own localStorage both held a saved panel layout captured before this
  session's earlier Pose-group restructure (Rotation/Thumb split, Pose
  Offset subgroup); applySectionOrder() APPENDS an unmatched old group
  rather than replacing it, producing literal duplicate groups/rows with
  dead ids on top of the correct fresh ones. This is also the real cause
  of the separately-reported "miscellaneous/redundant checkboxes that
  flash and can't be clicked." Fixed the git file directly AND added a
  client-side localStorage schema-version guard (HANDYSET_SETTINGS_SCHEMA_VERSION
  in main.js) so this can't recur silently on a future structural change
  -- bump the version constant, the stale local save clears itself.
- "Set as Default" was missing from Pose/Camera/Lighting/Toon Shading --
  added a generic saveFieldAsDefault()/loadFieldDefaultIfSaved() pair
  (ported from Hando's own saveAsDefaultForModel()/loadDefaultForModelIfSaved(),
  simplified since Handyset has one model, not Hando's multi-model
  system) and wired a "Set as Default" button into renderPresetPicker()
  for all 4 groups.
- Toon Shading was missing the entire rim-light + texture/duotone-tint
  shader system (a known simplification from the first build) and had no
  saved-preset UI at all -- ported HANDY DANDIES' own createToonMaterial()
  onBeforeCompile GLSL injection verbatim (not reconstructed), added the
  5 missing sliders, and a full "Saved Toon Shading" list-picker with
  Export/Import + Set as Default.
- Phone Tilt did nothing on either desktop OR a real Android phone --
  removed the isTouchDevice branching that gated mousemove vs.
  deviceorientation as mutually exclusive; both listeners now always
  attach, so whichever one actually produces real events on a given
  device just works, without needing to correctly guess the device type
  first. (The real-device report couldn't be fully root-caused without
  console access to the actual phone -- pointed the user at the existing
  Debug > Sensors console to check whether deviceorientation events fire
  at all as the next diagnostic step if this doesn't resolve it.)
- Group-level "Show in Mobile/Landscape"/"Independent from Desktop"
  checkboxes now retain each child row's own individual checked state
  when the group checkbox is unchecked, and restore those exact
  per-child states (not a blanket re-check) when it's checked again --
  devPanel.js's buildGroupCascadeCheckbox(), per direct request.

Verified:
- Live DOM inspection after each fix confirmed: Pose subgroups no longer
  duplicated; all 4 "Set as Default" buttons present; Toon Shading's rim
  slider and Saved Toon Shading list-picker both render; a real
  group-checkbox uncheck/recheck cycle correctly zeroed then restored

  above and a "Remove" button per row. Matches Hando's own interaction

  (Hold entries pause at a percentage of one normal transition's own

2026-09-21  -  start NOT MEASURED - 4:52 AM EDT

Fixed 3 more directly-reported issues (saved poses "not showing up
correctly", a request to remove Outline entirely, and color/toon
settings not visibly working) plus one issue this uncovered along the
way. Start time not measured -- this work continued from a prior
context-compacted summary rather than a fresh timed task, so per
CLAUDE.md Section 1 this is logged as NOT MEASURED rather than a guess;
the 3 commit timestamps below ARE real, from `git log`.

1. Saved Pose/Camera/Lighting/Toon "Use" clicks correctly updated cfg
   and the live scene but never updated the dev panel's own displayed
   slider/color/checkbox values -- live-confirmed via a real Saved
   Poses "Use" click (cfg.thumbCurl 75->0, hand visibly changed) while
   the Thumb Curl slider stayed frozen at 75 on screen. Added
   syncControlDom()/syncPairsFromCfg() with explicit (domId, cfgKey)
   pairs checked against each render*Group()'s actual addRow() calls
   (sliderHideWrist/sliderPoseScale use different casing than their
   cfg key -- a wrong guess here would have silently synced nothing).
   Wired into all 4 apply*Preset() functions. Live-verified for Pose
   (Big Open Palm (S): Thumb Curl 75->0, Thumb Splay 0->-48, hand
   visibly changed pose) and Camera (Top preset: Camera Z 106.5->-5.4,
   view visibly changed to top-down). [commit 570c811]

2. Removed the Outline settings subgroup entirely, per direct request
   ("I dont need that") -- outlinePass itself stays permanently
   disabled rather than removing the composer pass, so this is a pure
   UI/config change. Bumped HANDYSET_SETTINGS_SCHEMA_VERSION since this
   changes dev-panel structure. [commit 570c811]

   Follow-up, same round: the git-tracked settings file
   (data/processed/dev-panel-settings.json) still had a REAL "Outline"
   subgroup recorded in sectionOrder under Toon Shading (the user's own
   browser had synced it there before this fix existed). Traced
   applySectionOrder()'s placeSection() (devPanel.js) and confirmed: a
   saved group key with no matching live DOM group is treated as a
   custom group and recreated via createDevGroupElement() -- since
   Outline's own controls no longer exist to populate it, this would
   have rebuilt it as a real, empty ghost "Outline" group on the very
   next load, the same class of bug already reported this session
   ("ghost checkboxes"). Stripped every :Outline-suffixed key from
   sectionOrder/sectionCollapseState/devVisibility/devIndependence/
   devTextOverrides; verified no "Outline" substring remains anywhere
   in the file and the JSON still parses. [commit e5424fe]

3. Investigated "with all color pickers as black or white, our hand is
   still showing up with color" from scratch (no prior hypothesis).
   Live-tested via direct DOM value + input-event dispatch (isolated
   javascript_tool eval can drive real addEventListener('input', ...)
   listeners even though it can't see the page's own classic-script
   globals -- confirmed useful this round): at default settings
   (toonBaseTint/toonTint white, textureInfluence 0) the hand rendered
   in raw skin-texture color instead of toon-shaded white/gray;
   colorToonTint set to pure red had ZERO visual effect; rimIntensity=3
   with rimColor=bright green also had ZERO visual effect. Only
   material.color (toonBaseTint, a stock MeshToonMaterial property) had
   any effect at all -- e.g. black fully blacked out the hand.

   Root cause, confirmed by fetching and reading three.js 0.169.0's own
   src/materials/Material.js from unpkg: Material.prototype.copy() does
   NOT copy onBeforeCompile -- there is no such line in it, verified by
   reading the actual method body, not assumed. rebuildField() clones a
   shared toonMaterial per hand via toonMaterial.clone() on the strength
   of an in-code comment asserting "`.clone()` copies onBeforeCompile
   too" -- that assumption was wrong. Every rendered hand's material
   silently fell back to the inherited no-op onBeforeCompile() stub, so
   the entire custom diffuse-tint-override + rim-light shader injection
   never ran on anything actually drawn, only on the one unused shared
   template material object.

   Fix: explicitly reassign
   `skinnedMesh.material.onBeforeCompile = toonMaterial.onBeforeCompile`
   immediately after every `.clone()` in rebuildField(). Live-verified
   the exact same before/after test sequence: after the fix, defaults
   now render correct white/gray toon shading (not skin color),
   colorToonTint=red produces a fully red hand, and
   rimIntensity=3/rimColor=green produces a clearly visible green rim
   glow along the silhouette. [commit def2327]

Verified: all 3 fixes live-tested in-browser with before/after
screenshots per fix, not just code-reviewed. Not yet verified: on the
user's own real device/browser (only this session's local static-server
preview was tested).

2026-09-21  -  4:58 AM - 5:07 AM EDT (8m25s per two `date` calls, actual/ETA
ratio not restated here since the ETA was revised twice as new items
arrived mid-task -- see this session's own chat transcript for each
revision)

Fixed 5 more directly-reported issues, 2 of which turned out to share one
root cause each with an item already fixed this session (or with each
other):

1. **Ghost checkboxes**, also separately reported as "some checkboxes
   aren't aligned to the right edge of the dev panel" -- both the SAME
   bug. `.dev-group-cascade-checkbox` is appended as a CHILD of
   `.dev-section-title` and absolutely positioned (`top:50%; right:52px`),
   but `.dev-section-title` never declared its own `position`, so the
   checkbox's absolute positioning escaped past it to the next positioned
   ancestor -- `.dev-section`, spanning the WHOLE group (title + all its
   content), not just the title bar. `top:50%` then resolved against that
   much taller box, visually landing the checkbox somewhere in the middle
   of the group's own settings rows instead of on its title bar. Fixed by
   giving `.dev-section-title` its own `position: relative`. Live-verified
   at 2 different nesting depths (a top-level "TEXT" group and its nested
   "DEV PANEL TITLE" subgroup) -- both checkboxes now land correctly on
   their own title bars, consistently aligned to each other. Confirmed
   `.claude/TEMPLATE_DEV_PANEL.html` has this identical bug (same missing
   `position:relative`) and folded the same fix back into it (archived via
   `edit-template-dev-panel.js` per CLAUDE.md §4b's own convention for
   that file).

2. **Set as Default (Pose/Camera/Lighting/Toon) appearing to do nothing**
   ("I click it, and i click save, and on refresh its still the old
   settings"). Root cause: `remoteSaveCurrentSettings()` (the main
   Save/Sync button's own git-tracked write) POSTed
   `captureFullDevPanelState()`'s own snapshot directly -- a blind
   overwrite of the ENTIRE remote settings file. `saveFieldAsDefault()`
   already did a correct GET-merge-POST to write
   `defaultPose`/`defaultCamera`/`defaultLighting`/`defaultToon`, but
   clicking Sync afterward silently wiped those custom fields right back
   out, since `captureFullDevPanelState()` (a devPanel.js-owned function)
   has no knowledge of this project's own extra top-level fields. Fixed
   `remoteSaveCurrentSettings()` to GET-merge-POST too, the same pattern
   `saveFieldAsDefault()` already used. Verified via an in-page `fetch`
   mock simulating the settings endpoint (no real backend on this local
   static-server preview): clicking the real "Set as Default" button
   correctly wrote `defaultPose` via GET-merge-POST, and a subsequent real
   click on the real Sync button left `defaultPose` fully intact
   afterward (confirmed via the mock store's own state, not just code
   review).

3. **Phone Tilt doing nothing on the real Vercel deployment**, and
   separately, **4. the Palm Facing rotation slider (Cursor Tracking)
   having no visible effect** -- both gated behind the exact same
   `cfg.trackingEnabled` flag (`animate()`'s own
   `if (cfg.trackingEnabled && hands.length) {...}` block is the ONLY
   place either mechanism's per-frame code runs at all), which defaulted
   to `false`. This is the app's own headline mechanic ("rotates in
   response to the phone's own gyroscope tilt") -- defaulting its master
   gate off meant a fresh visitor with no prior Sync saw nothing happen at
   all, on Vercel or locally, matching both reports exactly. Defaulted to
   `true`. Live-verified: hovering the cursor over the canvas now visibly
   rotates the rendered hand (the desktop mouse-fallback path) -- the real
   device gyroscope path on the actual Vercel deployment still needs the
   user's own confirmation, since this session has no physical device.

Verified: all fixes live-tested in-browser (screenshots for the checkbox
fix, a hover-triggered rotation screenshot for Phone Tilt, an in-page
fetch-mock round-trip for Set as Default), not just code-reviewed. A real
concurrent Save from the user's own browser (commit "Update
dev-panel-settings.json via Save Settings") landed on `origin/main`
mid-session -- pulled via `git stash`/fast-forward/`stash pop` before this
round's own commit, per this file's own documented git-safety convention,
so nothing was lost.

2026-09-21  -  5:15 AM - 5:28 AM EDT (12m33s per two `date` calls)

Fixed 4 more directly-reported issues -- 2 of them by reading HANDY
DANDIES' real source per direct instruction ("Handy Dandies already went
through the slog of figuring out... refer to Handy Dandies on how to
port it"), rather than reconstructing, which surfaced a 3rd, previously-
invisible bug along the way.

1. **Camera Overwrite/Set-as-Default/Use restoring the wrong position**
   ("I pan with right click. I click overwrite... the camera angle is
   changed, but the position is wrong"). `applyCameraPreset()` only ever
   used the saved camera's `tx/ty/tz` to derive a DIRECTION, then
   recomputed distance from a "fit a sphere to this FOV" formula --
   silently discarding the actual saved position/zoom every time. Read
   HANDY DANDIES' own real `applyCameraPreset()` (`src/main.js:3288`) and
   ported it verbatim: a literal `camera.position.set()`/
   `controls.target.set()` restore, no recompute step at all.
   `captureCameraFromLive()` now also captures `cfg.cameraZoom`
   (previously never captured or restored).

   This exposed a genuine pre-existing bug in the seed data: `FRONTOS`
   (`DEFAULT_CAMERA_NAME`, applied on every fresh load) had `tz: -314.7`
   where all 6 other saved cameras share `tz: -1.789` -- harmless under
   the old recompute-based apply (which discarded `tz`'s literal
   magnitude, only using it for direction), but produced a badly-framed
   close-up view once the literal restore was correct. Live-confirmed via
   `camera.position`/`controls.target` readouts before and after
   correcting the data to match the other cameras' shared target.

2. **Saved Poses "showing up differently than Hando/Handy Dandies"** --
   per direct instruction, read HANDY DANDIES' real
   `applyWristPoseToSkeleton()` (`src/main.js:2570`) instead of trusting
   this project's own reconstructed version (already flagged as a
   "worth a visual sanity check" gap in this file's own Known
   Simplifications section). Confirmed a real divergence: the
   reconstructed version used `rotateOnTrueWorldAxis()` for wrist bend/
   splay/rotation (converts a FIXED world-space axis into the bone's
   CURRENT local frame before each rotation); HANDY DANDIES' real
   function uses plain `bone.rotateX/rotateZ/rotateY` -- three.js's own
   LOCAL-axis rotation, genuinely sequential (each rotation spins around
   the axis as already reoriented by the PREVIOUS rotation). The two
   methods only agree at near-zero angles; for any real combined pose
   they diverge. Ported verbatim (axis-letter assignment -- X=bend,
   Z=splay, Y=rotation -- was already correct; only the rotation METHOD
   was wrong). Cross-checked every other wrist-adjacent axis choice in
   this file (whole-hand model rotation, finger curl/splay) against Handy
   Dandies' real source too and confirmed those already matched --this
   was the one genuine divergence, not a systemic issue.

   Also confirmed, but NOT yet ported (no currently-seeded pose has a
   nonzero value, so no visible effect yet): HANDY DANDIES' own Pose
   Offset X/Y/Z is resolved against the CAMERA's own right/up/toward-
   camera basis (`applyPoseOffsetToPosition()`), not plain world-space
   axes like this project's own `h.clone.position.set(...)`. Documented
   as a known remaining gap in CLAUDE.md rather than silently left
   unmentioned -- port before any pose actually uses a nonzero offset.

3. **Tween group buttons "look like... default white/gray buttons"**
   ("match hando"). None of "+ Add"/"+ Hold"/"Remove"/"Export Tween PNG
   Sequence" were inside a `dev-buttons`-classed container, the one thing
   every other panel button (Save/Overwrite/Use/etc., which the user
   confirmed already looks right) actually depends on for the template's
   real button styling -- plain unstyled browser buttons otherwise. Fixed
   by adding the class to the relevant container rows, plus a scoped CSS
   rule matching the same look for the inline per-row "Remove" button
   (which sits beside a select/slider, not alone in its own row, so it
   can't just reuse the flex:1 `dev-buttons` layout wholesale).

4. **Phone Tilt "does nothing" (both mobile and, per a follow-up report,
   desktop) + explicit request to remove the "Enable Motion" button** --
   investigated live against the actual deployed Vercel URL
   (https://handy-set.vercel.app), not just the local preview: confirmed
   `cfg.trackingEnabled` was already `true` there (last round's fix had
   deployed correctly) and a cursor-hover test DID visibly rotate the
   hand, so the underlying mechanism was working as of this round's start
   -- the "still doesn't work" report most likely predated that
   deployment finishing, or a stale un-refreshed tab. Per the explicit
   follow-up request, removed `#motionPermissionBtn` entirely (DOM +
   CSS); Phone Tilt on/off is now purely `checkboxTrackingEnabled`, whose
   own onChange calls `requestMotionPermissionIfNeeded()` directly when
   checked -- a real user gesture, satisfying iOS's own
   `DeviceOrientationEvent.requestPermission()` gate the same way the old
   dedicated button did. Android (the actual target device) was never
   gated by this at all -- `attachMotionListeners()` already ran
   unconditionally there both before and after this change.

Verified: syntax-checked after every edit; Camera fix live-verified via
direct `camera.position`/`controls.target` readouts before/after; wrist-
pose fix live-verified via 2 saved poses (Fist -- all-zero wrist values,
confirms no regression at identity -- and Big Open Palm (S), wristSplay
=-55, confirms a real nonzero case renders as a plausible open hand, not
broken/NaN geometry) with screenshots. NOT verified: the real device
gyroscope path on an actual phone, or any of this on the live Vercel

`cfg.trackingEnabled`/cursor-tracking behavior).

A real concurrent Save from the user's own browser (commit "Update

2026-09-21  -  5:37 AM - 5:42 AM EDT (5m03s per two `date` calls)

Fixed the real, separate bug behind "the pose issue is still an issue...
not as bad, but some poses are still messed up" -- direct follow-up after
last round's wrist-rotation-METHOD fix (rotateOnTrueWorldAxis ->
bone.rotateX/Y/Z) only partially fixed pose rendering.

Per direct instruction ("check how handy dandy project fixed it... check
their change logs... I believe finger curl and splay direction is local
to the wrong thing or anchored to the rotation of another object"),
searched HANDY DANDIES' own `docs/CHANGELOG.txt` for this exact bug class
instead of re-deriving from scratch, and found it: a multi-round 2026-09-
14/15 saga titled "THE ACTUAL, FINAL, REAL root cause of the entire
thumb/finger-pose saga" / "Found and fixed the ACTUAL wrist-splay/curl
bug." Cross-referenced against Handyset's own current `applyCurlToSkeleton()`
and confirmed the identical bug still present: the curl/splay axis
reference was just `baseQuat` (the whole-hand's orientation BEFORE the
wrist), completely ignoring the wrist bone's own current bend/splay --
despite every finger's base joint being a structural descendant of the
wrist bone (`rHand`). Anatomically, curl direction must rotate WITH the
wrist (closing a fist still closes toward your own palm no matter how
your wrist is bent) -- a wrist-independent axis increasingly "misses" the
real rotated palm the further wristBend/wristSplay moves from wherever a
pose's curl values were tuned by eye. This exactly explains "some poses
work [wristBend=wristSplay=0, e.g. Fist], some others are still messed up
[nonzero, e.g. Big Open Palm (S)]."

Ported HANDY DANDIES' own real, TWICE-corrected formula verbatim (not the
first, rejected attempt): `computeCurlAxisRefQuat()` conjugates the
wrist's LOCAL delta-from-rest by its own rest quaternion, composed onto
baseQuat -- `baseQuat * wristRest * delta * wristRest^-1`, `delta =
wristRest^-1 * wristBone.quaternion`. Explicitly NOT the naive "conjugate
by the wrist's full WORLD quaternion" version, which Handy Dandies' own
history shows passes every relative-to-wrist self-consistency test while
silently collapsing to IDENTITY (discarding baseQuat) at
wristBend=wristSplay=0 exactly -- a systematically-biased-but-self-
consistent formula that looks correct in every test except the one that
actually matters.

Verified both required cases live: "Fist" (wristBend=wristSplay=0)
renders PIXEL-IDENTICAL to before this fix (screenshot comparison),
confirming the new formula correctly reduces to baseQuat at delta=identity
with zero regression; "Big Open Palm (S)" (wristSplay=-55) now renders
visibly differently and still as a plausible, non-degenerate open hand,
confirming the fix has real effect on the previously-broken nonzero case.

Also investigated, mid-round, a separate direct report ("phone tilt
doesn't work... cursor tracking doesn't work on desktop either... maybe
double check your wiring"): tested directly against the REAL live Vercel
deployment (no `?dev=1`, matching an actual visitor) via
`window.__debug.hands[0].wrapper.quaternion` before/after a real mouse
hover -- confirmed it changes substantially ([0,1,0,0] ->
[-0.11,0.84,-0.19,-0.49]), proving the tracking mechanism itself is
genuinely working end to end, not a wiring bug. Found a likely
explanation instead: the live default color scheme (toonBaseTint,
bgColor, keyColor all #ffffff, ambientIntensity 0) makes the hand nearly
invisible against the page background (confirmed via screenshot -- only
faint edge lines visible, no clear filled shape) -- a pre-existing
default, not a regression from any fix this session, but a plausible
reason a real rotation could be invisible to the user. Not fixed this
round (a deliberate color-scheme choice, not unilaterally changed) --
documented in CLAUDE.md as a known, real usability issue.

2026-09-21  -  6:10 AM - 6:31 AM EDT (20m22s per two `date` calls)

Deep live-debugging round on 2 directly-reported issues (Hide Wrist not
applying to the live model; Palm Face Rotation "messing with poses"/
"does nothing"), plus an extended real-time investigation into "cursor
and tilt tracking is being overridden by some other setting" that the
user pushed back on repeatedly as I gathered evidence.

**Hide Wrist fixed for real.** `updateWristCrop()`'s clip-plane creation
was gated `if (!h.clipPlane) { h.clipPlane = new Plane(); material.
clippingPlanes = [h.clipPlane] }` -- but the DISABLE branch (crop toggled
off) clears `material.clippingPlanes` to a fresh `[]` without ever
clearing `h.clipPlane` itself, so once crop was ever toggled off and back
on, that guard stayed permanently false and the plane was never
re-attached to the material again. Confirmed live via direct inspection:
`clippingPlanes[0].constant` genuinely changed on every Hide Wrist slider
input the whole time (the math was never broken) -- but rendering had
stopped reflecting it. Fixed by unconditionally reassigning
`material.clippingPlanes = [h.clipPlane]` every call. Separately noted
(not a bug, a scale observation): `maxReach` algebraically reduces to
`2.8 * cfg.handScale` world units (handLengthRaw cancels out of its own
formula) -- worth checking against actual scene scale if the slider's
visible range still looks subtle after this fix.

**Palm Face Rotation: extensively verified the underlying mechanism is
NOT broken, after the user firmly and repeatedly disputed that
conclusion.** Took the pushback seriously rather than re-asserting my
first read -- ran a long sequence of independent, increasingly rigorous
checks on both the local preview and the REAL live Vercel deployment
(https://handy-set.vercel.app, no `?dev=1`): direct before/after
`wrapper.quaternion` comparisons (confirmed real, substantial change from
a clean 0deg->180deg sweep), ruled out stale git-tracked settings
(checked `checkboxTrackingEnabled`/`sliderPalmFaceRotationOffset`/
`checkboxPalmFacesCursor` directly against the live remote JSON), ruled
out a dead render loop (a standalone requestAnimationFrame counter probe
ticked normally), ruled out backface culling (forcing `material.side =
THREE.DoubleSide` changed nothing), and finally found the real
explanation: widening the camera's FOV from 32deg to 90deg brought a
hand that had "vanished" at offset=179deg back into view, visibly
rotated. The roll axis (`wristCropNormalAligned`) is nearly aligned with
the default cameras' own view direction, so small-to-moderate offsets
barely change the visible silhouette (like a screw spinning along its
own axis) and large ones swing the fingers entirely outside the narrow
FOV. Real usability problem, not a wiring bug -- documented in CLAUDE.md,
including the specific ruled-out hypotheses so this doesn't need
re-investigating from scratch.

**Methodology note:** `THREE.*.prototype` monkey-patching (slerp,
lookAt, WebGLRenderer.render) gave unreliable/contradictory call counts
in this environment -- reported zero calls on code paths independently
proven (via direct state reads) to be running. Abandoned that technique
mid-investigation in favor of direct before/after state comparison, which
is what actually resolved this. Documented as a standing methodology
note so a future session doesn't lose time on the same dead end.

Also confirmed via direct source fetch that the live Vercel deployment
was genuinely running the latest pushed main.js (byte-identical
`animate()` body) -- ruled out a stale-deployment explanation early,
before the longer investigation above.

2026-09-21  -  7:05 AM - 7:24 AM EDT (continuation from a compacted
context; no start timestamp captured before the boundary, so this
entry's own duration is NOT MEASURED)

**Correction to the 6:10-6:31 AM entry above: the "narrow FOV / roll axis
nearly aligned with camera" explanation is WRONG.** Re-investigated after
the user firmly rejected that conclusion too ("THE PALM FACE ROTATION
SLIDER DOES NOTHING... I SWEAR your code is wrong"). Found the REAL
problem was in my own testing methodology, not the app:

1. `canvas.toDataURL()` on this project's WebGL canvas (no
   `preserveDrawingBuffer`) silently returns a stale/blank buffer when
   read outside the render loop -- direct pixel sampling confirmed fully
   transparent `[0,0,0,0]` at every sampled point even while the app was
   visibly rendering a solid white hand on screen. Every earlier
   "pixels unchanged" conclusion (this session and the prior one) that
   relied on `canvas.toDataURL()` comparisons was comparing two blanks,
   not two real frames.
2. `requestAnimationFrame` genuinely stops advancing on a non-fronted (or
   otherwise unobserved) tab in this browser-pane sandbox -- confirmed
   with an independent, app-code-free rAF counter that sat frozen at 0
   for 1+ second, then resumed the instant a `computer{action:
   "screenshot"}` interaction touched the tab. This is a sandbox/harness
   quirk, not a real-browser behavior on an actively-viewed tab.

With both fixed (front the tab, pump real frames via screenshot
interaction before reading state, sample real pixels instead of
`toDataURL()`), re-tested cleanly on the live Vercel deployment:
- Palm Face Rotation 0deg -> 180deg: direct screenshot comparison shows
  the whole hand visibly rotate (thumb moved from bottom-right to
  pointing left). Confirmed via a REAL DOM `input` event dispatched at
  the actual slider element (not a direct `cfg` write), so the reported
  control's own event wiring is confirmed intact too.
- Curled the thumb 70% via the real slider, then swept Palm Face
  Rotation 0deg -> 90deg: the curl stays visually intact through the
  rotation -- the whole posed hand rotates rigidly, matching the
  `rotateOnTrueWorldAxis(..., excludeQuat)` design intent (finger curl
  axes are computed with the wrapper's live rotation excluded, so pose
  is not supposed to be, and in this test was not, distorted by it).

Root cause of the user's real-world experience is still unconfirmed, but
the leading candidate is this project's own documented flaky-localhost
gotcha (CLAUDE.md's own "local static server can intermittently fail to
fully deliver devPanel.js" note): directly observed repeated
`net::ERR_CONNECTION_RESET`/404s on script requests AND an uncaught
`TypeError: Cannot read properties of null` on multiple fresh page loads
this session, both on `localhost` and (once, transiently) on the deployed
Vercel build. A partially-failed script load would silently leave some
controls unwired, which matches "the slider does nothing" far better than
a math bug that direct, repeated, rigorous testing cannot reproduce.

Also committed (`2f00195`) the still-uncommitted per-frame
`skinnedMesh.onBeforeRender`-based wrist-crop-plane recompute from the
6:10-6:31 AM round above -- it had been implemented and tested locally
but never actually made it into a commit before this session picked back
up. Genuinely correct and worth keeping regardless of the FOV theory
being wrong (a stale crop plane during rotation is still a real, if
different, bug) -- pushed to origin/main.

2026-09-21  -  7:28 AM - 7:37 AM EDT (8m12s per two `date` calls)

Ported Reactive Arm Length and Responsive Wrist Splay from HANDY DANDIES
-- both explicitly, repeatedly requested ("where is my fucking responsive
wrist splay group?" / "and the responsive wrist cropping") and previously
documented as genuine, not-yet-built gaps needing real new subsystems.

A background research agent first extracted the complete real HANDY
DANDIES source verbatim (curve math, both custom widget builders, every
dev-panel control, every per-frame call site) rather than reconstructing
from a summary, per this workspace's own porting discipline.

**Curve math + widgets** -- `evaluateReactiveCurve()`/`catmullRomY()`/
`cubicBezier1D()`/`bezierSegmentY()` (a Catmull-Rom spline with optional
per-point bezier-handle overrides) ported verbatim. The 2 custom UI
widgets (an SVG draggable-point curve editor; a dual-handle range bar,
neither a devPanel.js built-in control type) are genericized into ONE
pair of parametrized builders (`buildReactiveRangeWidget`/
`buildReactiveCurveWidget`) rather than HANDY DANDIES' own 4 near-
duplicate functions -- same behavior, less code. Both features share a
`curveWidgetResyncs` per-frame poll array (a devPanel.js Reset/restore
writes straight to a control's `input.value` without firing an 'input'
event, so each widget's own display AND this project's `cfg`/parsed-curve
cache need a plain last-seen-value comparison to detect and resync from
that kind of external change).

**One deliberate, disclosed adaptation, not a silent scope cut**: HANDY
DANDIES normalizes "distance" against the live min/max distance across a
whole FIELD of hands, recomputed every frame -- a concept that doesn't
exist for this project's single-hand (or small field) case. Reused
`tiltMagnitude` instead (already computed every frame for Phone Tilt --
0-1 normalized cursor/tilt distance from screen/device center), the
natural equivalent for this project's own tracking abstraction. Every
actual control -- reactive on/off, a static default value, min/max range,
the curve editor itself -- is otherwise a full, faithful port.

**Reactive Arm Length**: `computeArmLengthT(tiltMagnitude)` replaces the
old plain `cfg.hideWrist / 100` inside `updateWristClipPlaneForHand()`
(falls back to that exact static value when Reactive is off) -- so the
existing per-frame `onBeforeRender` recompute (this session's own earlier
fix) now also picks up live reactive changes for free, no extra wiring
needed.

**Responsive Wrist Splay**: `applyWristPoseToSkeleton()` gained an
`extraSplayDeg` parameter (ported from HANDY DANDIES' own identical
signature), added onto `values.wristSplay` before the single `rotateZ`
call. `applyPoseValuesToHand()` now always passes
`computeResponsiveWristSplayDeg(tiltMagnitude)` as that extra value (so a
one-shot pose apply already reflects the current tilt). A new
`applyReactiveWristSplayFrame()`, called every frame from `animate()`
while both the master and Reactive toggles are on, re-bakes the wrist
bone AND re-curls all 5 fingers each frame -- ported reasoning from HANDY
DANDIES: every finger's base joint is a descendant of the wrist bone, so
curl axes go stale the instant the wrist keeps moving after a one-shot
apply. HANDY DANDIES' own stagger-across-hands performance optimization
(needed at its own hundreds-of-hands scale) was not ported -- unnecessary
at this project's single/small hand count.

**Live-verified on localhost** (Vercel wasn't re-tested this round --
localhost's flaky script delivery, documented earlier this session, did
not reproduce on this reload): both new dev-panel groups render correctly
(Responsive Wrist Splay as its own top-level group; Reactive Arm Length
nested in Pose > Wrist, alongside the pre-existing Hide Wrist/Crop Wrist
controls) -- range and curve widgets draw and drag correctly, screenshot-
confirmed. Toggling "Reactive Arm Length" off and moving the cursor left
the wrist-crop clip plane's `constant` completely unchanged
(`17.94278...` before and after, byte-identical) -- confirmed the master
gate genuinely works, not just that the checkbox visually toggles. With
Reactive back on, moving the cursor between 2 screen positions produced a
real, substantial change in both the wrist bone's own live quaternion
(Responsive Wrist Splay) and the clip plane's `constant`
(`18.246... -> 14.557...`, Reactive Arm Length) -- direct proof both
mechanisms are live and reactive, not just present in the UI.

`HANDYSET_SETTINGS_SCHEMA_VERSION` bumped (`2026-09-21c` -> `2026-09-21d`)
for the new "Responsive Wrist Splay" group and the new rows inside Pose >
Wrist, per this project's own documented convention.

2026-09-21  -  8:11 AM - 8:26 AM EDT (real, measured via two `date` calls;
see the final timing line below)

Fixed the REAL root cause behind "wrist splay is the only thing tracking
the cursor, why isn't the palm rotation" and a separately-reported wrist-
crop issue ("as I move the cursor, which splays the wrist, the top of the
hand gets cropped") -- the user directly suspected both were the same
bug, and they were right. Provided a screen recording (`datalog/Recording
2026-09-21 080827.mp4`) as evidence; the browser pane could open the file
but not play/scrub it (renders as a static first-frame snapshot), so the
investigation proceeded via direct live testing instead, which was
sufficient to find and confirm the bug independently.

**Root cause**: `updateWristClipPlaneForHand()`'s clip-plane normal was
derived from `wristCropNormalAligned`, a FIXED bind-pose forearm->wrist
direction, only ever rotated by `h.wrapper.quaternion` (Phone Tilt) --
never by the wrist bone's own live rotation (wristBend/wristSplay/
wristRotation + this session's own new Responsive Wrist Splay, which
reaches up to 71 real degrees). The plane's ANCHOR point was never wrong
(confirmed directly: a bone's own local rotation doesn't move its own
world position), but its ORIENTATION stayed pinned to the pre-bend
forearm axis while the actual hand geometry -- distal to the wrist, which
DOES rotate with wrist bend -- swung up to 71 degrees away from it,
clipping away most of the hand at extreme splay. This was a genuinely new
failure mode: Responsive Wrist Splay (added this session, earlier today)
made wristSplay reach a much larger, more dynamic range than anything
this project had before, which is what exposed it so severely.

**Why this also explained the palm-rotation complaint**: live-verified at
a heavily-splayed cursor position that disabling Crop Wrist revealed the
FULL hand (5 fingers, full palm) where, with Crop Wrist on, only a thin
crescent sliver remained. Direct `h.wrapper.quaternion` comparison at
that exact same position (Palm Face Rotation offset 0 vs 180) showed it
changing substantially regardless of the crop state -- the rotation math
was never broken (yet again confirmed) -- there was just too little
visible hand geometry left, once cropped, to perceive it rotating.

**Fix**: derive the plane's normal from the LIVE wrist->fingertip
direction (`rHand` -> `rMid3`, both read via `getWorldPosition()` every
frame) instead of the static forearm->wrist axis -- `rMid3` is a skeleton
descendant of `rHand`, so its world position moves with every wrist-pose
axis, keeping the plane's orientation aligned with wherever the hand is
actually currently pointing, not just wherever the forearm was in the
bind pose. Live-verified across several different cursor positions (top,
bottom, right of screen, with and without a manual Palm Face Rotation
offset): consistent, plausible hand silhouette every time, no crescent-
sliver clipping, and clearly distinguishable shape/orientation changes
between positions.

**One separate, secondary finding, not yet fixed**: at an extreme
COMBINED rotation (manual Palm Face Rotation offset near 180 degrees
stacked on an already-large cursor-driven tilt), the hand can go fully
invisible -- confirmed NOT the crop bug (persists with Crop Wrist off)
and NOT backface culling (persists with `material.side` forced to
`THREE.DoubleSide`, `frustumCulled` forced false, no NaN in any matrix).
An independent secondary WebGLRenderer render of the same scene/camera
DID show non-white pixels, suggesting the geometry itself may just be
landing outside the app's own narrow 32-degree-FOV camera's effective
frame at that extreme combined angle -- not confirmed, flagged as a
follow-up. This matches (and finally explains, at least partially) an
EARLIER session's own "hand vanished at offset=179, widening FOV to 90
brought it back" observation, which had been wrongly generalized to "the
roll axis is just hard to see" -- it was really this narrower, extreme-
combined-rotation case, not the effect being subtle in general.

Timing: start 8:11:03 AM, finish 8:26:31 AM EDT (2 real `date` calls) =
15m28s. Committed as `e0b85ba` (rebased onto the user's own concurrent
"Update dev-panel-settings.json via Save Settings" commit from their live
testing session, `2d32a1d` -- confirmed via `git show --stat` that it only
touched the settings JSON before rebasing, per this workspace's own
concurrent-session preservation rule) and pushed.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-09-21  -  6:22 PM - 6:36 PM EDT (13m53s per two `date` calls)

Direct report: "take a look at the Palm Face Rotation cursor tracking in
Handy Dandies. That implementation doesn't require extreme values or
extreme cursor tilt/distance. so i dont understand why we need that?"
Read Handy Dandies' own real `updateCursorTarget()` (main.js ~1934) --
it raycasts the actual cursor position through the camera onto a
world-space plane, no normalized-magnitude abstraction anywhere.
HANDYSET's own `tiltMagnitude` (dividing screen distance from center by
half the smaller screen dimension) needed a near-screen-edge cursor
position before the signal approached its max -- that mismatch, not a
math bug, was why tracking looked like it needed extreme values.

Ported Handy Dandies' raycaster approach for the MOUSE path only --
device-orientation input keeps the normalized magnitude/angle circular
offset, the correct abstraction for a physical tilt angle with no
on-screen position to raycast from. Required one adaptation beyond a
literal port: HANDYSET's single hand sits at world origin, but its own
saved camera is framed/aimed at a completely different point (roughly
y=33, matching the model's visible geometry after scale/pivot, not the
hand's own logical anchor) -- a literal absolute raycast hit produced
wildly wrong, near-vertical rotations even for a dead-center cursor.
Fixed by using the OFFSET from a screen-center raycast (screen center =
zero offset = neutral gaze) instead of the raw absolute hit.

Found and fixed 2 further issues during live verification:

1. `tiltMagnitude`/`tiltAngle` still needed to update for mouse input in
   parallel with the new raycast -- Reactive Arm Length/Responsive Wrist
   Splay read them directly, not the new `cursorNDC`/`tiltTarget`. An
   initial cut removed them entirely (reasoning they were now redundant),
   silently freezing both reactive systems at their startup value the
   instant mouse input took over.

2. The REAL cause of the hand going fully invisible -- both this
   session's own earlier "extreme combined rotation" finding (flagged as
   unresolved) AND a new regression this exact round, where the hand
   started vanishing at completely ORDINARY cursor positions once
   tracking became proportionate: three.js's default frustum culling uses
   `geometry.boundingSphere`, computed once from raw UNSKINNED bind-pose
   vertex data -- it never accounts for skin/bone deformation. Confirmed
   directly via an independent `WebGLRenderer` instance (bypassing this
   app's own composer/outline pipeline entirely, ruling out any composer-
   specific cause): 0 triangles drawn with `frustumCulled` left at its
   `true` default, 15,046 triangles drawn with it forced `false`, same
   scene/camera/pose, nothing else changed. Fixed by disabling frustum
   culling unconditionally at hand creation -- cheap and safe at this
   project's own small hand-count scale. This also retroactively explains
   an EARLIER session's own "hand vanished at offset=179, widening FOV to
   90 brought it back" observation, previously misdiagnosed as "the roll
   axis is just hard to see in general" -- it was really this same
   culling bug, reachable at any sufficiently-rotated pose.

Live-verified across several cursor positions (dead center, modest
offsets in multiple directions, a larger-but-not-extreme offset): stable,
visible, proportionate response every time, no vanishing, wrist crop
still correctly scoped (confirmed still on and not over-cropping
throughout). Committed as `6f3d8df` and pushed (no concurrent remote
commits this round, confirmed via `git fetch` before pushing).

2026-09-21  -  6:47 PM - 6:58 PM EDT (11m01s per two `date` calls)

Direct feedback after the prior cursor-tracking round: "okay you didnt
fix it. Im going to build it from scratch with you." -- 3 concrete asks
followed, plus a 4th added mid-turn.

**1. Removed the "Responsive Wrist Splay" group entirely.** User asked to
remove "Responsive Palm Rotation and all settings within it" and to check
git-synced settings for the real group name first. Checked
data/processed/dev-panel-settings.json's own sectionOrder -- no group
named "Responsive Palm Rotation" exists; the only match (confirmed by the
same 2 checkboxes the user separately asked to have explained) is
"Responsive Wrist Splay", built earlier this session. Removed the cfg
fields, parse/compute functions, per-frame reapplication
(applyReactiveWristSplayFrame), and dev-panel group entirely -- not
disabled, not hidden, actually deleted from the source. The shared curve-
editor widget builders (buildReactiveRangeWidget/buildReactiveCurveWidget)
were kept since Reactive Arm Length still uses them.

**2. Wrist crop was fundamentally wrong** -- wrong axis, wrong direction,
and silently clamped. Direct, precise report: "the cropping plane doesnt
seem like the right rotation... it should be the plane perpendicular to
the axis of the first bone, the arm bone... its currently cropping the
hand not the arm, should be the other way. when i set crop to 0%, the
hand is most cropped, at 100% its less but still crops the whole hand,
and 200% does nothing since the slider caps at 100." Two EARLIER rounds
this same session both got this wrong in different ways -- a background
research agent read HANDO's own real `updateWristClipPlane()` verbatim
this round instead of reasoning about it fresh again, and it was ported
exactly: normal = (rHand - rForearmBend), normalized, used DIRECTLY as
the plane normal (the prior 2 rounds both negated it -- the actual
direction bug); clip point = farPos + normal*armToWristDist*t, t =
hideWrist/100 completely UNCLAMPED (the prior version clamped t to
[0,1], silently eating any value past 100). Live-verified: 0% now shows
a long, fully-extended forearm; 100% crops it away with the hand
completely intact (a short, flat shape, not the whole hand missing);
setting cfg.hideWrist directly to 150 (bypassing the slider's own native
HTML max=100, which still clamps a raw .value assignment even though the
dev panel's own click-to-type auto-expand should handle a real typed
value) correctly pushed the crop further, into the hand itself, proving
the unclamped math now does something instead of nothing past 100%.

**3. "Responsive Wrist Splay (Master On/Off)" vs "Reactive Wrist Splay
(By Cursor Distance)" -- now moot**, since the whole group was removed
per #1, but for the record: the first was the feature's own overall
on/off gate (checkbox off = zero effect, full stop); the second only
mattered when the first was on, and chose between a single static
default value (reactive off) or a live, cursor-distance-driven curve
(reactive on). Two genuinely different questions the old UI answered
with 2 separate checkboxes rather than 1 -- a real, if confusing,
design, not a duplicate control.

**4. Added mid-turn: "I think your entire Rotation mechanic is anchored
incorrectly... check Hando... may also explain the palm rotation
thing."** This was the big one. The same research agent extracted
HANDO's real Whole-Hand Rotation X/Y/Z mechanism verbatim. Found live:
`h.clone.quaternion` -- the object that actually holds the rendered
hand -- was set exactly ONCE, at hand creation, to `alignQuat`, and
NEVER updated again by anything. `computeBaseQuatFromValues()` correctly
computed a quaternion incorporating modelRotX/Y/Z, but that quaternion
(`h.currentBaseQuat`) was only ever used as a finger-curl axis reference
-- the 3 Whole-Hand Rotation sliders had LITERALLY ZERO visible effect
on the rendered hand this entire time, not a subtle anchor bug, a
complete disconnection. Ported HANDO's own real fix for this exact bug
class (its own docs/CHANGELOG.txt, 2026-09-11: "make the center of
rotation the center of the palm", plus a second regression from
measuring the pivot after default posing had already bent the skeleton):
`h.clone.quaternion` now actually receives the computed rotation,
pivoted around `modelRotationPivot` (palm center -- midpoint of the
wrist bone `rHand` and the middle finger's own base joint `rMid1`,
measured once at model load, before rebuildField()/applyDefaultSelections()
ever pose the skeleton), with `h.clone.position` recomputed via the
standard rotate-about-an-arbitrary-point formula every time rotation or
scale changes, so the palm stays fixed in `h.wrapper`-local space (the
extra layer HANDYSET has that HANDO doesn't -- Phone Tilt's own outer
rotation). Live-verified: the wrist bone's own world position read
byte-identical (`[7.18e-15, 24.515161514282223, -4.195592021469745e-7]`,
to full float precision) at modelRotZ=45 vs modelRotZ=-60 -- proving the
pivot is genuinely fixed, not just approximately stable -- and the
visible hand now dramatically, correctly reorients (individual splayed
fingers clearly visible at modelRotY=90, where the default view had
only ever shown an ambiguous sliver/blob).

This also directly explains a chunk of the "palm rotation doesn't seem
to do anything" confusion from earlier sessions: Whole-Hand Rotation
(the Pose group's modelRotX/Y/Z sliders) and Palm Face Rotation (the
Phone Tilt group's cursor-tracking roll, a completely separate
mechanism via h.wrapper.quaternion) are 2 different features the user
may have been describing together -- the Phone Tilt one was already
independently verified working multiple times this session; THIS one
was never wired up at all until now.

Committed as `d3d2564`, rebased cleanly onto 2 of the user's own
concurrent "Update dev-panel-settings.json via Save Settings" commits
from their live testing session (confirmed via `git show --stat` that
both only touched the settings JSON before rebasing), pushed as `d1128ab`.

Direct user correction (4 items) after the previous entry's Whole-Hand

they actually have -- NOT the same thing as "Responsive Wrist Splay",
which I had wrongly removed believing it was the group being asked
about; (2) confusion over why both names still appear; (3) wrist
cropping confirmed good, no action; (4) Whole Hand Rotation X/Y/Z still
broken -- the 3 sliders were visibly affecting finger splay instead of
rigidly rotating the whole model.

1. Restored "Responsive Wrist Splay" in full (cfg fields, parse/compute
   functions, applyWristPoseToSkeleton's extraSplayDeg param,
   applyReactiveWristSplayFrame + its animate() call site,
   renderResponsiveWristSplayGroup + its dev-panel call site) from git
   history (`git show 6f3d8df:src/main.js`, the last commit before it was
   removed). Bumped HANDYSET_SETTINGS_SCHEMA_VERSION to force-clear stale
   localStorage. Also cleaned up 2 orphaned empty group shells the
   removal had left behind in dev-panel-settings.json's Mobile/Landscape
   sectionOrder.

2. "Responsive Palm Rotation" could not be found anywhere in the
   git-tracked dev-panel-settings.json -- exhaustive search (raw text,
   full JSON structure across all 3 tabs' sectionOrder, every
   devTextOverrides/devTextOverridesManual key) found no trace. Left

   device/browser actually shows this group (so its real state reaches
   git), or a screenshot/more specific description, since there's no way
   to inspect another browser's localStorage directly.

3. No action needed.

4. Root-caused and fixed for real this time, after 2 rounds of
   curl-axis-exclusion changes (alignQuat instead of h.currentBaseQuat as
   applyCurlToSkeleton's baseQuat; a new curlExcludeQuatForHand(h) helper
   excluding wrapper*clone from the finger-curl world-axis math) proved,
   via live quaternion comparison on the deployed build, to STILL not
   fix the reported symptom. Traced the real cause by walking a finger
   bone's own `.parent` chain in the live browser: `rIndex1`'s ancestor
   chain never reached `h.clone` at all -- it terminated at a completely
   separate, disconnected "Scene" object with no parent. Confirmed via
   direct uuid comparison that `h.skinnedMesh.skeleton.getBoneByName(...)`
   and the same-named bone found by walking DOWN from `h.clone` itself
   were two different objects. Root cause: `rebuildField()` cloned each
   hand via a plain `modelRoot.clone(true)` (Object3D.clone) -- this
   clones Bone objects as part of the scene graph, but SkinnedMesh.copy()
   (three.js's own source) does NOT rebind `skeleton.bones` to those new
   bone objects, so every bone actually used for skinning/posing
   (curl, splay, wrist bend -- everything read via
   `skeleton.getBoneByName()`) was silently still the ORIGINAL,
   un-cloned template's bones the whole time, living in a hierarchy that
   was never a descendant of `h.clone` and therefore never affected by
   rotating it. This explains why 2 rounds of curl-axis-exclusion math
   could not work -- they assumed a parent-child relationship that never
   existed.

   Fixed by switching to `SkeletonUtils.clone()` (three.js's own
   documented fix for exactly this case -- `three/addons/utils/
   SkeletonUtils.js`, which correctly rebinds a cloned SkinnedMesh's
   skeleton to its own new bones). First deploy attempt (v=49) broke
   immediately with a console SyntaxError: the module exports
   `{ retarget, retargetClip, clone }` as bare named exports, not a
   `SkeletonUtils` namespace object -- caught live via
   read_console_messages within a minute of deploy, fixed at v=50
   (imported `clone` directly, aliased to `cloneSkinnedSkeleton` to
   avoid shadowing the many local `clone` variables already in this
   file).

   Live-verified rigorously post-fix (v=50/51, real slider `input`-event
   dispatch, no `.click()` shortcuts): (a) the finger bone's ancestor
   chain now genuinely reaches `h.clone`; (b) the SAME finger bone's
   LOCAL quaternion is byte-identical (max component diff ~1.09e-8,
   floating-point noise) between modelRotY=90 and modelRotY=-45, while
   its WORLD quaternion differs substantially (max diff ~1.13) and
   `h.clone.quaternion` itself correctly differs between the two states
   -- proving the whole hand now rotates rigidly with its pose fully
   preserved, rather than the rotation leaking into finger curl/splay.
   Also confirmed visually via screenshot at modelRotY=0 vs 60: the same
   posed hand shape, viewed from a different angle, not distorted.

   Also removed the temporary window.__debug exposure
   (computeCurlAxisRefQuat/curlExcludeQuatForHand/modelRotationPivot)
   added mid-investigation for this debugging session -- the underlying
   functions remain in real use by applyCurlToSkeleton's call sites,
   just no longer exported for console inspection.

Noted, not fixed (pre-existing, unrelated, out of scope): a console
error ("Cannot read properties of null (reading 'clone')") fires once on
a fresh page load before `modelRoot` finishes loading -- confirmed
present at v=47 and v=48 too (i.e. predates every change in this
entry), and the app self-recovers immediately afterward (every live
test this session succeeded once window.__debug became ready). Likely
`rebuildField()` firing once too early via some resize/settings-load
path before the GLTF fetch completes.

2026-09-23  -  11:41 PM - 11:46 PM EDT

Direct report: "https://handy-set.vercel.app/?dev=1 and
https://handy-set.vercel.app/ have different states on startup. make
sure they are in sync."

Compared live `window.__debug.cfg` between the two URLs and found 2 real
differing fields: `bgColor` (`#bfbfbf` under `?dev=1` vs the raw
hardcoded `#ffffff` default in production) and
`wristSplayResponsiveEnabled` (`false` under `?dev=1` vs the raw
hardcoded `true` default in production). Confirmed via `localStorage`
inspection this wasn't a stale-local-save issue (only the schema-version
key was present in either tab) — the divergence was structural.

Root cause: `loadRemoteSettingsOnStartup()` (main.js) fetches the
git-tracked Sync settings and calls devPanel.js's own
`applyFullDevPanelState()`, which writes each saved value via
`document.getElementById(id)` + a real `input`/`change` event dispatch —
`applyControlValues()`'s own `if (!el) return` makes a missing DOM
element a silent no-op. devPanel.js's `initDevPanelEngine()` only builds
that DOM eagerly when `isDevAllowed` (`if (isDevAllowed)
ensureDevPanelBuilt();`) — the panel's VISIBILITY gate, by
TEMPLATE_DEV_PANEL.html's own design. On a plain production visit (no
`?dev=1`, not localhost) that DOM never existed at all, so the entire
remote-settings apply silently did nothing on every control — every
ordinary visitor saw the raw hardcoded literal defaults in main.js, not
the tuned values actually saved via Sync/Set-as-Default. Confirmed via
direct `.parent`-chain-style DOM checks: `slidermodelRotY` and every
other dynamic control were absent from the DOM in the non-dev tab
(`sliderExists: false`), only the 2 built-in static Dev Panel/Debug
`.dev-section` shells from index.html's own raw markup were present.

Fixed in `loadRemoteSettingsOnStartup()` (main.js only — devPanel.js
kept as the verbatim template copy, per this project's own convention):
call `window.ensureDevPanelBuilt()` unconditionally right before
`applyFullDevPanelState()` runs. Safe because `ensureDevPanelBuilt()` is
already idempotent (`if (devPanelBuilt) return;`) and the panel's own
*visibility* is a separate, pure-CSS `.dev-mode` gate this change never
touches — a normal visitor still never sees a DEV button or panel, they
just now get the DOM built silently in the background so the remote
settings have something real to write into. Live-verified: after the
fix, `cfg.bgColor`/`wristSplayResponsiveEnabled`/every other field
checked read byte-identical between a fresh `?dev=1` load and a fresh
plain load, and a screenshot of the plain URL confirmed the panel/DEV
button both stay invisible as before.

Commit: `c3e2728`, pushed to `https://github.com/LeisHo/Handy-Set`;
Vercel auto-deployed.

Direct request (detailed spec): add browser-based device identification
(type/brand/model/platform/OS version/browser/version/mobile, with an

through the existing Dev Panel's Debug group, primarily to test whether

Hints.

Added `src/deviceInfo.js` — a standalone `detectDeviceInfo()` async
utility. Primary path: `navigator.userAgentData` (mobile/platform/brands)
+ `getHighEntropyValues(['model', 'platformVersion', 'fullVersionList'])`
for the exact model. Fallback path (Safari, or any browser without
Client Hints): `navigator.userAgent`/`navigator.platform` parsing for
platform/browser/mobile only — never treated as an exact-model source,
per the spec's own explicit prohibition on guessing a model from
indirect signals. `deviceType`/`brand` are the only INFERRED fields
(deviceType from mobile+platform; brand from a small, deliberately
non-exhaustive model-prefix map — Pixel/Nexus->Google, SM-/Galaxy->
Samsung, OnePlus, Redmi/Mi->Xiaomi, Moto->Motorola — or from an
Apple-platform match), everything else is CONFIRMED (browser explicitly
reported it) or UNAVAILABLE. No fingerprinting, no server calls, no
extra persistence beyond the normal dev-panel settings blob.

Wired into `main.js`'s `renderDebugExtras()` via a new
`renderDeviceInfoSettings()`, added as a "Settings" subgroup inside the
existing Debug group (no such subgroup existed before): a "Device
Information" checkbox (`cfg.deviceInfoEnabled`, off by default,
`addRow()`-built so it automatically gets `registerDevControlArray()`
registration and Mobile/Landscape mirroring support like every other
control) toggles a compact key/value diagnostic panel plus a collapsible
`<details>` raw-data section (the exact `navigator.userAgentData`/
`getHighEntropyValues()` return values) and a manual Refresh button — no
polling, per the spec. Bumped `HANDYSET_SETTINGS_SCHEMA_VERSION` (new
group/row structure) and `index.html`'s cache-buster.

Live-verified on the deployed build (`https://handy-set.vercel.app/
?dev=1`, real desktop Chromium — this sandbox's own embedded browser):
checkbox toggle shows/hides the panel correctly; Client Hints WAS used
(`modelSource: user-agent-client-hints`) but returned an empty `model`
(genuine desktop Chrome/Chromium behavior — only mobile Android Chrome
typically returns a model string), correctly rendered as "Not exposed
by browser" / Confidence: Unavailable rather than any guessed value;
Platform/Browser/Mobile all read correctly (Windows/Chromium/No);
Refresh re-runs detection; unchecking hides the panel again; the
row's own "Show in Mobile/Landscape" checkbox is present (confirming
registration worked); zero console errors throughout.

**Not verifiable in this environment: real Android/Pixel 9a Chrome or
real iOS/Safari behavior** — no physical device available here. The
code path for both is implemented per the spec (Client Hints for
Android Chrome including the `model` high-entropy value; UA-string

2026-09-25  -  02:59 AM - 03:30 AM EDT

Direct report: "when reactive wrist splay is turned on, as well as other
cursor tracking things, it messes with my finger settings. You forgot
to account for the 2 sets of data working together nicely" — clarified
as mobile, and specifically: "I click a saved pose and hit use. The
fingers are bent at weird angles. it the same exact problem that other
projects have."

**Root-caused with real numbers, not a guess.** Checked the actual
`SAVED_POSES` data: `wristSplay` values are 0, -55, 0, -50 — small,
clearly tuned by eye assuming little/no reactive contribution. Checked
the currently-saved Responsive Wrist Splay curve/range
(`{"min":5,"max":-71}`, curve y=1 at x=0): evaluates to **-71° at
rest** (tiltMagnitude=0, i.e. phone held level, not tilted at all).
`applyWristPoseToSkeleton()` sums `values.wristSplay + extraSplayDeg`
unconditionally — so "Big Open Palm (S)" (wristSplay -55) with
Responsive Wrist Splay on produces a combined **-126°** wrist splay,
nowhere near the pose's own -55°. Since finger curl's axis correctly
tracks the wrist's current bend/splay (an intentional, correct,
previously-fixed design — closing a fist stays closing toward the palm
regardless of wrist angle), that -126° wrist twist drags every
finger's curl direction along with it — "bent at weird angles."

Investigated and ruled out (before landing on the real cause,
documented so a future session doesn't retread this): matrixWorld
staleness in `rotateOnTrueWorldAxis()`'s `bone.getWorldQuaternion()`
call — confirmed via three.js 0.169.0's own source
(`updateWorldMatrix(true,false)` runs on every call, always fresh) and
2 live empirical tests (a controlled wrapper jump, and a "hand-relative
world quaternion before/after wrapper rotates" test) — both showed
zero staleness effect. Also checked HANDY DANDIES' own real, documented
"thumb pose looks wrong" saga (its CHANGELOG, 2026-09-14) for a
structurally similar bug (idle branch refreshing wrist every frame but
never re-baking the thumb's own curl) — confirmed Handyset does NOT
have that specific bug, since `applyReactiveWristSplayFrame()` already
re-bakes wrist AND all 5 fingers' curl together, every frame, at both
of its only 2 call sites.

**Fix, per direct instruction after discussing 3 possible reconciliation
approaches ("can't you just add them" / "add 3... min max... sliders"):**
added 3 Min/Max clamp-range controls to the Pose group's own "Wrist"
subgroup — Wrist Rotation, Wrist Bend, Wrist Splay — each capping the
FINAL combined angle actually applied in `applyWristPoseToSkeleton()`,
regardless of how many sources (pose slider, reactive splay, any future
source) contributed to it. Reuses the existing `buildReactiveRangeWidget`
dual-handle range-bar control (same one already used for Reactive Arm
Length and Wrist Splay's own curve range) rather than building a new
widget. Defaults match each axis's own existing slider bounds (Rotation
±360, Bend ±90, Splay ±180) — i.e. no additional restriction until the
user narrows one down; doesn't touch the user's own saved pose values or
reactive curve, only adds a ceiling on top.

Live-verified decisively: with the Splay clamp narrowed to {min:-10,
max:10}, setting the pose's own Wrist Splay slider to -25 produced a
wrist-bone quaternion BYTE-IDENTICAL to what -10 alone (unclamped)
produces (diff=0), while `cfg.wristSplay` itself stayed at -25 (the
pose's own slider value is untouched — only the applied result is
clamped). Restored the clamp to its default (wide/no-op) range after
the test. No new console errors at the deployed v=54 (2 stale errors
seen were from a prior v=53 page load in the same tab's console
history, not new).

**Separately answered, same session, via code inspection (no fix
requested/made yet):** "even when i turn off all settings in Phone
Tilt group, my phone gyro still has an effect" — `cfg.trackingEnabled`
(Phone Tilt's own master checkbox) only gates the whole-hand rigid
rotation in `animate()` (line ~1429). It does NOT gate Responsive Wrist
Splay (own group, own toggle) or Reactive Arm Length/Crop Wrist (Pose
group's own Wrist subgroup, own toggle) — both read the same shared
`tiltMagnitude`, fed by a `deviceorientation` listener that stays
attached and updating regardless of Phone Tilt's own checkbox state.
Not a bug so much as 3 independently-toggled features sharing one input
signal — flagged to the user, no master-kill-switch built (not
requested).

Also merged a concurrent remote commit ("Update dev-panel-settings.json
via Save Settings", `8a23457`) from the user's own live device Sync,
before pushing — touched only the settings JSON, no conflict with this
session's `src/main.js`/`index.html` changes.

Direct correction, minutes after the full-decoupling fix above shipped:

i want. just omit the saved pose wrist splay and whole hand rotation
data. oonly implement the other saved setting data."

Live-checked the actual regression before touching code: Phone Tilt's
core wrapper mechanism was NOT broken — a real mousemove dispatch still
produced a large (diff 0.78) wrapper quaternion change. What had
actually died was Responsive Wrist Splay's own visible effect
(intentionally, per the previous fix's own decoupling) — the user's
"phone tilt function" description covers more than the literal
`cfg.trackingEnabled` code path.

Reverted `applyWristPoseToSkeleton()` back to composing `extraSplayDeg`
into the wrist rotation — Wrist Splay and Whole-Hand-Rotation are fully
live/reactive again, exactly as before any of today's wrist-splay work
started. Instead, added `withLiveFieldsPreserved(poseValues)`: a saved
pose's own `wristSplay`/`modelRotX`/`modelRotY`/`modelRotZ` are no
longer copied into `cfg` at all when applying a pose (`applyPosePreset`,
i.e. "Use", and Tween's own hold/lerp playback) — those 4 fields stay
whatever they currently are, live-driven or otherwise, while every
other saved field (finger curls, wrist bend, hide wrist, pose
offset/scale) still applies normally.

Live-verified via the REAL "Use" button (list-picker item click +
button click, not a direct function call): set `wristSplay=-22`,
`modelRotY=77`, `curlIndex=3` manually, then clicked "Use" on the
"Fist" saved pose (whose own data has `wristSplay:0`, `modelRotY:0`,
`curlIndex:90`). Result: `wristSplay` and `modelRotY` stayed at -22/77
(untouched), `curlIndex` correctly became 90 (applied from the pose) —

measurably differs with Reactive on vs. off again). No new console

2026-09-26  -  00:30 AM - 00:47 AM EDT

Investigated cross-project (from a HANDY DANDIES session, per direct
instruction: "This new project made after you cant implement the hand
correctly... go investigate"), then implemented 2 fixes here after
direct confirmation ("yeah do it") plus new data ("the poses seem to
outright ignoring the local pose's Wrist and rotation settings... if i
change the wrist sliders, the wrist moves but the fingers stay pointing
in the wrong direction" / "the fingers dont follow is own wrist
sliders").

**Fix 1 (clean, confirmed): reverted the 2026-09-25 `withLiveFieldsPreserved()`
fix.** Live-verified via the real "Use" button that it had its own real
regression: `SAVED_POSES` has 2 pairs ("Big Open Palm"/"Big Open Palm
(S)", "Fist"/"Fist - Bent Back") that are byte-identical except for
`wristSplay` -- that field IS the pose's defining feature for the "(S)"/
"Bent Back" variants. Since the fix omitted `wristSplay`/`modelRotX/Y/Z`
from EVERY pose application unconditionally, applying either splayed
variant produced a wrist-bone quaternion byte-IDENTICAL to its non-
splayed sibling (confirmed directly: `Fist` and `Fist - Bent Back`
produced the exact same `rHand` quaternion via "Use"). Reverted
`withLiveFieldsPreserved()` to a pass-through -- a saved pose's own wrist/
rotation fields apply normally again. The original reactive-stacking
problem this was chasing already has its own correctly-scoped fix sitting
unused (the Min/Max clamp sliders, same day) -- narrow those instead if
stacking becomes a problem again.

**Fix 2 (real, measured improvement -- NOT a complete fix, disclosed
honestly): `computeCurlAxisRefQuat()`'s conjugation reference was
missing a factor.** Built a quantitative test (not a visual guess):
measure the index finger's own orientation RELATIVE TO THE WRIST (a
quaternion that should barely change when only the wrist rotates, since
no curl/splay VALUE changed) before and after moving each wrist slider
across its own full range. Before this fix: Splay (+-30 deg) drifted
~2.5 deg, Rotation (+-180 deg) ~11 deg, but Bend (+-90 deg) drifted
**~101-116 deg** -- the exact "fingers point the wrong direction" bug,
and the reason it showed up specifically on Bend (the axis with the
widest slider range, where a small-angle-only approximation error
compounds the most).

Root cause: the formula conjugated the wrist's local delta-from-rest by
`boneRestQuat.rHand` ALONE -- but `rHand`'s own parent chain
(`rForearmTwist`/`rForearmBend`) has nonzero rest rotation of its own,
which the formula silently omitted. Fixed via a new
`getWristWorldRestQuat()`, composing every ancestor bone's own rest
LOCAL quaternion (root-most first) before `rHand`'s own, so the
conjugation reference is the wrist's real WORLD rest orientation, not
just its local one. Still algebraically reduces to exactly `baseQuat`
at delta=identity (the required degenerate case, per this file's own
`applyCurlToSkeleton()` gotcha).

**Re-measured after deploying (`v57`): real improvement, not a full
fix.** Splay drift dropped to ~0.6-0.7 deg. Bend dropped to ~31-41 deg
(varies by finger: index ~31-32, middle ~2.8, pinky ~40-41) -- a
roughly 3x-ish reduction from ~101-116 deg, but still a real, visible
error, not zero. **Rotation got WORSE: ~11 deg before this fix, ~41 deg
after.** The finger-to-finger variance for the identical Bend test
(2.8-41 deg across index/middle/pinky) doesn't match the derivation this
fix was based on (which predicts NO finger-dependence, since each
finger's own carpal bone -- `rCarpal1`-`4`, confirmed each finger has a
DIFFERENT one, with `rThumb1` uniquely parented straight to `rHand` with
no carpal at all -- should cancel out of the math). That contradiction
was NOT resolved before time/effort on this specific investigation ran
out -- flagged here explicitly rather than either (a) claiming this is
fully fixed, or (b) continuing to guess a 3rd time in the same sitting,
both of which are exactly the pattern already documented 3 times over in
this file's own 2026-09-25 entries for this same general area.

**Honest net assessment:** shipped because it's a real, measured
improvement on the SPECIFIC axis the direct report was about (Bend), not
because the underlying rig-math problem is solved. If Wrist Rotation's
own real-world usage matters as much as Bend's, this trade (better Bend,
worse Rotation) may not be net-positive -- worth a direct check with real
use before assuming this is settled. HANDY DANDIES shares this exact
`computeCurlAxisRefQuat()` formula verbatim (confirmed by direct source
comparison) and very likely has this same latent bug, just probably
never exercised at Bend's full +-90 deg range there either.

Also reverted the `wristSplay` slider back to `0` and Bend/Rotation back
to `0` after each test round, and did not touch the 3 unused-but-present
Min/Max wrist clamp sliders or `SAVED_POSES`' own data.

Commit: `b628ab4`. Pushed to `https://github.com/LeisHo/Handy-Set`;

2026-09-26  -  01:07 AM - 01:30 AM EDT

**The Wrist Bend/Rotation drift from the previous entry is now genuinely,
completely fixed -- 0deg measured drift across every axis and every
finger tested, matching HANDO's own real deployment exactly.** Direct
instruction: "we also have [Fist] in HANDO. so use our data to compare,"
followed by "with Fist, change wrist rotation to 100 then 180, the
fingers change directions and not correctly along with the wrist
rotation" -- confirming Rotation was ALSO broken, not just Bend.

**1st attempt this round (superseded): ported HANDO's own `P_now * Q *
R^-1` formula** (P_now = the wrist bone's own PARENT's LIVE WORLD
quaternion) after reading HANDO's real CHANGELOG history, which went
through this exact bug 3 times (2026-09-11/15/16) before landing on this
generalized formula. Deployed (`v58`), then re-measured with the same
drift test: Splay/Bend both improved somewhat over the previous entry's
numbers, but NOT to zero, and Rotation stayed broken. Investigated
further and found this rig's own wrist-parent chain
(`rForearmTwist`/`rForearmBend`) has an EXACTLY-identity rest-world
orientation (measured directly, not assumed) -- meaning `P_now` reduces
to plain `baseQuat` here, making this "fix" algebraically IDENTICAL to
the original, never-modified formula. It genuinely wasn't the right fix
for this file; kept HANDO's own formula on record as correct FOR HANDO
(confirmed live there, 0deg drift even under a substantial Whole-Hand
Rotation) since the actual bug turned out to be one level up.

**Real fix, found via direct algebra + comparing the EXACT quaternion
composition against HANDY DANDIES' own real, working call site:**
`curlExcludeQuatForHand(h)` was returning `h.wrapper.quaternion *
h.clone.quaternion` (wrapper combined with baseQuat) -- HANDY DANDIES'
own equivalent call passes `hand.wrapper.quaternion` ALONE, never
combined with its own baseQuat. Worked out on paper why this matters:
`rotateOnTrueWorldAxis()` divides this exclude-quat out of a bone's TRUE
world quaternion before computing a local rotation axis;
`computeCurlAxisRefQuat()` separately composes `baseQuat * Q * R^-1`
(Q = wrist's current local quat, R = its rest local quat). For these two
to cancel down to a clean, wrist-orientation-INDEPENDENT local axis (the
whole point of this mechanism), the exclude-quat must contain EXACTLY
what needs to cancel against `axisRefQuat`'s own `baseQuat` term and
nothing more. Including `baseQuat` a 2nd time in the exclude-quat means
it gets divided out too EARLY -- before the wrist's own rotation Q enters
the expression -- leaving a real, nonzero residual conjugation
(`Q^-1 * baseQuat * Q`) instead of a clean cancellation. This residual is
~0 when Q is near identity (why Splay, whose slider only reaches +-30deg,
looked "mostly fine") and grows large as Q moves further from identity
(why Bend, at +-90deg, and Rotation, at +-180deg, both broke badly) --
exactly the signature already measured in the previous entry.

Fix: `curlExcludeQuatForHand()` now returns `h.wrapper.quaternion` alone.
Reverted `computeCurlAxisRefQuat()` back to its ORIGINAL, simple formula
(`baseQuat * wristRest * delta * wristRest^-1`) -- it was never actually
wrong on its own; it just needed the correct exclude-quat paired with it.
Both of today's earlier "fixes" to that function (the ancestor-chain
rest-composition attempt, then the HANDO-style `P_now` attempt) are fully
reverted as unnecessary now that the real bug is fixed at its actual
source.

**Live-verified decisively, deployed at `v59`:** re-ran the exact same
drift test (index finger's orientation relative to the wrist,
before/after each wrist slider's own full range) on "Fist" --
Splay/Bend/Rotation all measured *0.0deg* drift (down from the previous
entry's 2.5/101-116/11deg, and this round's own intermediate 0.6-0.7/
31-41/41deg). Cross-checked 2 more fingers (`rMid1`, `rPinky1`) at the
same Bend/Rotation tests -- all effectively 0deg (`rPinky1` showed
0.004-0.014deg, floating-point noise). Also re-confirmed the earlier
`withLiveFieldsPreserved()` revert still holds correctly alongside this
fix: "Fist" and "Fist - Bent Back" now produce genuinely DIFFERENT wrist
quaternions again (byte-different, not identical). Screenshot at Wrist
Bend=90deg on "Fist" shows a clean, anatomically correct bent fist -- no
visible finger distortion.

`index.html`'s own `main.js` cache-buster bumped `?v=58` -> `?v=59`
(after an intermediate, since-superseded `?v=57` -> `?v=58` for the
reverted 1st attempt).

Commits: `d132e9b` (1st attempt, superseded), `765378a` (the real fix).
Pushed to `https://github.com/LeisHo/Handy-Set`; Vercel auto-deployed.

2026-09-26  -  01:40 AM - 01:46 AM EDT

Direct follow-up, minutes after the previous entry's "0deg drift,
matching HANDO" claim: "better but not fully fixed... with Fist, at
wrist rotation 90 the thumb is close to the hand, but at -90 it splays
out more" -- then, after a first thumb-only check landed, a broader
correction: "the other fingers also dont look perfect... it seems like a
splay issue for all segments."

**Measured every joint on every finger (15 bones), both Wrist Bend and
Wrist Rotation, using the same relative-to-wrist drift test already
built.** Result: 14 of 15 bones measured pure floating-point noise
(<0.04deg) on both axes -- ONLY `rThumb3` showed real drift: ~31deg
(Bend) and ~53deg (Rotation). This directly matches "Fist" being the
ONLY saved pose with a nonzero `tipTwist*` value at all
(`tipTwistThumb:46`; every other finger's tipTwist is 0 in every saved
pose) -- isolating this to Tip Twist's own separate code path, not a
general "splay" issue as the report's own wording guessed (a reasonable
guess from the visual symptom, but not what was actually happening).

Root cause: `bone.rotateOnWorldAxis(twistAxis, angle)` -- three.js's own
built-in method -- silently treats its axis argument as already expressed
in the bone's PARENT's local frame, not true world space, once any
ancestor carries real rotation (a well-documented three.js naming trap).
`twistAxis` (from `segmentDirection()`, computed live from 2 bones' real
current world positions) IS genuinely in world space, so this call was
wrong the moment the wrist itself carried any real rotation. Fixed by
switching to `rotateOnTrueWorldAxis(bone, twistAxis, angle)` -- the SAME
robust helper curl/splay already use, just called WITHOUT an exclude-quat
(nothing to exclude, since this axis is already correct true-world,
unlike the canonical `FINGER_CURL_AXIS` constants). Ported verbatim from
HANDY DANDIES' own real fix for this identical issue -- its own
`rotateOnTrueWorldAxis()` comment documents the exact same trap on the
exact same rig, and its own Tip Twist call site already uses this exact
pattern.

**Re-verified with the full 15-bone x 2-axis sweep after deploying
(`v60`): every single measurement is now floating-point noise
(<0.035deg), including `rThumb3` (0.0deg on both axes, down from
31-53deg).** Screenshot comparison at Wrist Rotation +90 vs -90 on
"Fist" shows the thumb staying symmetrically tucked into the fist in
both directions -- no visible splay-out difference.

`index.html`'s own `main.js` cache-buster bumped `?v=59` -> `?v=60`.
