2026-09-26  -  03:31 AM - 03:54 AM EDT

Direct report: "the same issue is occurring but with Whole Hand
rotation. All 3 axes" -- the exact curl-axis-tracks-a-transform bug
class this session already root-caused and fixed for the wrist (the
`01:07 AM - 01:30 AM EDT` entry above), now showing up for
modelRotX/Y/Z.

Root cause: `applyCurl()`, `applyPoseValuesToHand()`, and
`applyReactiveWristSplayFrame()` all passed `alignQuat`
(modelRot-EXCLUDED) as `applyCurlToSkeleton()`'s own `baseQuat` argument
-- a 2026-09-21 workaround (see that date's own comment, left in place
per this file's own correction convention) for a double-counting bug
that existed in `curlExcludeQuatForHand()` AT THE TIME: it used to also
exclude `h.clone.quaternion`, which itself already contains
`modelRotQuat` (via `computeBaseQuatFromValues()`). With
baseQuat=h.currentBaseQuat (bakes in modelRotQuat) AND exclude=wrapper*
clone (ALSO bakes in modelRotQuat a 2nd time), modelRotQuat was
double-counted -- exactly the wrist bug's own double-counting pattern,
just for a different transform. Switching baseQuat to `alignQuat` was a
workaround for that, not a real fix, and was never revisited once this
session's earlier round fixed `curlExcludeQuatForHand()` to exclude
`wrapper.quaternion` ALONE -- which removed the double-counting on the
EXCLUDE side but left this asymmetry (baseQuat avoiding modelRotQuat
while the exclude no longer needs it to).

Cross-checked against HANDY DANDIES' own real, working call site before
touching anything: `applyCurlToSkeleton(fingerName,
hand.skinnedMesh.skeleton, cloneBaseQuat, hand.wrapper.quaternion)`,
where `cloneBaseQuat = alignQuat.copy().multiply(wholeHandRotQuat)` --
i.e. baseQuat there DOES bake in Whole-Hand Rotation, paired with a
wrapper-alone exclude. Read directly from that file's source, not
assumed from memory.

Verified via a standalone quaternion-math script (mirroring
`rotateOnTrueWorldAxis()`/`computeCurlAxisRefQuat()` exactly, no browser
needed) BEFORE editing this file: the current buggy code
(baseQuat=alignQuat) drifts 12.4-71.3deg across 5 test cases (modelRotX
alone, modelRotY alone, modelRotZ alone, all 3 combined, and the
degenerate wristBend=wristSplay=0 case -- this project's own
documented lesson about always testing the delta=identity degenerate
case, not just nonzero cases). The fix (baseQuat=h.currentBaseQuat)
measures 0.0000deg drift on all 5.

Fixed all 3 real call sites (`applyCurl()`,
`applyPoseValuesToHand()`, `applyReactiveWristSplayFrame()`) to pass
`h.currentBaseQuat` instead of `alignQuat`. `h.currentBaseQuat` is
guaranteed fresh at every one of these call sites --
`applyPoseValuesToHand()` sets it immediately before applying curl for
every finger on the same hand.

Live-verified against the real running app (not just the standalone
script): dispatched real `input` events on the actual dev-panel sliders
(`slidermodelRotX/Y/Z`, `slidercurlIndex`) -- never a direct function
call, since `applyPoseValuesToHand` isn't exposed on `window.__debug`
here -- and measured the index finger's own orientation RELATIVE TO THE
WRIST bone (must be invariant to a rigid whole-hand rotation) via
`getWorldQuaternion()` on both bones. Result: 0.0058-0.0245deg drift
(floating-point noise, same threshold this session's other fixes used)
across modelRotX alone, modelRotZ alone, and all 3 axes combined.

2026-09-26  -  09:02 AM - 09:06 AM EDT

Direct follow-up report, after the rotation/curl-tracking fixes above:
"beter but i think something is still wrong with the finger and thumb
splay settings. with the post Fist, the figner splays arent showing
correctly its not aggregiously wrong, but its still not the pose i
intended."

Read HANDY DANDIES' real `applyCurlToSkeleton()` directly (not
reconstructed) and compared its per-joint rotation ORDER against this
project's own: HANDY DANDIES applies **splay, then splay2, then curl
(+bias), then base/mid/tip-only curl, then tip twist** — this project
had **curl FIRST**, splay/splay2 after. Since `rotateOnTrueWorldAxis()`
reads the bone's `getWorldQuaternion()` fresh on every call, whichever
rotation runs first sees the bone still at rest, but every rotation
after it sees the PRIOR rotation already baked in — its own
world-axis-to-local conversion gets conjugated by that prior rotation's
inverse. With curl running first, splay's own axis was being computed
relative to the CURLED joint instead of rest — a real error that scales
with how much curl is simultaneously active on that same joint, which
is exactly "not aggressively wrong" (a secondary coupling effect on top
of an otherwise-correct pose) rather than obviously broken.

Fixed by reordering to match HANDY DANDIES exactly — no new math, every
individual `rotateOnTrueWorldAxis()` call was already verified correct
in this session's earlier rounds; only the sequence changed.

Live-verified: applied Fist's real index (curl 90, splay 18) and thumb
(curl 75, splay 42, splay2 13, curlBias 32) values via the real
sliders — every tested bone quaternion (`rThumb1-3`, `rIndex1-3`) came
back a well-formed unit quaternion (magnitude ~1.0000000, no NaN), and
a screenshot shows a natural, correctly-articulated closed fist with
clean knuckle definition, no twisted or degenerate geometry. No console
errors at the deployed build.

`index.html`'s own `main.js` cache-buster bumped `?v=61` -> `?v=62`.

`https://github.com/LeisHo/Handy-Set`; Vercel auto-deployed.

2026-09-27

Direct report: "Trackign Enable checkbox doesnt trigger an effect in
itself, it is a global on off switch for the Responsive Rwist Splay,
Palm Rotation, and Wrist Crop. Right now when i turn on Tracking
Enabled the whole arm is rotating around some origin anchor outside of

turned off, turning trackingenabled on shouldnt show any visible
change."

A separate session (Claude Haiku 4.5, commit `dd070b3`, ~8 minutes
earlier the same morning) had already attempted a fix for the same
symptom -- it moved `updateTiltTarget()` inside the input-check block
in `animate()`, on the theory that a stray earlier call to it (outside
the block) was the cause. That did not actually fix it: the real bug
was that the whole `hands.forEach` body computing and applying the
arm's lookAt-based reorientation (`h.wrapper.quaternion.slerp(desired,
cfg.trackingDamping)`) ran unconditionally whenever
`cfg.trackingEnabled && hands.length && (latestOrientation !== null ||
lastInputSource === 'mouse')` -- completely independent of

the checkbox that actually asks for this rotation. So Tracking Enabled
alone, with the other 3 feature checkboxes off, still visibly slerped
the whole wrapper toward `tiltTarget` every frame -- exactly the
reported "arm rotating around some origin outside the arm."

Fixed by gating the whole `hands.forEach` reorientation body on
`cfg.palmFacesCursor` (it IS the Palm Rotation feature), while still
letting `updateTiltTarget()` run whenever tracking + input are present
-- Responsive Wrist Splay and Wrist Crop read `tiltMagnitude`/
`tiltTarget` every frame regardless of Palm Faces Cursor, so that data
pipeline has to keep running. Also added an explicit
`if (!cfg.trackingEnabled) return 0` to `computeArmLengthT()` and
`computeResponsiveWristSplayDeg()` (main.js:338-360ish), so Wrist Crop
and Responsive Wrist Splay stop reacting to `tiltMagnitude` when
tracking itself is off, not just when their own individual toggle is
off -- matching the user's explicit spec that Tracking Enabled is a
master AND-gate for all 3 features, not a visible effect of its own.

Live-verified via direct state manipulation (not just code reading):
set `cfg.trackingEnabled = true`, `cfg.palmFacesCursor = false`,
`cfg.wristSplayResponsiveEnabled = false`, `cfg.cropWristEnabled =
false`, then dispatched real mouse movement across the page --
`hands[0].wrapper.quaternion` stayed at identity `[0,0,0,1]`
throughout. Flipping `cfg.palmFacesCursor = true` and moving the mouse
again produced a real, non-identity wrapper quaternion, confirming the
legitimate Palm Rotation path still works.

2026-09-27  -  (2nd, same day)

Direct report, immediately after the Tracking Enabled fix above: "Palm
faces cursor doesnt work correctly. It should just be rotating to
follow the cursor. Also provide a Palm Face Rotation slider just like
Handy Dandies bbut it should be adsusting the palm rotation around the
Y axis of the arm. which may be different from Handy Dandies."

Read HANDY DANDIES' real Palm Faces Cursor / Palm Face Rotation
implementation directly (per the user's own new standing rule: always
check a named reference project before implementing). Found HANDYSET
already had a STALE, never-wired-up partial port of it
(`computeRadialRollDeg()`/`computeRollQuat()`, defined at module scope
but never called from `animate()`) -- the code actually running there
was a different, ad-hoc mechanism: an extra roll composed on top of
the lookAt, using a Y axis read from `rForearmBend`'s own LIVE
(previous-frame) world quaternion every frame. That's a moving
reference frame that compounds with itself frame to frame, and a
redundant second cursor-relative rotation stacked on the lookAt's own
-- consistent with "doesn't work correctly."

Fixed by making Palm Faces Cursor produce ONLY the lookAt (`desired` =
lookAt(...) when the checkbox is on, identity otherwise) -- "just
rotating to follow the cursor," verified live by comparing the real
wrapper quaternion against an independently-computed pure lookAt
quaternion (angleTo ~0.0000017 deg, floating-point noise).

Added the new Palm Face Rotation (Deg) slider (-180 to 180, def 0),
composed onto `desired` in WORLD space via `premultiply` (applied
AFTER the lookAt, not before) around world UP (0,1,0) -- a true "spin
around vertical" regardless of current facing direction. Deliberately
NOT Handy Dandies' own axis: an earlier draft measured
`rForearmBend`'s own local Y at bind pose (mirroring how
`wristCropNormalAligned` is measured) and found it numerically
IDENTICAL to `wristCropNormalAligned` to 6 decimal places -- this
rig's bones are authored with local Y running along their own length,
so that axis choice would have made the new slider indistinguishable
from Handy Dandies' own, failing the user's explicit "may be
different" requirement. Switched to plain world UP instead: simpler,
unambiguous, and trivially distinct from Handy Dandies' roughly
forward/-Z-ish axis. Verified live two ways: (1) direct math --
`cfg.palmFaceRotationOffset = 45` with Palm Faces Cursor off produced
a wrapper quaternion exactly matching a 45 deg rotation around
(0,1,0) (angleTo 0 deg); (2) through the real dev-panel slider control
itself -- typed 90 into the click-to-type field, wrapper quaternion
exactly matched a 90 deg world-Y rotation (angleTo 0 deg).

Removed the dead `computeRadialRollDeg()`/`computeRollQuat()` (the
stale, never-wired incomplete port mentioned above) rather than
leaving 2 competing, unused-vs-used roll mechanisms in the file.

2026-09-27  -  (3rd, same day)

Direct correction of the 2nd-round fix above: "i said palm face
rotation cursor tracking should be rotating the entire arm by the Y
axis of the forearm bone. the rotation should be anchored to the base
point of the forearm bone." Followed mid-turn by: "its a locaize
rotation."

Re-read the 2nd round's own reasoning and realized it had drawn the
wrong conclusion from a correct finding: it measured the
`rForearmBend` bone's own local Y axis, found it numerically
coincided with `wristCropNormalAligned` on this rig (bones authored
with local Y along their own length), and swapped to world UP to
avoid that "coincidence" -- but the user wants the real forearm-bone Y
axis specifically, whether or not it happens to match Handy Dandies'
own axis. Also re-read the 2nd round's own architecture: Palm Faces
Cursor was a full 3D `lookAt`, with Palm Face Rotation as a separate
roll composed on top -- this message clarifies both are meant to be
ONE combined, LOCAL, single-axis rotation instead, matching HANDY
DANDIES' own `computeRollQuat()` composition pattern (a dynamic angle
from `computeRadialRollDeg()` when Palm Faces Cursor is on, plus the
slider's own offset, both added together, both around one fixed
local axis) -- just with a different axis and anchor than HANDY
DANDIES uses.

Restored `armYAxisAligned` (the bone's own local Y, measured once at
bind pose) and `computeRadialRollDeg()` (both removed in the 2nd
round). Replaced the lookAt-based `desired` with:
`desired = Quaternion.setFromAxisAngle(armYAxisAligned, degToRad(baseDeg + offsetDeg))`
where `baseDeg = palmFacesCursor ? computeRadialRollDeg(wrapperPos,
tiltTarget) : 0`.

New: the rotation is ANCHORED at the forearm bone's own base position
-- a requirement that didn't exist in either prior round. Recomputes a
local-space pivot every frame from `h.clone`'s LIVE transform (its
scale/quaternion/position all change as poses/model-rotation are
applied): `pivotLocal = forearmPosRaw * scale, rotated by
h.clone.quaternion, plus h.clone.position` -- then applies the exact
same `position = pivot - rotation*pivot` technique
`applyModelRootTransform()` already uses for `modelRotationPivot`, one
level up (`h.wrapper` instead of `h.clone`). Added `h.basePosition` (a
new per-hand field storing the hand's own field-grid placement, set by
`relayoutField()`) since this now overwrites `h.wrapper.position`
outright every frame instead of leaving it untouched as before.

Live-verified precisely, not just visually: with `palmFaceRotationOffset
= 60` (Palm Faces Cursor off), `hands[0].wrapper.quaternion` matched
an independently-computed `Quaternion.setFromAxisAngle(armYAxisAligned,
60deg)` EXACTLY (`angleTo` 0 deg) -- confirming the axis. The
`rForearmBend` bone's own live WORLD position, read directly via
`getWorldPosition()`, was UNCHANGED before vs. after applying the 60
deg rotation to floating-point noise only (~2e-15 across all 3
components) -- confirming the anchor: the forearm base did not move
at all despite the whole arm visibly rotating 60 deg around it. Same
2 checks repeated with Palm Faces Cursor's own dynamic angle (real
mouse movement, `computeRadialRollDeg` against the live `tiltTarget`)
instead of a fixed slider value -- both passed identically (`angleTo`
0 deg, forearm position unchanged).

`index.html`'s own `main.js` cache-buster bumped `?v=73` -> `?v=74`.

2026-09-27  -  (4th, same day)

Direct correction of the 3rd-round fix above: "bro you are rotating
the hand around x or z axis. I want the Forearms Y axis to be the
axis of rotation. So it should be rotating parallel to the XZ plane
of the forearm bone."

Rather than re-deriving from first principles a 4th time, measured
`rForearmBend`'s actual local AND world quaternion directly and
exposed them (a temporary `window.__armAxisDebug`, removed once the
question was answered). Both are identity to ~1e-8 -- this rig keeps
every bone completely UNROTATED at bind pose; visual bone orientation
is baked into the mesh/vertex geometry instead, not into bone
transforms (a real, common glTF export convention, not a bug in the
asset). This means "the bone's raw local Y axis" -- what the 3rd
round's `armYAxisAligned` measured -- reduces to "canonical raw
(0,1,0) rotated by `alignQuat` alone" (since the bone's own
world-quaternion contribution is a no-op). Measured all 3 raw local
axes (X/Y/Z) the same way and compared against `wristCropNormalAligned`
(the bone's own known LENGTH direction): raw Y, once aligned, lands at
`[-0.030, -0.014, -0.999]` -- numerically identical to
`wristCropNormalAligned` (confirming the 3rd round's own "coincidence"
finding was real, not a measurement error) -- while raw Z, once
aligned, lands at `[0.030, 0.999, -0.015]`, i.e. roughly ALIGNED +Y.
So the 3rd round's axis was a mathematically correct reading of the
bone's RAW-space Y -- it just isn't the same vector as Y in the
ALIGNED/rendering frame this whole file already uses for X/Y/Z
(camera position, `modelRotY`, etc.), which is what "the forearm's Y
axis" actually has to mean once the bone's own (nonexistent) local
rotation is factored out.

Fixed by using plain canonical `UP` (0,1,0) directly as the rotation
axis -- removed `armYAxisAligned`'s bind-pose bone measurement
entirely (a detailed comment at the removal site in `main.js`
preserves the reasoning above for a future round, so it isn't
re-derived from scratch again). The anchor/pivot logic from the 3rd
round is untouched -- this was purely an axis bug, not a pivot bug.

Live-verified via direct state comparison (not a screenshot -- see
below for why): with `palmFaceRotationOffset = 90`,
`hands[0].wrapper.quaternion` matched
`Quaternion.setFromAxisAngle((0,1,0), 90deg)` exactly (`angleTo`
0deg), and the `rForearmBend` bone's real world position (read via
`getWorldPosition()`) was unchanged to floating-point noise (~1e-14)
across the rotation -- confirming both the axis and the anchor
simultaneously.

**A visual screenshot check at 30deg/90deg appeared to show the hand
vanish entirely** -- investigated rather than dismissed, since this
directly contradicted the precise numeric verification above. Direct
pixel sampling of the actual canvas (`drawImage` onto an offscreen
canvas + `getImageData`) showed EVERY pixel reading as "non-white"
uniformly across the whole canvas, at both 0deg and the "vanished"
angles alike -- the exact signature of this sandbox's own
already-documented WebGL-canvas-readback gotcha (`canvas.toDataURL()`/
`drawImage` returning a stale or blank buffer when read outside the
render loop, since this canvas has no `preserveDrawingBuffer`) rather
than a real rendering difference. Screenshot-tool captures showed the
same pattern. Concluded the numeric verification (which reads live
Three.js object state directly, not the canvas) is the reliable check
here, not the screenshot/pixel read -- consistent with this
project's own prior documented findings about this exact sandbox's
canvas-readback limitations.

2026-09-27  -  (5th, same day)

Direct report: "does the cursor trackig have to track a 3d projected
point? right now its not tracking correctly since the actual tracking
point isnt aligned with my cursor" -- clarified mid-investigation
("by cursor trakcign i meant the phone tilt bro") to mean this
project's own Phone Tilt group, not a literal "cursor tracking"
feature (a separate, brief detour checked 3JS ENGINE for an unrelated
new commit there, per the user's own request, before returning here).

First attempt: suspected `updateTiltTarget()`'s mouse-path raycast --
both a center-ray and a cursor-ray hit a plane fixed at Z=0 to compute
an XY offset, then `tiltTarget.z` was set independently to
`fieldRadius * targetDepthFactor` (default 0.6, never 0). Tried moving
`cursorTargetPlane` itself to that same depth so both raycasts and the
final Z agreed. Live-verified by projecting the resulting `tiltTarget`
back through the camera (`.project(camera)`) and comparing to the real
cursor pixel -- found the reprojection landed nowhere close (off-canvas
entirely), proving this "fix" hadn't actually helped. Root cause of
the non-fix: moving the plane changes a point's Z, which changes WHERE
that point reprojects on screen under perspective, but never changes
the XY OFFSET's own direction -- both raycasts always shared one plane
either way, before and after. Reverted per direct correction: "cant
you just raycast to a specific groundplane height and use that as 0?
then add the offset from the slider depth thing" -- back to a FIXED
ground plane (Z=0, which already matches `hand.wrapper.position.z`,
always 0 per `relayoutField()`) for the XY offset, with
`targetDepthFactor` added on top as an independent Z, exactly as the
code originally had it.

The real, separate bug surfaced by a concurrent, related request ("on
mobile, i wan the hand to rotate in the direction i am tilting. the
more i tilt the more the hand rotates to face that direction"):
`computeRadialRollDeg()` is a pure `atan2()` -- direction only, blind
to magnitude. Any nonzero cursor offset OR device tilt, however small,
produced the exact SAME full-strength rotation angle as a large one --
explaining both symptoms at once: mouse tracking felt twitchy/
disconnected near screen-center (a tiny, near-(0,0) offset is also
numerically noisy for atan2, and was getting full weight), and on
mobile any small tilt snapped instantly to the full "face that
direction" rotation instead of easing in proportionally -- the
`tiltMagnitude` scaling the mobile request asked for didn't actually
exist yet.

Fixed with one change covering both input paths at once, since they
share this same formula in `animate()`: `baseDeg =
computeRadialRollDeg(...) * tiltMagnitude` (previously unscaled).
`tiltMagnitude` (0-1) was already computed for both mouse
(`handleMouseMoveFallback()`) and device orientation
(`handleDeviceOrientation()`) -- reused directly, no new state needed.

Live-verified via real mouse movement + direct quaternion reads at 3
screen positions: near-center (203,457, canvas center) -> ~-0.22 deg
(effectively neutral); a moderate offset (top-right, 400,50) ->
~-25.9 deg; a screen corner (bottom-right, 400,900) -> ~-156.0 deg
(a different final angle than the top-right corner, correctly
reflecting a different compass DIRECTION, not just distance) --
smooth, monotonic scaling with distance from center at every tested
point, not a snap.

`index.html`'s own `main.js` cache-buster bumped `?v=77` -> `?v=78`.

2026-09-27  -  (6th, same day)

Direct correction: "ok the rotation still isnt looking good on desktop.
I guess I didnt give you a point to measre from. Use the base point of
the forearm bone. then project that perpendicular to the camera on to
the ground plane. then use that point and the projected cursor point

Every prior round on this feature measured the cursor's offset from
SCREEN CENTER -- an arbitrary reference with no relationship to where
the hand actually is -- then added that offset onto
`hand.wrapper.position` afterward. Never actually measured "cursor
relative to the hand" as one consistent operation.

Rewrote `updateTiltTarget()`'s mouse branch exactly per spec:
- Point A (new module var `tiltOriginGround`): `rForearmBend`'s own
  LIVE world position (already held stable frame-to-frame by the
  existing pivot/anchor math in `animate()`), projected onto the world
  Y=0 ground plane by sliding it along the CAMERA's own forward
  direction (`camera.getWorldDirection()`) -- "project that
  perpendicular to the camera," i.e. along the camera's own optical
  axis. Degenerate fallback (camera looking perfectly horizontally,
  forward.y ~0) drops straight down instead of leaving a stale value.
- Point B (`tiltTarget`, reused): the cursor's own raycast through the
  camera, now hitting that SAME Y=0 ground plane (previously a
  vertical Z=0 plane, `cursorTargetPlane` -- removed entirely, fully
  unused after this change; the device-orientation branch never used
  it either).
- `baseDeg` (mouse path only) now reads X/Z off these 2 ground points
  via `computeRadialRollDeg()`'s existing `{x,y}` shape (world Z
  aliased into the `.y` slot). Device-orientation is untouched -- no
  camera ray to ground-project in the first place.

Live-verified: `rForearmBend`'s real world position unchanged (~7e-14
noise) across the rotation -- the anchor still holds. Cursor at screen
center (203,457) -> ~-0.44 deg (correctly near-neutral); a lower-right
position (350,700, near `tiltMagnitude`'s 1.0 clamp) -> a large
rotation -- confirms the angle now genuinely responds to the cursor's
position relative to the hand, not an arbitrary screen-center
reference.

Direct report: "When my cursor moves more to the left, the hand

Smoothness: investigated before guessing (a synthetic atan2-wrap sweep
fed through `setFromAxisAngle`+`Quaternion.slerp`, measured via

compass angle, independent of `cfg.trackingDamping`'s own quaternion-

jump instantly); sub-frame empirical capture was attempted but proved

2026-09-27  -  (12th, same day)

Direct report: "The whole hand rotation at base is suffering the same
rotatin axis messing with Fiigner poses as before. fix that."

Root cause: `applyBaseArmRotation()` (`baseRotationX/Y/Z` sliders)
mutates `h.clone.quaternion` directly via its own delta-tracking,
bypassing `h.currentBaseQuat` -- the `baseQuat` every
`applyCurlToSkeleton()` call uses as its finger-curl axis reference.
Same bug class already fixed 3 times in this file (Whole-Hand Rotation
`modelRotX/Y/Z`, wrist bend/splay/rotation). Fixed by applying the
exact same `deltaQuat` to `h.currentBaseQuat`, and re-baking finger
curl/splay immediately after (matching
`applyReactiveWristSplayFrame()`'s own pattern) so the actual bone
quaternions don't stay stale.

Disclosed: this does not unify Base Rotation's own pivot
(`rForearmBend`'s live position) with Whole-Hand Rotation's own pivot
(`modelRotationPivot`/palm center) into one formula -- a later
`applyModelRootTransform()` call can still shift where Base Rotation's
pivot ends up. Not the reported bug (curl axis, now fixed); flagged as
a known follow-up.

Verified using this project's own established methodology for this bug
class (bone orientation RELATIVE to `h.clone.quaternion`, before/after,
expecting ~0deg drift): with real curl+splay on 2 fingers and a
combined 45/60/-30 deg base rotation change, drift was 0.000-0.016deg
(floating-point noise) across all 4 tested joints.

2026-09-27  -  (13th, same day)

Direct report: "h and double check the saed cameras. it doesnt seem to
save still" (Saved Cameras not persisting).

Root cause, confirmed via direct localStorage inspection after a real
Save click (monkey-patched window.prompt/confirm to drive the flow):
`buildListPicker()`'s Save/Overwrite/Rename/Delete/+Group/Import
handlers all mutate their own `items` array in memory, then call
`saveDevPanelSettings()` hoping it persists -- but that (devPanel.js-
owned) function only captures state from REGISTERED dev-panel controls
(hidden inputs, sliders, etc.). A raw JS array like SAVED_CAMERAS has
no such registration, so it was completely invisible to Sync --
`devPanelSettings` in localStorage had no field for it at all, only
the standard controls/layout/style keys. This silently affected all 5
list-pickers built by this shared function (Saved Tween Sequences,
Poses, Cameras, Lighting, Toon Shading), not just Cameras -- every
mutation appeared to work (the in-memory array and its DOM list both
updated) but reset to the hardcoded seed data on every reload.

Fixed with a dedicated persistence layer, keyed by a new required
`opts.storageKey` per list-picker: `persistListPickerItems()`
(localStorage, always-on baseline per CLAUDE.md Section 12l, plus a
best-effort async GET-merge-POST to the same git-tracked settings
endpoint the existing `saveFieldAsDefault()`/`loadFieldDefaultIfSaved()`
pattern already uses) is called after every mutation handler, in
addition to the existing `saveDevPanelSettings()` call.
`loadListPickerItemsFromLocalStorage()` runs synchronously at module
load (right after the 5 SAVED_* arrays are declared), before anything
else in the module reads them, so even Tween's own direct read of
SAVED_POSES sees restored data. Remote (git-synced) data is merged in
via `loadListPickerItemsFromRemoteData()`, called from the existing
`loadRemoteSettingsOnStartup()` using its one shared GET response
(rather than 5 separate per-list fetches) -- merged in BEFORE
`ensureDevPanelBuilt()` runs, so a fresh production visit (no `?dev=1`,
panel not yet built) renders each picker already showing the remote
data; a dev-mode session whose panel was already eagerly built before
this fetch resolves still gets the arrays updated correctly, only that
one picker's already-rendered list doesn't visually refresh until next
reload -- a minor, disclosed gap, since every later Save/Overwrite
still reads the live (now-correct) array.

Verified live end-to-end: cleared localStorage's list-picker key,
clicked Save on Saved Cameras (name "TestCameraSaveXYZ") with prompt/
confirm monkey-patched, confirmed the new entry appeared in
`localStorage.handyset_listPicker_cameras`; reloaded the page for
real (a genuine navigation, not a soft re-render) and confirmed the
saved camera was still present in both localStorage AND the rendered
list-picker UI -- the actual reported symptom, now fixed. Then
exercised Delete on that same entry and confirmed localStorage
correctly dropped back to the 7-camera seed set, proving the fix
covers removal as well as addition. (Hit this sandbox's own documented
"local static server intermittently fails to fully deliver a large
script" gotcha twice during this verification -- `main.js` came back
`net::ERR_CONNECTION_RESET` on 2 of 4 navigations; not a code issue,
resolved by retrying the navigation.)

`index.html`'s own `main.js` cache-buster bumped `?v=85` -> `?v=86`.

Commit: `333201a`. Pushed to `https://github.com/LeisHo/Handy-Set`;
Vercel auto-deployed.

2026-09-27  -  (14th, same day)

Direct interjected request, mid-turn: "when i turn on show target
marker, also show a marker for where the forearm base point is which
we are using to measure the degree of rotation. Height should not
matter to rsponsie pam rtation since its purely an XZ plane angle
measurement. the distance of the cursor to the hand should also have
no effect o nte responsive palm rotaiton."

3 changes, all in `updateTiltTarget()`/`animate()`'s own Palm Rotation
block:

1. New `forearmBaseMarkerMesh` (cyan sphere), synced to
   `tiltOriginGround` every frame, sharing `checkboxShowTargetMarker`'s
   own visibility with the existing (magenta) target marker rather than
   getting its own separate checkbox -- the request asked for it as an
   addition to that existing control.
2. `tiltOriginGround` (Point A of the angle measurement) no longer
   slides the forearm bone's world position along the camera's forward
   direction until it reaches Y=0 -- it now just drops the Y coordinate
   outright (`set(x, 0, z)`). The camera-ray slide made the resulting
   XZ landing point depend on BOTH the forearm's own height and the
   camera's pitch/yaw (a taller/shorter forearm, or a repositioned
   camera, would slide the point sideways before the angle was ever
   computed) -- exactly the height-coupling this request rules out. No
   camera-ray math is needed at all once Y is simply discarded; removed
   the now-unused `_cameraForwardVec` alongside it.
3. Removed the `* tiltMagnitude` scaling on Palm Rotation's `baseDeg`
   (added in the 5th round, both the mouse and device-orientation
   paths) -- `computeRadialRollDeg()` is a pure `atan2`, direction
   only; the rotation now applies that angle directly with zero
   distance/magnitude component, matching "distance of the cursor to
   the hand should have no effect." Reactive Arm Length and Responsive
   Wrist Splay are untouched -- they still read `tiltMagnitude` for
   their own curves; this change is scoped to Palm Rotation's `baseDeg`
   only.

Verified live via direct state inspection (not just visual): with a
real mouse move driving the rotation, `impliedYdeg` (extracted from the
resulting `wrapper.quaternion` via `Euler.setFromQuaternion`) matched
an independently-computed `atan2(tiltOriginGround, tiltTarget)` to 13
decimal places (168.2148200002795 vs 168.21482000027956) -- confirming
no residual scaling factor survived the removal. Repositioned the
camera to look down toward Y=0 to confirm both markers render at their
correct, distinct world positions (cyan near the forearm, magenta at
the cursor's ground hit) -- screenshot-verified. An initial attempt at
this same distance-independence test (comparing a "tiny" 8px cursor
offset against a "far" 350px one) produced identical results and
briefly looked like a bug; traced to the tiny-offset test point simply
falling in the already-documented Y=0 raycast dead-zone (top ~2/3 of
screen misses the plane), which correctly freezes state rather than
computing a bogus angle -- not a flaw in this fix, a flawed test
setup, corrected by re-deriving the check from the exact-match
`atan2` comparison instead.

`index.html`'s own `main.js` cache-buster bumped `?v=86` -> `?v=87`.

Commit: `e0677cd`. Pushed to `https://github.com/LeisHo/Handy-Set`;
Vercel auto-deployed.

2026-09-27  -  (15th, same day)

Implements items 4-6 of the earlier 6-part request (items 1-3 were the
Palm Rotation/Whole-Hand-Rotation-at-Base fixes, entries 11th-12th):
"withi nthe phone tilt group, add a RESPONSIE ARM ROTATION AT BASE...
Provide the same 2 new inputs for the Responsive wrist splay... add a
'Responsive Pose Tween' subgroup."

New shared distance metric, `armBaseDistanceT`: per the request ("make
the right side of the X axis (max distance) be set as the farthest
distance from the arm base point to the edge of browser... if parallel
to the floor is 0, then 90 degrees in any tilt is 100"), this is
DELIBERATELY different from the existing `tiltMagnitude` (screen-
distance-from-CENTER). On the mouse path: the real world-space XZ
distance from `tiltOriginGround` (the forearm base's own ground point)
to `tiltTarget` (the cursor's own ground hit), normalized 0-1 against
the farthest such distance reachable from any of the 4 screen corners
(`computeMaxArmBaseGroundDistance()`, a new function -- 4 corner
raycasts onto the same ground plane, only refreshed on a genuine hit
so a dead-zone freeze holds this steady too). On the device-
orientation path: identical to `tiltMagnitude` -- there's no cursor/
ground geometry on mobile, and "the further the phone tilt" already IS
what `tiltMagnitude` measures there.

Item 4 -- new "Responsive Arm Rotation at Base" subgroup (Phone Tilt
group): On/Off checkbox, fine-tune slider, min/max range, curve graph
-- matching the request's own control list exactly (no separate
"reactive" sub-toggle the way Reactive Arm Length/Responsive Wrist
Splay have, since the request's list is just those 4 controls).
`applyBaseArmRotation()` (the already curl-axis-safe function from the
12th entry's fix) gained an `extraX` parameter -- the reactive amount
is added onto `cfg.baseRotationX` every frame via a new
`applyResponsiveBaseArmRotationFrame()`, reusing the SAME delta-
tracking rather than a 2nd rotation system, so it inherits that fix's
correctness for free.

Item 5 -- Responsive Wrist Splay's existing curve/range now read
`armBaseDistanceT` instead of `tiltMagnitude` (2 call sites:
`applyPoseValuesToHand`, `applyReactiveWristSplayFrame`), plus updated
UI captions/labels to describe the new metric. Reactive Arm Length is
untouched, still reads `tiltMagnitude`.

Item 6 -- new "Responsive Pose Tween" subgroup: On/Off, a Target Pose
`<select>` over `SAVED_POSES` (new `wireSelect()` helper), and a curve
graph. "Default pose" is read as `DEFAULT_POSE_NAME` (a named pose,
parallel to Target Pose also being named) rather than a live snapshot
of whatever's posed when the feature is turned on -- a disclosed
interpretation choice, documented at `findSavedPoseByName()`'s own
comment. `applyResponsivePoseTweenFrame()` blends EVERY numeric pose
field between the 2 named poses by the curve's own Y output (0=Default,
1=Target), then hands the blended object to the EXISTING
`applyPoseValuesToHand()` pipeline -- deliberately never writing new
bone-rotation code, so it inherits that pipeline's already-correct
baseQuat/curl-exclude-quat pairing instead of risking a 5th instance of
this file's own recurring curl-axis bug class. Mutually exclusive with
Responsive Wrist Splay's own per-frame reapplication in the same frame
(animate()'s own `if (cfg.poseTweenResponsiveEnabled) {...} else
{applyReactiveWristSplayFrame()}`) -- Pose Tween already re-bakes the
whole pose including wristSplay every frame; running Wrist Splay's own
reapplication in the same frame would immediately overwrite that
blended wrist value with `cfg.wristSplay` (the Pose group's own
slider, untouched by the tween), undoing part of what the tween just
did.

Verified live via direct state inspection (finger-bone quaternion
reads, not just screenshots -- a screenshot-only check would have been
insufficient, see the debugging note below):
- Base Arm Rotation: with `baseRotationX=0`, toggling On applied a
  measured +30deg delta (matching the default curve's near-arm-base
  value); toggling Off reverted `h.clone.quaternion` to the exact
  pre-reactive baseline to floating-point noise (~1e-19).
- Pose Tween: with Target Pose = "Big Open Palm" (curlIndex 0) against
  Default = "Fist" (curlIndex 90), moving the cursor between 2 screen
  positions produced 2 clearly DIFFERENT `rIndex1` bone quaternions
  (`[0.104,0.070,-0.657,0.743]` near the arm base vs.
  `[-0.120,-0.007,-0.034,0.992]` farther away) and 2 visibly different
  screenshots (a closed fist vs. a splayed-open palm); disabling
  reverted the bone exactly back to the live Fist-slider value.

Debugging note, worth flagging for future work on this feature: an
EARLIER round of this same verification (Base Arm Rotation's On/Off
toggle) produced a confusing false negative -- comparing quaternion
values captured across SEPARATE page reloads (rather than within one
continuous session) meant each reload's own actual starting baseline
differed (this project's dev panel persists control state), so 2
numbers that looked like "it didn't reset" were actually 2 DIFFERENT
sessions' own correct-but-different baselines being compared against
each other. Resolved by re-testing within a single uninterrupted
session (localStorage cleared once, then on/off toggled back-to-back
without an intervening reload) -- confirmed the delta math was correct
the whole time. If a future test on this project's own reactive/delta-
tracking features seems to show a "doesn't reset" result, verify the
before/after reads came from the SAME session before concluding it's a
real bug.

`index.html`'s own `main.js` cache-buster bumped `?v=87` -> `?v=92`
(v88-v91 were intermediate debug-logging builds during the false-
negative investigation above, never shipped as final).

2026-09-27  -  (16th, same day)

Direct report: "when i set responsive pose tween, the hand dssappears.
im rpetty sure its some bug clash with the rotation Whole hadn rotation
at base slider."

Root cause, confirmed live (not just from reading the code): the
user's own hypothesis was exactly right. `applyModelRootTransform()`
(called every frame by Responsive Pose Tween, via
`applyPoseValuesToHand()`) unconditionally overwrites
`h.clone.quaternion`/`position` from `h.currentBaseQuat` -- but never
told `applyBaseArmRotation()` (the Base Rotation X/Y/Z sliders' own
function) that this happened. That function's own delta-tracker
(`h.lastBaseArmQuat` -- "what I last applied") went stale the instant
`applyModelRootTransform()` ran again, computing its next delta
relative to a rotation that no longer actually existed in
`h.clone.quaternion`. With Responsive Arm Rotation at Base ALSO
reactive (running every single frame right after the Pose Tween
branch in `animate()`), the 2 systems fought over the exact same
transform every frame using mutually inconsistent assumptions about
what was already applied.

Reproduced deliberately before attempting a fix: set Base Rotation X
to 45deg via its plain slider, enabled Responsive Pose Tween (target
"Big Open Palm"), then also enabled Responsive Arm Rotation at Base --
screenshot showed the hand collapsed to a tiny sliver near the dev
panel, matching the report exactly.

Diagnosed WHY no single number looked wrong: `h.clone.quaternion`/
`position`/`scale`, individual finger-bone quaternions, camera
position/target, and frustum-culling state were all individually
sane (no NaN, no zero-scale, reasonable magnitudes) when checked one
at a time -- the actual smoking gun only showed up projecting the
hand's own live bounding-box corners through the camera into screen
space: they scattered to values like x=-3789 to x=2801 against a
405px-wide viewport, meaning the camera had effectively ended up
inside/beside the misplaced geometry. A pure "is this one number
reasonable" check missed it; a screen-space projection check caught
it immediately.

Fixed by resetting `h.lastBaseArmQuat = null` inside
`applyModelRootTransform()` itself -- the NEXT `applyBaseArmRotation()`
call (same frame, since `animate()` calls it right after the Pose
Tween/Wrist Splay branch) now treats the just-reset transform as its
new baseline and applies its own rotation as ONE clean delta from
identity, rather than a stale, no-longer-meaningful one. This also
fixes the SAME latent clash for the non-reactive case (a plain Pose
slider touch, or a saved-pose "Use", after Base Rotation X/Y/Z had
been set) -- that combination silently discarded the base rotation
too, just without the frame-repeated compounding that made the
reactive case visually catastrophic. This is the file's own
established "whole-hand-level rotation must invalidate/bake into
whatever downstream system tracks it" lesson (previously hit 4 times
on the baseQuat/curl-axis side) showing up a 5th time, on the
position/quaternion delta-tracking side instead.

Live-verified: re-ran the exact reproduction steps after the fix --
hand renders normally (not a sliver, not scattered) with Pose Tween
and reactive Base Arm Rotation both active simultaneously; confirmed
the plain Base Rotation X slider (no Pose Tween involved) still
visibly rotates the hand correctly on its own, no regression.

`index.html`'s own `main.js` cache-buster bumped `?v=92` -> `?v=93`.

Commit: `8b048f5`. Pushed to `https://github.com/LeisHo/Handy-Set`;
Vercel auto-deployed.

2026-09-27  -  (17th, same day)

Direct follow-up, same day as the 16th entry above: "you didnt fix the
pose tween thign fix that... for example, turn it on, then set the
target pose to Big Open Palm or anyting else... messingw it hteh Curve
graph does noting, so even the defualt position hand gets shifted...
so i think aybe you are applying the target pose' rotation to
everything."

The 16th entry's fix (invalidating `h.lastBaseArmQuat` on every
`applyModelRootTransform()` call) was real and necessary, but not
sufficient -- it fixed the specific Base-Arm-Rotation clash, not the
underlying reason Pose Tween itself looked broken.

Root cause: `armBaseDistanceT` (added in the 15th entry, shared by
Wrist Splay/Base Arm Rotation/Pose Tween) only ever updated inside
`updateTiltTarget()`'s `if (haveHit) {...}` branch -- on a raycast
MISS it was simply left untouched, frozen at whatever it last held.
The ground-plane raycast misses for roughly the top 2/3 of the screen
(this project's own already-documented camera-framing dead zone,
originally scoped to Palm Rotation) -- and the dev panel, where a user
actually drags curve control points, sits almost entirely in that
dead zone. Confirmed live: 2 direct bone-quaternion reads, minutes
apart with real curve/mouse interaction in between, came back
bit-identical -- proof the value never moved.

This explains all 3 symptoms as ONE root cause: "curve does nothing"
(frozen X meant the curve's own shape stopped mattering, especially
right at its clamped endpoint x=0 or x=1); "even default gets shifted"
(the frozen value on enable was whatever UNRELATED leftover the
raycast last produced, not actually 0/Default); "applying the target's
rotation to everything" (if that leftover happened to be near 1, the
hand would look permanently stuck near the target pose regardless of
what the user did next).

Palm Rotation's OWN freeze-on-miss (`tiltTarget`/`tiltTargetValid`) is
a reasonable, deliberately-requested choice for that feature (a single
rotation staying wherever it was). It's the wrong choice for a metric
driving a FULL-POSE swap. Fixed by falling back to `tiltMagnitude`
(screen-distance-from-center -- always live, no raycast dependency,
already used by Reactive Arm Length) whenever the ground-plane raycast
misses, so `armBaseDistanceT` never truly freezes again.
`tiltTarget`/`tiltTargetValid` themselves are untouched -- Palm
Rotation's own behavior is unchanged.

Live-verified: with the mouse held directly over the dev panel (a
confirmed dead-zone position), the pose now tracks `tiltMagnitude`
smoothly -- matches Target near the panel edge, matches ~Default at
screen center, and a live curve-shape edit at a FIXED mouse position
immediately changed the applied pose (previously zero effect).
Re-ran the 16th entry's own Base-Arm-Rotation-clash repro afterward
with this fix also in place -- still resolved, no regression.

`index.html`'s own `main.js` cache-buster bumped `?v=93` -> `?v=94`.

Commit: `d3b294a`. Pushed to `https://github.com/LeisHo/Handy-Set`;
Vercel auto-deployed.

2026-09-27  -  (18th, same day)

Direct request, same day as the 16th/17th entries: "for the settings i
set in Whole hand rotation at base, and rotation, apply those to

Gap: Pose Tween's per-frame blend read `modelRotX/Y/Z` straight from
the 2 named poses' own saved fields -- every seeded SAVED_POSES entry
has these at 0, so the blend silently discarded whatever the user had
live-tuned on the "Whole-Hand Rotation X/Y/Z" sliders, every single
frame, for as long as the feature stayed on. "Base Rotation X/Y/Z"
(applied via a separate function/pivot, `applyBaseArmRotation()`/
`rForearmBend`) had a related but distinct gap: the 16th entry's fix
stopped the 2 systems from fighting/scattering, but didn't make the
PLAIN (non-reactive) slider's own effect stay applied each frame --
nothing was re-triggering `applyBaseArmRotation()` after Pose Tween's
own per-frame transform reset invalidated its delta-tracker, unless
Base Rotation was ALSO set to reactive.

Fixed in `applyResponsivePoseTweenFrame()`: after building the
blended pose-values object, override its `modelRotX/Y/Z` with the
live `cfg` values before handing it to `applyPoseValuesToHand()`; and
re-apply Base Rotation's current slider value every frame via a plain

(the reactive case is already covered by `animate()`'s own
unconditional `applyResponsiveBaseArmRotationFrame()` call right
after -- calling both would just be a redundant, harmless extra
delta-tracker round-trip, so it's skipped).

distinctive nonzero values (45deg, 20deg, 30deg across 2 rounds),
enabled Pose Tween with a target pose, confirmed `h.clone.quaternion`

16th entry's own disappearing-hand repro with Responsive Arm Rotation

2026-09-27  -  (19th, same day)

Direct follow-up, same day as the 18th entry: "and the responsive pose
tween doesnt dissappera anhymore. but the pose isnt chanigng. the
wrist data does. but fingers odnt."

Root cause: a direct side effect of the 18th entry's own fix, which
re-applies Base Rotation's slider value every frame from inside
`applyResponsivePoseTweenFrame()` so it stays live throughout the
tween. `applyBaseArmRotation()`'s own finger re-bake (at the end of
that function, pre-existing since the 12th entry) was hardcoded to
read from `cfg` -- correct for its normal call sites (a plain slider
touch, or the reactive per-frame call), but wrong when called
immediately after Pose Tween's own blend: it re-baked every finger
curl/splay straight from `cfg`'s own live (un-tweened) slider values,
silently overwriting the finger blend `applyPoseValuesToHand(blended)`
had JUST correctly applied one line above. Wrist bone rotation lives
in a completely different function (`applyWristPoseToSkeleton`,
called only from inside `applyPoseValuesToHand`), untouched by this --
exactly why wrist responded to the tween while fingers stayed frozen.

Fixed by giving `applyBaseArmRotation()` an explicit `poseValues`
parameter (default `cfg`, unchanged for every other call site) and
threading `blended` through from `applyResponsivePoseTweenFrame()` for
both the plain and reactive Base Rotation cases (also gave
`applyResponsiveBaseArmRotationFrame()` the same parameter).
`animate()` no longer calls `applyResponsiveBaseArmRotationFrame()` a
2nd time when Pose Tween is active, since that call would only ever
have used `cfg` too -- Pose Tween's own branch now handles Base
Rotation's reapplication itself, correctly, either way.

Live-verified: reproduced the exact symptom first (wrist bone
quaternion changed between 2 cursor positions -- center vs. a far
corner -- while the finger bone stayed bit-for-bit identical), then
confirmed the fix makes the finger bone correctly track the blend too
(matching the Big Open Palm target's own value at the far position).
Re-confirmed rotation sliders + reactive Base Arm Rotation still work
correctly combined with this fix (no regression on the 16th/18th
entries' own fixes).

2026-09-27  -  (20th, same day)

Direct report: "sae button isnt saving my phone tilt settings."

Root cause, confirmed via the real git-tracked `dev-panel-settings.json`
(not just code reading): `addRow()` explicitly excluded every
`'text'`/`'number'` type control from `HANDYSET_CONTROLS` -- meaning
EVERY curve/range JSON field in the entire project (Reactive Arm
Length, Responsive Wrist Splay, the 3 wrist-axis clamps, Responsive
Arm Rotation at Base, Responsive Pose Tween) was never registered
with devPanel.js's own generic Save/Sync mechanism
(`captureAllRegisteredControlValues()`/`applyControlValues()`, JS-13)
-- both fully type-agnostic (`document.getElementById` +
`.value`/`.checked`, neither cares about `ctrl.type` at all). Checked
the real settings file directly: after 7 genuine Save/Sync round-trips
from live usage, `textWristSplayRange`, `textArmLengthRange`,
`textBaseArmRotationCurve`, `textPoseTweenCurve`, etc. were ALL absent
from the saved `controls` blob -- only the simpler checkbox/slider/
select fields had ever actually been saved. **This was a pre-existing,
systemic gap, not something introduced this session** -- it affected
every curve/range control in the project from the start, not just the
new Phone Tilt ones that happened to surface it first.

Fixed by removing the exclusion in `addRow()` -- every control now
registers regardless of type. The exclusion only ever needed to route
`'text'`/`'number'` rows to `buildTextInputRow()` instead of
`buildUniformControlRow()` (the actual DOM/row-builder choice on the
line above, untouched) -- it never needed to also skip registration,
since `registerDevControlArray()` and its 2 consumers make no
assumption about which builder produced the element, and
`buildTextInputRow()`'s own output (a plain `<input id=ctrl.id>`)
already satisfies their only real requirement.

Live-verified: changed a curve/range text field (Base Arm Rotation's
own min/max range), clicked Sync, confirmed the new value appeared in
`localStorage.devPanelSettings` alongside the other fields; reloaded
for real (not a soft re-render) and confirmed both the DOM control and
`cfg` correctly restored the saved value. Console clean beyond the
project's own already-documented pre-existing gotchas (the local
static server's flaky large-file delivery, the `/api/save-settings`
404s with no local backend).

`index.html`'s own `main.js` cache-buster bumped `?v=96` -> `?v=97`.

2026-09-27  -  (21st, same day)

Direct report: "the camera sliders feel a bit weird. 1. why is it
showing like 10 decimal points? 2. When i drag the sliders, it is
jittery. 3. allow me to click and type to overwrite the number values
fo those sliders."

Root cause 1/2 (decimals + jitter): `syncCameraPanelFromLive()` runs
every single frame (called from `animate()`, right after
`controls.update()`), feeding the camera's own raw floating-point
position straight into `syncValue()` -- which displayed the FULL
unrounded float (e.g. `"3.598517809628556"`) and unconditionally
overwrote `slider.value` even while a slider was being actively
dragged by the user, fighting their own input every frame. Fixed by
(a) rounding only the DISPLAYED text to 2 decimals in `syncValue()`
(the underlying slider `.value`/`cfg` stays full precision, unaffected
-- purely cosmetic) and (b) skipping the resync entirely for whichever
slider currently has focus (`document.activeElement === el`), so the
live-camera resync never fights an in-progress drag -- it resumes the
instant focus leaves the slider.

Root cause 3 (click-to-type): devPanel.js's own click-to-edit
infrastructure (workspace CLAUDE.md Section 12h) was already partially
present in HANDYSET's copy, but had fallen behind 2 real upstream
fixes already landed in the canonical `.claude/TEMPLATE_DEV_PANEL.html`
(both dated 2026-09-24 there, confirmed by direct comparison against
the real template file rather than assumed): `buildSliderRow()` wasn't
adding the `dev-value-editable` class at ROW-CREATION time (only via a
one-time init-time `makeDevValuesEditable()` pass, which never reached
any row built dynamically afterward) or wiring a live text update from
the slider's own `'input'` event (leaving the readout stale during a
drag whenever a project's own onInput handler didn't separately
refresh it); and the click-to-edit popup's own `<input>` never
inherited the source slider's `step`/`min`/`max` at all, so a
decimal-step slider's popup (e.g. Camera X/Y/Z, step=0.5) silently
defaulted to whole-number-only typing. Re-synced both fixes verbatim
from the canonical template into HANDYSET's own copy.

Live-verified: Camera X/Y/Z/Yaw/Pitch/FOV/Zoom now display at 2
decimals; holding a slider via `focus()` and pumping a real
`animate()` frame (a screenshot) confirmed the dragged value (`100`)
survived instead of snapping back to the live camera position, and
correctly resumed live-syncing (`100.00`) the instant `blur()` was
called; clicking a value opened a working edit box with `step=0.5`/
`min=-500`/`max=500` correctly inherited, and typing `42.5` correctly
updated both `cfg.cameraX` and the live `camera.position.x`. Also
confirmed no regression on a normal (non-camera) slider's own display
update (a Pose group finger-curl slider).

`index.html`'s own `main.js` cache-buster bumped `?v=97` -> `?v=98`;
`devPanel.js` cache-buster bumped `?v=4` -> `?v=5` (its first bump this
project, since this is the first time this file itself has changed).

2026-09-27  -  (22nd, same day)

Direct report: "I'm regards to phone tilt functions. When I check the
show in mobile checkbox, the min max sliders and curve graphs aren't
showing up in mobile."

Root cause: the 3 curve/range controls in the Phone Tilt group
(`textBaseArmRotationRange`, `textBaseArmRotationCurve`,
`textPoseTweenCurve`) are built on top of a hidden `type: 'text'`
control. devPanel.js's own generic per-device mirroring
(`ensureDynamicTargetRow()`) explicitly excludes `'text'`/`'number'`
controls -- confirmed via direct comparison against the real, canonical
`.claude/TEMPLATE_DEV_PANEL.html` that this is a genuine, permanent,
documented scope limit of the shared engine itself ("outside this
system's scope for now"), not something HANDYSET's own copy had fallen
behind on this time. The "Show in Mobile/Landscape" checkbox itself
still existed and toggled correctly (its own state doesn't depend on
the registry), which is exactly what made checking it silently do
nothing rather than error.

Asked the user via AskUserQuestion whether the Mobile/Landscape mirror
should be a fully independent per-device copy or a shared value; user
chose Shared (matches how these 3 settings already work today -- none
of them are split per-device anywhere else in the file). Implemented a
HANDYSET-owned mirroring mechanism (`PHONE_TILT_MIRROR_WIDGETS` +
`syncPhoneTiltWidgetMirrors()`, main.js), polled every frame via the
same pre-existing `curveWidgetResyncs` array the curve/range widgets
already use to detect a devPanel.js Reset/Restore writing straight to
`input.value` -- one mechanism handles a checkbox click, a Sync/Reset
restore, and a tab switch uniformly. Every frame it also force-syncs
the Desktop input and both mirrors' values to the canonical `cfg`
field; each widget's own internal resync then redraws, so editing ANY
of the 3 (Desktop, Mobile, Landscape) correctly propagates to the
other 2.

Bug found DURING live verification, fixed in the same pass: the
mirror-creation code initially used devPanel.js's own
`window.findGroupContent(tab, groupSid, ...)` to locate each mirrored
row's target group -- but that function's own selector uses a
direct-child combinator (`#mobileTabContent > .dev-section >
.dev-section-title[data-sid="..."]`), so it can only find a TOP-LEVEL
group, never a nested subgroup. Both target groups here ("Responsive
Arm Rotation at Base", "Responsive Pose Tween") are nested one level
inside the top-level "Phone Tilt" group -- confirmed live via repeated
`console.warn` output from `findGroupContent` itself ("group not
found"), while a plain any-depth `querySelector` for the same
`data-sid` found the element without issue. Fixed by adding a small
HANDYSET-owned `findNestedGroupContent(tabId, groupSid)` helper
(main.js) -- identical to devPanel.js's own function except it drops
the `>` restriction -- and using it in place of `window.findGroupContent`
at the one call site.

Live-verified after the fix (localStorage cleared, clean load,
real `switchDevPanelTab()` calls + real frame pumps via screenshot, not
just code reading): all 3 controls' Mobile AND Landscape mirror rows
now render correctly (a working drag-range slider showing "Min: 0°

Direct request: load 8 smartphone GLBs (data/processed/SMARTPHONE

Tilt in that order.

Resolved via AskUserQuestion before building, per workspace CLAUDE.md
§0a: the spec's own "RESPONSIVE BEHAVIOUR - PHONE" (for the phone
model) collided by name with renaming the hand's existing "Phone Tilt"
group to the same string. Direct answer: the hand's group is
"RESPONSIVE BEHAVIOUR - HAND"; "RESPONSIVE BEHAVIOUR - PHONE" is
actually Level 2, nested inside PHONE MODEL (not top-level as the
original shorthand implied).

Phone Model mechanics: `loadPhoneModel()` (GLTFLoader, `encodeURI()`
for the filenames' literal spaces) loads into `phoneModelWrapper`
(position = Offset sliders, "world origin" when Offset is 0,0,0) ->
`phoneModelRaw` (scale + rotation, pivot-compensated). "Anchor of
rotation will be the centroid of the 3d geometry" -- measured once via
`Box3().setFromObject()` right after load (before any scale/rotation),
then kept fixed every frame via the EXACT `position = pivot -
rotation*(scale*pivot)` formula `applyModelRootTransform()` already
uses for the hand's own `modelRotationPivot` -- reused, not
re-derived.

Responsive Rotation: same 4-control pattern as Responsive Arm Rotation
at Base (On/Off, Fine-Tune, Min/Max Range, Curve) -- direct request --
but ONE shared curve/range now drives 3 INDEPENDENT axes
(`computePhoneResponsiveAxisDeg(rawComponent)`, |component| through the
curve for magnitude, `Math.sign(component)` for direction). "On
Desktop, it will rotate around its local y and z axes only, but on
mobile it will rotate on all axes" falls out of WHICH raw signals
exist on which platform, not an explicit device branch: Y/Z reuse
`tiltMagnitude`/`tiltAngle` (already computed identically for mouse
and device orientation) reconstructed into nx/ny components; the 3rd
(X) axis is `phoneTiltAxisXRaw`, sourced from the device's own compass
heading (`e.alpha`, baselined on first reading then read as a centered
delta) -- a signal that only ever exists for a real device, so it
naturally stays 0 on desktop (mouse has no compass equivalent).

Object Axes: ported fat Line2/LineMaterial axes visualization
(`createFatAxesVisualization()` et al.) plus a minimal
`registerSceneObject()`/`sceneObjectEntries` scene-object registry
(scoped down from 3JS ENGINE's own version, which also backs a full
property Inspector this project doesn't have) -- hands (one entry per
`rebuildField()`-built hand) and the Phone Model (registered on load,
unregistered on remove) both populate it. World Axes wasn't ported
(not requested).

4 direct follow-ups on the 23rd entry's Phone Model feature: add a new

entirely, and an empty phantom "ROTATION" group had appeared inside

Root cause of both symptoms at once: the 23rd entry's own PHONE MODEL
group named its rotation subgroup literally "ROTATION" -- an EXACT
string collision with Pose's own pre-existing "ROTATION" subgroup
(`slidermodelRotX/Y/Z`, `renderPoseGroup()`). `data-sid` identity is
flat per device tab, not scoped by parent, so 2 subgroups sharing one
name anywhere in the same tab is a real structural bug, not a cosmetic
one -- confirmed directly in the real git-tracked
`dev-panel-settings.json`: Pose's own `items` array had literally TWO
`"key": "desktop:ROTATION"` entries, one empty (the phantom) and one
holding SIX rows -- `sliderPhoneModelRotX/Y/Z` merged together with
`slidermodelRotX/Y/Z` -- while `desktop:PHONE MODEL` had no ROTATION
entry of its own at all. Fixed in code by renaming Phone Model's own
subgroup to "PHONE ROTATION" (unique), and in the git-tracked settings
file via a 2nd surgery script (Python, same discipline as the 23rd
entry's own HAND MODEL reorg): split the merged 6-row phantom entry
back into its 2 real halves by row-id set membership, deleted the
empty duplicate, rebuilt a clean 3-row "ROTATION" under Pose, and
created a new "PHONE ROTATION" entry under PHONE MODEL holding the 3
phone rows. Verified via a full row-level key-diff (zero lost, zero
added) plus an explicit duplicate-key scan (zero duplicates across all
3 device tabs) both before and after. Re-applied a 2nd time after a
newer remote Sync commit landed mid-task (same "re-run against the
freshest pull" discipline as the 23rd entry).

A 3rd, separate direct report arrived after this fix was already
underway: "responsive phone rotation should be anchored by the phone
models OWN geoemtr origin... its currently rotating around some world
origin." The original implementation pivoted on a COMPUTED
bounding-box centroid (`Box3().setFromObject()`), not the model's own
authored local origin -- a GLB's local (0,0,0) is whatever point the
modeler chose and isn't guaranteed to coincide with its geometric
bounding-box center. Fixed by removing the centroid computation and
pivot-compensation math entirely: `phoneModelRaw.position` now simply
stays `(0,0,0)` always (rotating purely around its own local origin),
with `phoneModelWrapper.position` (the Offset sliders) doing all the
actual world-space placement. Live-verified: a 90° Y rotation left
`phoneModelRaw.position` at exactly `(0,0,0)`, confirming no residual
drift-compensation logic remains.

New HAND MODEL checkbox (`checkboxPhoneModelEnabledHandModel`): a
plain duplicate wired to the same `setPhoneModelEnabled()`, kept in
sync with the PHONE MODEL group's own checkbox via a new
`syncPhoneModelEnabledCheckboxes()` helper (both checkboxes' `.checked`
are updated together on every enable/disable, regardless of which one
was actually clicked). Live-verified in both directions.

Live-verified end-to-end after all 4 fixes, against the real
git-tracked settings (fetched via `fetch()` into `localStorage` in the
absence of a local serverless backend, not just fresh-load defaults):
PHONE MODEL correctly shows OFFSET/PHONE ROTATION/RESPONSIVE
BEHAVIOUR - PHONE with no missing content; Pose's own ROTATION is

`rebuildField()` can throw `Cannot read properties of null (reading
'clone')` inside `SkeletonUtils.clone()` if a Field Layout slider's
restore-triggered `rebuildField()` call fires before the hand's own
async `GLTFLoader.load()` has resolved `modelRoot`; observed once
during this session's own synthetic (~283KB) localStorage injection,
self-recovered (hands.length back to 1 immediately after), not yet
reproduced under normal load conditions -- flag if a future report

2026-09-28  -  (28th)

Direct request, prompted by a question about what the Sensors log's
`Gyro α/β/γ` fields actually are: explained that those are
`DeviceMotionEvent.rotationRate` (angular VELOCITY, deg/s), not the
same numbers as `DeviceOrientationEvent.beta/gamma` (absolute tilt
ANGLE, degrees) that Phone Tilt's own `handleDeviceOrientation()`
actually reads -- the log never captured the latter at all. Added 2
new Sensors-group checkboxes, `Log Beta (Front/Back Tilt Angle)` /
`Log Gamma (Left/Right Tilt Angle)` (`cfg.sensorLogOrientBeta`/
`sensorLogOrientGamma`, both default true), following the exact
existing per-sensor-toggle pattern (`sensorLogAccel`/`Gyro`/`Compass`).
The new log segment is labeled `Orient β:_ γ:_` specifically so it
never reads as a duplicate of `Gyro`'s own α/β/γ despite sharing
greek-letter names.

Separately, direct spec to fix Phone Model Responsive Rotation for
mobile so it matches real device tilt: "tilted forwards ... facing the
user, top of phone pointing up = 90 X, tilting backwards = -90 X.
Tilted left 90 = 90 Z, tilted right = -90 Z. Handle the leftover axis
(Y)." Remapped `computePhoneCombinedQuat()`'s 3 responsive axes:
X <- beta (front-back tilt, NOT inverted -- W3C spec: positive beta
means the device's top tilts toward the user, exactly the "tilted
forwards" example given); Z <- gamma (left-right tilt), INVERTED (W3C
spec: positive gamma = right edge down/tilting right, the OPPOSITE of
this project's left=+90/right=-90 spec); Y <- compass heading delta
(the "leftover axis" -- the only remaining independent orientation
signal a real device provides that a 2D mouse position can't).
Previously X held the compass axis and Y/Z held gamma/beta
respectively; renamed the compass-delta variable
`phoneTiltAxisXRaw` -> `phoneTiltAxisYRaw` throughout to match its new
role, rather than leaving a stale name (this project's own established
gotcha pattern -- see the Hand Model checkbox repurpose history).

NOT verified against a real device this session (no physical phone
available in this sandbox, and `DeviceOrientationEvent.requestPermission()`
here auto-denies with no real user gesture behind it, so not even a
synthetic `deviceorientation` event reaches `handleDeviceOrientation()`
-- a stricter limitation than this file's other already-documented
"no real sensors on Desktop" gotcha, since it blocks simulated events
too). Verified instead via a standalone Node script
(`verify_phone_rotation.js`, scratchpad-only, not committed)
replicating `computePhoneCombinedQuat()`'s exact arithmetic with
synthetic beta/gamma/alpha inputs: confirmed each of the 6 test cases
(rest, forward, backward, left, right, compass-turn) produces rotation
on exactly the intended single axis with the correct sign and zero
cross-talk onto the other 2 axes. The beta/gamma SIGN CONVENTION
itself is per spec, not measured -- if any one axis comes out backwards
on a real phone, flip that axis's own sign in
`computePhoneCombinedQuat()` (negate the `rawComponent` passed to
`computePhoneResponsiveAxisDeg()`) rather than re-deriving the mapping.

Both changes landed in `src/main.js` only; `index.html`'s cache-buster
bumped `?v=110` -> `?v=111` (before the concurrent dev-panel-migration
session's own later `?v=112` bump superseded it). This session and the
one doing the dev-panel-template re-sync (see the 27th entry above)
share the same physical working directory (not separate worktrees), so
these 2 changes rode along inside that session's own `c69cb8a` commit
automatically -- confirmed after the fact via `git show c69cb8a:src/main.js
| grep sensorLogOrientBeta/phoneTiltAxisYRaw`, both present. No separate
code commit was needed; this entry documents work that was already
pushed under someone else's commit message, which is why it wasn't
mentioned there.

2026-09-28  -  (29th)

3 direct follow-up reports on the previous entry's Phone Model rotation
remap: (1) "the rotation motion is jittery and not smooth"; (2) "the
rotation of the phone model doesnt match what i requested. It looks
like its missing an axis of rotation"; (3) request to add a "Phone
Model Log" Debug subgroup. Mid-diagnosis of (2), a 4th message
resolved it directly: "when the phone model rotates as i tilt my
phone, it's object axes (turned on in debug group) does no rotate with
it... The entire model instance should be rotating, not ust the
geoemtr."

Root cause of (2) and the Object Axes report, confirmed by reading
`ensureObjectAxesFor()`: it parents the axes gizmo group directly onto
`entry.object3d` and relies on ordinary THREE.js parent-child
inheritance to follow that object's transform -- for Phone Model,
`entry.object3d` is `phoneModelWrapper` (see `registerSceneObject`),
but `applyPhoneModelTransform()` was writing the rotation quaternion to
`phoneModelRaw` (the CHILD), never touching the wrapper itself. The
gizmo -- parented to the wrapper -- never rotated at all, even though
the actual mesh (a grandchild of the wrapper) rotated correctly on all
3 axes the whole time. This also explains report (2): the user was
almost certainly reading the frozen gizmo as "the model isn't really
rotating." Fixed by moving the quaternion write to
`phoneModelWrapper.quaternion`. No visual regression to the mesh's own
rotation or pivot: `phoneModelRaw.position` stays `(0,0,0)` relative to
the wrapper, so rotating the parent around its own origin produces an
identical world-space result to rotating the child did. Scale
deliberately stays on `phoneModelRaw`, not the wrapper, so the gizmo's
own line length doesn't grow/shrink with Phone Model Scale (same
reasoning as Finger Gizmos not scaling with the hand).

Fixed (1) by adding `cfg.phoneRotationDamping` (default `0.25`, same
1=instant/lower=smoother semantic as the existing `cfg.trackingDamping`)
and slerping `phoneModelWrapper.quaternion` toward the freshly computed
target each frame instead of copying it directly -- real device
beta/gamma/alpha readings carry natural high-frequency sensor noise;
this damps it out at the rotation level. New "Rotation Damping
(1=Instant)" slider added to the Responsive Rotation subgroup.

Built (3): a new "Phone Model Log" subgroup in the Debug group, logging
the phone model's `Pos x/y/z` (`phoneModelWrapper.position`) and
`Rot x/y/z` (`phoneModelWrapper.quaternion` decomposed to Euler XYZ
degrees) once per tick, with Copy/Save/Clear buttons matching the
Sensors log's own pattern exactly. "Same rate as the sensor log" is
satisfied by reading the same `cfg.sensorIntervalMs` and starting/
stopping from the same `Stream Sensor Data` checkbox (`checkboxSensorStream`)
-- implemented as its OWN timer (`restartPhoneModelLogTimer()`) rather
than folding into `restartSensorTimer()`'s existing interval, because
that function's own outer guard bails entirely on non-touch devices
(correct for accel/gyro/compass, which genuinely don't exist on
Desktop) -- but Phone Model position/rotation is an ordinary scene-
object property, updated from mouse input on Desktop too via the same
`computePhoneCombinedQuat()` pipeline, so gating it identically would
have silently disabled the new log in this sandbox (no real device
available here at all).

Also discovered while implementing "include timestamps like the sensor
log": the Sensors log did NOT actually have per-line timestamps despite
the wording implying it already did -- the earlier "Add timestamps to
all debug logs" commit (`f3efd23`) only prefixed `console.error()`/
`console.warn()` calls (browser DevTools console), never the on-panel
Sensors log itself. Added a `ts()` prefix to `pushSensorLog()` too, so
the request's own premise is now actually true going forward.

Not independently browser-verified this round -- per direct
instruction ("no needd ot verify anyting after"), only a syntax check
(`node --check`) was run. The rotation-node fix, damping slerp, and
Euler-decomposition-for-logging are all standard, well-established
patterns already used correctly elsewhere in this file (e.g.
`cfg.trackingDamping`'s own slerp, `computePhoneCombinedQuat()`'s own
Euler->quaternion construction) rather than novel math, which is why
this was judged safe to skip a live pass on -- but it is still
genuinely unverified in a real browser or on a real device.

`index.html`'s cache-buster bumped `?v=112` -> `?v=113`.

2026-09-28  -  (30th)

Direct report after the previous entry's fix: "rotation still no good,"
with a real Phone Model Log + Sensor Log capture pasted (4 held test
movements: Left, Right, Facing Me, Facing Away/bottom-up) and a
pointed question -- "why is this so difficult? isnt it just mapping 1
to 1... i gave you sample data of my sensors and told you what phone
tilt matches what model rotation."

Cross-referenced the 2 logs by timestamp instead of guessing. Found
several held plateaus in the Phone Model Log (each lasting multiple
identical-looking ticks) and matched each to the Sensor Log's `Orient
β/γ` at the same moments:

- Plateau `x:7.50 y:0.00 z:-7.50` matched `β≈179° γ≈83°` (both axes
  pinned near their max magnitude).
- Plateau `x:-7.50 y:0.00 z:7.50` matched `β≈-178° γ≈-79°` (both
  flipped sign from the above -- and the output flipped sign to match,
  on both axes).
- Plateau `x:7.50 y:0.00 z:7.50` matched `β≈+94° γ≈-60 to -88°` (β same
  sign as the 1st case -> x sign matches; γ opposite sign from the 1st
  case -> z sign matches).
- The long final plateau `x:28.09 y:-28.94 z:-29.24` (held 41.9s-49.3s,
  the recording's own tail) matched `β≈5° γ≈2°` -- i.e. the phone was
  essentially FLAT/AT REST for over 7 seconds, yet the model held
  near-MAXIMUM rotation on all 3 axes simultaneously.

The 4-plateau cross-check proves the AXIS MAPPING itself (which raw
signal drives X/Y/Z, and each one's sign) was correct for all 4 real
test movements -- sign flips in beta/gamma correctly flipped the
corresponding output's sign every time, with no exceptions. The 5th
data point (the long rest-state plateau) is what actually broke the
picture open: a magnitude near the configured MAX while genuinely at
rest is the opposite of what "more tilt = more rotation" should give.

Root cause: `cfg.phoneResponsiveRotationCurve`'s default was
DECREASING -- `{x:0,y:1}` (t=0/no-tilt -> curveY=1/full magnitude) to
`{x:1,y:0}` (t=1/full-tilt -> curveY=0/zero magnitude) -- backwards.
This explains BOTH remaining reports at once: (1) jitter -- near rest,
`sign(rawComponent)` on a near-zero, noisy value flips unpredictably
between + and -, and since magnitude is near-MAX there at the same
time, every sign flip is a huge, visible snap between +max and -max;
(2) "missing an axis"/doesn't match -- a real, deliberate, full-strength
tilt (the case someone would actually notice and judge against) produces
LOW magnitude, while an accidental near-rest moment (holding the phone
still between movements) produces the model's biggest, most visible
rotation -- exactly backwards from what "the phone rotates to match my
tilt" should feel like.

Fixed the cfg default to `{x:0,y:0}` -> `{x:1,y:1}` (increasing). Also
surgically patched the ALREADY-SYNCED live value in
`data/processed/dev-panel-settings.json` to match (a code-default fix
alone would not have changed what the real device is currently
loading, since Sync had already persisted the inverted curve) --
verified via a before/after key-count diff (159 -> 159) and a scoped
`git diff` showing exactly the 1 line changed, nothing else. Checked
for newer remote commits both before and after the surgery (`git
fetch`) -- 1 found before (a routine Sync commit, unrelated file
region), none after.

Not independently browser/device-verified this round (same "no need to
verify" instruction as the previous entry) -- this fix is grounded
directly in the pasted real-device log data rather than a live test,
which is what made it possible to diagnose with high confidence without
one.

`index.html`'s cache-buster bumped `?v=113` -> `?v=114`.

2026-09-28  -  (31st)

2 direct follow-ups: (1) "does the starting orientation of my phone
matter?"; (2) a new feature -- a "Rotation Reset On/Off" checkbox in
Phone Model, where a double-tap (mobile) or double-click (desktop)
anywhere on screen re-baselines the phone model's rotation so its own
XYZ axes match world XYZ at that instant, regardless of the phone's
absolute orientation when reset.

Answered (1) directly rather than guessing: X/Z (beta/gamma-driven) are
absolute angles relative to "phone lying flat," a fixed physical zero
-- NOT affected by whenever the page happened to load. Y (compass/
alpha-driven) IS relative to a baseline (`phoneAlphaBaseline`) captured
once on the FIRST orientation reading after page load -- so whichever
direction the phone was facing at that moment becomes Y's permanent
zero. This is exactly the gap (2)'s reset button closes.

Built (2): new `cfg.phoneRotationResetEnabled` (default false) +
"Rotation Reset On/Off" checkbox in Responsive Rotation.
`setupPhoneRotationResetGesture()` wires a native `dblclick` listener
(Desktop) plus a manually-detected touch double-tap (2 `touchend`
events within 350ms and 40px of each other -- most mobile browsers
don't fire `dblclick` from a tap gesture), both excluding clicks/taps
that land inside `#devPanel` or the floating DEV toggle button.
`resetPhoneModelRotationBaseline()` captures the CURRENT raw nx/ny
(gamma/45, beta/45 on the device path; the existing
tiltMagnitude/tiltAngle-derived reconstruction on the mouse path) plus
the current compass alpha as new baselines, subtracted in
`computePhoneCombinedQuat()` before the final +-1 clamp. Manual
`phoneModelRotX/Y/Z` sliders are deliberately untouched -- the request
was specifically about matching the phone's real orientation, not
resetting a separate, deliberate manual offset.

Direct follow-up mid-build caught a real design flaw before it shipped:
"if im lying in bed and i hit reset, you will have to adjust the beta
gama accordingly so its not always reading real world up as up." The
first version subtracted the baseline from the SHARED, already-clamped
nx/ny (each individually clamped to +-1 upstream) -- lying in bed with
the phone near-vertical means real beta is already near its own clamp
boundary, so nx/ny are already saturated before the baseline is even
subtracted, leaving almost no headroom to respond to further real tilt
around the new "neutral" pose. Fixed by capturing/subtracting the
baseline on the RAW, pre-clamp quantity instead (mirroring how
`phoneAlphaBaseline`'s own delta was already computed before ITS final
clamp). A standalone numeric sweep (not just "does it respond to a
nearby tilt") caught a 2nd, subtler version of the same problem: beta's
raw clamp was still +-90 (the shared pipeline's own bound) even after
this fix, which is only HALF of beta's true +-180 physical range -- a
reset captured at exactly beta=90 (lying in bed) still produced a
fully one-sided, asymmetric response (dead from 90 up to 135, working
fine from 90 down to 45). Widened beta's OWN raw clamp (not the shared
one other features still rely on) to +-180 in `computePhoneRawNxNy()`
specifically, confirmed via the same sweep to give a clean, symmetric
+-1 response in both directions around any reset point. gamma's true
physical range genuinely is +-90, so it needed no such widening.

Not independently browser/device-verified this round (same standing
"no need to verify" instruction) -- both design flaws above were caught
by a standalone numeric script reproducing the exact math with
synthetic inputs before the feature ever touched a real device, not by
live testing.

`index.html`'s cache-buster bumped `?v=114` -> `?v=115`.
