2026-09-28  -  (32nd)

Direct report after real device testing (phone lying flat/stationary,
tested single taps, double taps, then physical tilts left/right/up/
down): "No good... single tap causes changes in the phone which
shouldnt be happening... double tap or tap orients the phone
differently each time... rarely the orientation i want." Pasted a
Phone Model Log + Sensor Log capture from the actual test.

Cross-referenced the 2 logs by timestamp (same technique as the 30th
entry): the Sensor Log's `Orient β/γ` held flat (~0.5°/-1.0°) for the
ENTIRE 20+ second stationary test, yet the Phone Model Log showed the
rotation swinging between values as large as +-57° with no correlation
to any tap or real input. Checked the live git-tracked settings
directly rather than guessing: `textPhoneResponsiveRotationRange` had
drifted to `{"min":-57,"max":45}` (not this project's own `{0,30}`
default) and `sliderPhoneRotationDamping` was `1` (instant -- the
28th/29th entries' smoothing fix had zero live effect). Root cause of
the swinging: `magnitude = min + (max-min)*curveY` means a NEGATIVE min
produces a nonzero magnitude even at curveY=0 (near-zero tilt) --
multiplied by `Math.sign(rawComponent)`, which flips unpredictably
between +1/-1 from ordinary real sensor noise on a value that should be
~0. This had nothing to do with tapping -- the model was jittering the
whole time and the taps just coincided with what the user was watching.
Direct follow-up confirmed setting the range to "unlimited" would make
this WORSE (a wider range doesn't remove a negative min's nonzero
baseline), and revealed the range was never meant to affect mobile at
all: "i had it since on desktop, the phone model rotation was
determined by distance of the cursor... in a no cursor scenario i have
no use for it."

Also flagged 2 more issues from the same test: (1) "when i rotate past
180 degrees, i dont want it to suddenly flip to -180" and "there
shouldnt be any rotational thresholds on mobile"; (2) after checking
the real Blender model's own local axes directly ("+y is the top of
the phone... +x is right... Z axis [is] perpendicular to the true phone
screen"), the left/right<->axis mapping from the 29th entry was WRONG:
gamma should drive Y, not Z (compass moves to Z instead).

Fixed all of it as one redesign, verified via 2 standalone numeric
scripts before shipping (no live device access from this sandbox):

1. Added a small deadzone (`PHONE_RESPONSIVE_DEADZONE = 0.02`) to
   `computePhoneResponsiveAxisDeg()` -- any sufficiently small
   `rawComponent` now returns exactly 0 regardless of what min/max a
   user sets, closing this bug class permanently rather than just
   fixing today's specific bad value.
2. Split Phone Model rotation into 2 genuinely different pipelines by
   input source. MOBILE (`lastInputSource === 'device'`): direct,
   UNWRAPPED, unthresholded passthrough of real beta/gamma/alpha
   delta-from-baseline -- no curve, no range, no deadzone, no clamp.
   New `phoneUnwrappedBeta/Gamma/Alpha` state (separate from the shared
   `tiltMagnitude`/`tiltAngle` pipeline other features still use)
   accumulates the SHORTEST delta between consecutive raw readings onto
   a running total that can exceed +-180/+-360 arbitrarily, so a
   continuous real rotation past the wrap boundary produces continuous
   output with no snap -- verified with a synthetic reading sequence
   (170->175->179->-179->-175->-170->-160) climbing smoothly through
   180 to 200 instead of snapping. DESKTOP: unchanged cursor-distance-
   driven curve/range/fineTune/deadzone system, confirmed as the right
   scope by the user's own "no cursor scenario" statement above.
3. Replaced a sequential Euler X-then-Y-then-Z composition with a
   single combined axis-angle rotation for X/Y (Z composed separately
   on top) -- a sequential Euler composition rotates around the Y axis
   AFTER it's already been tilted by the X rotation, introducing real,
   visible cross-axis distortion for any compound tilt (roughly the
   product of the 2 angles in radians -- ~16deg at two simultaneous
   30deg tilts). The combined approach (axis = normalize(xDeg,yDeg,0),
   angle = hypot(xDeg,yDeg)) reduces to an exact pure-X or pure-Y
   rotation in each single-axis case and works for unbounded magnitudes
   (needed since mobile no longer clamps) -- verified via a standalone
   script rotating world +Y/+Z test vectors through pure-X, pure-Y,
   compound, and a large (120deg) unbounded case, confirming zero
   leakage into the wrong axis in every case.
4. Remapped the axis assignment: X <- beta (unchanged), Y <- gamma
   (inverted, moved from Z), Z <- compass (moved from Y).
5. `resetPhoneModelRotationBaseline()` updated to match: baselines
   `phoneBeta/Gamma/AlphaBaseline` (mobile, against the new unwrapped
   values) or `phoneNx/NyBaseline` (desktop, raw pre-clamp domain,
   unchanged reasoning from the 31st entry) depending on the active
   input source.

Settings-file surgery: reset the live, drifted
`textPhoneResponsiveRotationRange` from `{"min":-57,"max":45}` back to
`{"min":0,"max":30}` -- explicit permission given ("you can set my min
max to whatever you want"). Verified via a scoped `git diff` showing
exactly the 1 line changed. `sliderPhoneRotationDamping` (currently `1`,
instant) was left untouched -- not reported as a problem, and no longer
relevant to the mobile bug now that mobile bypasses the whole
curve/range/damping-adjacent pipeline this round didn't touch damping
itself.

Not independently browser/device-verified this round (same standing
instruction) -- every fix in this entry was verified via a standalone
numeric script with synthetic inputs, cross-checked against the user's
own real device log data, not a live test.

`index.html`'s cache-buster bumped `?v=115` -> `?v=116`.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-09-28  -  (33rd)

Direct report after further real device testing: "better but not fully
fixed." 3 distinct issues:

1. "Double tap doesnt work. Its registering single tap, but its acting
   buggy. I tap, and for a split second i see the phone model out of
   orientation, then it flashes back... when i tap on the right of the
   screen, the phone is tilted clockwise... if i tap on the left side,
   its tilted anticlockwise. I think the 3js pan and zoom controls may
   be interfering." Follow-up: "or maybe its the phone tilt cursor
   tracking bugging it up... using both the phone tilt and the cursor
   tracking."

   Root cause, confirmed rather than guessed: many mobile browsers fire
   a SYNTHETIC `mousemove` (a touch-compatibility shim) after a real
   touch event. Without a guard, a single tap briefly flipped
   `lastInputSource` to `'mouse'` and computed Phone Model's rotation
   from the TAP'S OWN SCREEN POSITION via the desktop cursor-distance
   path, until the next real `deviceorientation` event reverted it a
   frame or two later -- exactly matching "flash then revert," and the
   position-dependence (tap right/left) is literally that formula
   reacting to the tap's own X position. Fixed by guarding
   `handleMouseMoveFallback()` with
   `MouseEvent.sourceCapabilities.firesTouchEvents` (true only for a
   synthetic mouse event generated from touch, never a real mouse/
   trackpad move). Separately, the user's own OrbitControls suspicion
   was ALSO real: OrbitControls attaches its own touch handlers to the
   canvas and commonly calls `stopPropagation()` (standard practice to
   block the browser's own default touch/scroll gestures) -- a
   BUBBLE-phase listener on `window` (the Rotation Reset gesture's
   original implementation) could never see an event OrbitControls
   stopped first. Fixed by switching both the `dblclick` and `touchend`
   listeners to the CAPTURE phase, which always runs top-down before
   the event reaches the canvas, regardless of what OrbitControls does
   afterward.

2. "When I lock pan, zoom, or rotate, lock the relevant sliders as
   well. Those 2 should be in sync. I guess FOV can just not be locked
   ever." Added `setSliderLocked()`, wired into `applyCameraLockState()`:
   Lock Pan disables the 3 camera position sliders (X/Y/Z), Lock Zoom
   disables the Zoom slider, Lock Rotate disables Yaw/Pitch; FOV is
   never touched. New `.dev-row-locked` CSS class (style.css) greys out
   a locked row. Also fixed a related latent bug found while touching
   this function: `applyCameraLockState()` was only ever called from
   the 3 lock checkboxes' own `wireCheckbox` callbacks, so a RESTORED
   "locked" state from a Sync never actually disabled OrbitControls (or
   now the sliders) until the checkbox was re-toggled by hand -- fixed
   by also calling it once at Camera-group build time.

3. "I am going to describe both the phone model and my real phone with
   the same axes... Z is pointing to the sky, X is rightwards, y is
   up... When I rotate my real phone around the Y axis, the phone model
   on screen rotates around the Z axis. And vice versa as well. Tilting
   the phone up and down seems to work good." A clean, reproducible,
   BOTH-DIRECTIONS Y<->Z swap with X (beta) confirmed unaffected is the
   signature of the standard Blender Z-up -> glTF Y-up axis conversion:
   the real Blender model is authored Z-up (Z=sky when flat), but
   glTF/three.js is Y-up, and glTF exporters standardly bake in a
   fixed conversion rotation on export that swaps Y and Z while
   leaving X alone. This means world-Y and world-Z in this file's own
   code were ALREADY swapped relative to the Blender-described axes the
   29th entry's mapping assumed -- independent of the rotation formula
   itself, whose sign/direction logic (verified against 4 real test
   movements in that same entry) was correct the whole time, just
   landing on the wrong WORLD axis letter. Fixed by swapping which
   world axis gamma and alpha target: gamma (left-right tilt) now
   combines with beta's axis-angle rotation on world X/Z (was X/Y);
   alpha (compass/spin) now gets its own separate rotation around world
   Y (was Z). Renamed the supporting quaternion/axis variables
   (`_phoneTiltAxis`/`_phoneTiltQuat`/`_phoneSpinQuat`/
   `PHONE_SPIN_AXIS`, replacing the old `_phoneXYAxis`/`_phoneXYQuat`/
   `_phoneZQuat`/`PHONE_Z_AXIS`) to match, rather than leaving
   Y/Z-labeled names now pointing at the other axis. Verified
   numerically: a pure beta, pure gamma, and pure alpha input each now
   produce a rotation with the corresponding axis vector completely
   unchanged and the other 2 axes correctly swinging -- zero leakage
   in either direction.

Not independently browser/device-verified this round (same standing
instruction) -- fix #3 was verified via a standalone numeric script
reproducing the exact quaternion composition; fixes #1 and #2 are
standard, well-understood browser/library behaviors (synthetic touch-
mouse events, event capture vs. bubble phase) applied directly, not
something a script could meaningfully simulate without a real touch
device or a real OrbitControls-attached canvas.

`index.html`'s cache-buster bumped `?v=116` -> `?v=117`.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-09-28  -  (34th)

3 more direct follow-ups: (1) "provide me a checkbox to turn the Z axis
rotation on and off" (revised mid-turn to "actually provide me a check
box for all 3 axes on and off"); (2) "double tap works. BUT. after the
realignment, the rotation becomes wrong. I think its still using the
rotaiton axes prior to the realignment"; (3) "Provide me a slider per
axes for me to scale the rotational relationship. In case i want to
slow down or speed up the rotaion of the phone model."

Diagnosed (2) before implementing (1)/(3): `resetPhoneModelRotationBaseline()`
used to branch on `lastInputSource`, updating ONLY the mobile
(beta/gamma/alpha) baseline OR ONLY the desktop (nx/ny) baseline,
never both. If `lastInputSource` read wrong for even a single frame at
the exact tap instant -- a real, established risk this session (see
the 33rd entry's synthetic-touch-mousemove finding) -- the reset
silently re-baselined the INACTIVE pipeline while leaving the REAL one
completely untouched, exactly matching "still using the axes prior to
the realignment." Fixed by updating BOTH baselines unconditionally on
every reset, regardless of which input source is flagged active at
that instant -- whichever pipeline actually ends up driving the
rotation afterward is guaranteed to have a freshly-captured baseline
either way, removing the dependency on `lastInputSource` being exactly
right at that one moment.

Built (1) and (3) together as 6 new controls in Responsive Rotation:
`checkboxPhoneAxisXEnabled`/`Y`/`Z` (default all true) and
`sliderPhoneRotationScaleX`/`Y`/`Z` (0-3x, default 1). Axis letters
match the WORLD axis each real motion drives per the 33rd entry's
mapping: X = beta (up/down), Y = alpha/compass (spin), Z = gamma
(left/right). Applied in ONE shared place in `computePhoneCombinedQuat()`,
after both the mobile and desktop branches compute their own
`betaDeg`/`gammaDeg`/`alphaDeg`, so both pipelines respect the same 6
controls without duplicating the logic per-branch. Disabling an axis
zeroes it outright (not just scale=0) so a disabled axis can't
contribute even a tiny floating-point residual.

Not independently browser/device-verified this round (same standing
instruction) -- the reset-baseline fix removes a specific branching
dependency rather than introducing new math to verify, and the 6 new
controls are a direct, mechanical application of already-verified
per-axis values (scale/enable applied before the already-tested
axis-angle combine step from the 33rd entry).

`index.html`'s cache-buster bumped `?v=117` -> `?v=118`.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-09-28  -  (35th)

Direct report: "when i tilt far, when the phone model reaches some
rotational threshold like 180 degrees i think, it jumps then rotates
180. I cant really describe it as its diffretn for each axis. But
asically i want the rotation to cotinue forever. like if i spun my
phone continnually around its x axis, the phone model would show that."

Diagnosed this as a FUNDAMENTAL limitation of the previous design, not
a patchable bug: mobile's rotation was driven by `deviceorientation`'s
beta/gamma/alpha, which are absolute orientation angles derived via
Euler decomposition. Euler angles have a hard representational limit --
gamma physically CANNOT exceed +-90 degrees, because past that point
the SAME real 3D orientation gets re-expressed through a DIFFERENT
combination of beta/alpha instead (gimbal lock) -- which is exactly why
the symptom looked "different for each axis": beta spans -180..180,
gamma only -90..90, alpha wraps at 360, each hitting its own
representational boundary differently. The previous session's
"unwrapping" fix (30th/33rd entries) only solved the RAW READING'S own
wraparound jump -- it did nothing for the deeper issue that a FRESH
axis-angle quaternion reconstructed from an ever-growing angle, once
slerp-damped toward from the previous frame's displayed orientation,
effectively reverses direction past 180 degrees (quaternion double-
cover: the shortest path back toward a "191 degrees" target is actually
via an equivalent "-169 degrees" representation) -- the reported "jump
then rotates 180."

No incremental patch to the existing angle-based math can fix this,
because the limitation is in the SIGNAL itself (deviceorientation's
absolute, Euler-decomposed angles), not in how it was being processed.

Replaced mobile's rotation source entirely with GYROSCOPE integration:
`devicemotion.rotationRate` (already visible in this project's own
Sensors log as "Gyro a/b/y") gives TRUE, instantaneous angular velocity
around the device's own CURRENT local X/Y/Z axes -- a fundamentally
different kind of signal than an absolute orientation angle, with no
representational range limit and no gimbal lock. New
`integratePhoneGyroRotation(e)`, called once per REAL `devicemotion`
tick (not per render frame, since it needs the actual elapsed time `dt`
between samples to integrate `deg/second` correctly): computes a tiny
incremental rotation for that one tick (beta+gamma combined into one
axis-angle rotation, same Euler-decoupling technique as the existing
tilt logic, just now in the LOCAL/body frame; alpha composed separately
around local Y) and composes it onto a PERSISTENT quaternion
(`phoneGyroQuat`) via RIGHT-multiply -- the standard way real IMU/AHRS
orientation tracking integrates body-frame angular velocity over time.
Because this never reconstructs an absolute angle from scratch --
only ever composes a tiny, always-valid increment onto whatever the
current accumulated orientation already is -- there is no
representational limit at any accumulated total, at any axis.

Verified numerically before shipping (no physical device in this
sandbox): simulated a constant 90deg/s spin around local X at a
realistic ~60Hz tick rate for 8 seconds (720 degrees of physical
rotation, 2 full spins) -- the resulting quaternion tracked correctly
at every 90-degree checkpoint with no discontinuity, and a separate
4-second pure-spin test showed the spin axis itself drifting by only
~5.5e-16 (floating-point noise, not a real error) the entire time.

Desktop (mouse) is UNCHANGED -- a cursor position has no physical "spin
past 180 degrees" concept, so the existing curve/range-driven system
(confirmed desktop-only in the 33rd entry) still applies there
unmodified. Rotation Reset simplifies dramatically for mobile: it's now
just `phoneGyroQuat.identity()`, since there's no separate baseline to
manage any more. The per-axis enable/scale controls (34th entry) now
gate the raw gyro RATE before integration on mobile, or the raw tilt
DEGREES on desktop -- same 6 controls, same user-facing effect either
way. Removed the now-fully-dead unwrap-tracking state
(`phoneUnwrappedBeta/Gamma/Alpha`, `phoneBetaBaseline/Gamma/AlphaBaseline`,
`unwrapDelta360()`) rather than leaving it unused -- it was built and
shipped just this same day (30th/33rd entries) before this deeper
limitation was found.

Not independently browser/device-verified this round (same standing
instruction) -- verified instead via a standalone numeric integration
script simulating multiple seconds of continuous real-time gyroscope
samples, which is a stronger test of "does this handle unbounded
continuous rotation correctly" than a live device session could easily
reproduce on demand anyway.

`index.html`'s cache-buster bumped `?v=118` -> `?v=119`.

2026-09-28  -  (36th)

Direct report, same day the gyroscope-integration switch (35th entry)
shipped: "you crossed some wires. the rotation match is off. real world
Z rotation now roates the model around its Y. real world Y rotation
now rotates the model around X. real world x rotation is now Z."

Read this as a clean, consistent 3-way CYCLIC relabeling between what
`main.js` calls local X/Y/Z and what the RENDERED MODEL actually shows
-- independent of which sensor drives it: beta's motion (coded to axis
X) visually landed on Z; gamma's motion (coded to axis Z) visually
landed on Y; alpha's motion (coded to axis Y) visually landed on X.
Re-derived the geometric correspondence between physical phone motions
and Blender-authored axes from scratch (confirmed self-consistent: beta
=pitch=X, gamma=roll=Y, alpha=spin=Z in Blender's own frame) and
cross-checked it against the already-shipped, already-verified 33rd-
entry world-frame mapping -- both checks kept coming back consistent
with what was already coded, which meant the bug was NOT in the
beta/gamma/alpha-to-physical-motion correspondence, and almost
certainly specific to something the switch from world-frame absolute
angles to body-frame gyroscope integration introduced (most likely:
`devicemotion.rotationRate`'s own alpha/beta/gamma axis correspondence
not matching `deviceorientation`'s the way assumed, though this
couldn't be conclusively confirmed without either real device
instrumentation or authoritative spec access, neither available this
round).

Rather than continue re-deriving the W3C spec correspondence from
memory a further time, applied the user's own precise, empirical,
3-way-cyclic correction DIRECTLY: whichever axis slot previously
produced a given wrong letter now receives the signal that should
produce the CORRECT letter instead. Concretely, in
`integratePhoneGyroRotation()`: beta now targets the combined tilt
axis's Y-slot (was X) -- `_phoneGyroTiltAxis.set(dGammaDeg, dBetaDeg, 0)`,
gamma now targets the X-slot (was Z, its own left/right sign inversion
preserved), and `PHONE_GYRO_SPIN_LOCAL_AXIS` (alpha) now points at
local Z (was Y, `(0,0,1)` instead of `(0,1,0)`). Beta+gamma stay
combined into one axis-angle pair (both are still simultaneous "tilt"
motions, Euler-decoupling still applies), and alpha stays the separate
"spin" axis -- only WHICH of the 3 code axes each of the 3 groups
targets changed, not the underlying combine-vs-separate structure.

Desktop's cursor-driven rotation is explicitly UNTOUCHED -- this is
specific to the gyroscope body-frame integration path introduced in the
35th entry, not the pre-existing world-frame cursor-distance system,
which was already confirmed correct before gyro integration existed
and has no rotationRate dependency at all.

Not independently browser/device-verified this round (same standing
instruction) -- this fix is a direct, careful application of the
user's own empirical report rather than new math requiring its own
standalone numeric proof; if any ONE axis still comes out wrong on the
next real test, the fastest path forward is naming that ONE axis
specifically (not re-describing the whole permutation), since the
underlying combine-vs-separate structure is now confirmed sound and
only a single-axis relabel would be needed.

Traced this precisely against the actual shipped code instead of

The real, much narrower bug: `devicemotion.rotationRate.alpha` and

rate (not spin, as assumed since the 35th entry), `rr.gamma` is
actually the SPIN rate (not roll). Fixed in
`integratePhoneGyroRotation()` by reading `rr.alpha` for roll and
`rr.gamma` for spin, with the downstream axis SLOTS left completely
unchanged from the 36th entry's own structure (pitch+roll still combine
into one axis-angle rotation on X/Y, spin stays the separate rotation
on Z). Renamed the 3 per-tick local variables from their raw property
name (`dBetaDeg`/`dGammaDeg`/`dAlphaDeg`) to their PHYSICAL role
(`dPitchDeg`/`dRollDeg`/`dSpinDeg`) -- the mismatch between "which raw
property a variable was named after" and "which physical motion it
actually carried" is exactly what made this bug hard to track across 3
rounds on one feature, and the renaming is meant to prevent that same
confusion recurring on the 5th round if there is one.

Desktop's cursor-driven rotation remains untouched -- still no evidence
it's affected, since all reports so far have come from real device
testing specifically.

Not independently browser/device-verified this round (same standing
instruction) -- this is a targeted, traced correction to a specific,
now well-understood mismatch (2 named sensor properties reporting
swapped roles), not new math requiring its own standalone numeric
proof. If a single axis is STILL wrong on the next real test, the
fastest path is describing that ONE axis using the same unambiguous
physical-description style this entry's report used (naming a real,
concrete landmark like "perpendicular to the screen" or "top/bottom
edge oscillate"), not axis letters alone -- letters alone are what led
this feature through 2 extra, partially-wrong correction rounds.

2026-09-28  -  (38th)

Direct follow-up, same day, showing the 37th entry's "identity, just
alpha/gamma crossed" fix was itself still wrong: "bro still wrong. real
world Z - model y. real world x - model z. real world y - model x." --
the exact same 3-way cyclic permutation the 36th entry had already
described and supposedly fixed. Direct instruction this round: "bro
just switch the outputs i told you" -- an explicit request to stop
re-deriving a new root-cause theory and just apply the reported
before/after mapping directly.

Took that literally: relabeling "whatever used to visually rotate
around axis A now rotates around axis B" for every one of the 3
reported cases is a fixed 3-cycle permutation (old-Z->new-X for pitch,
old-X->new-Y for roll, old-Y->new-Z for spin). Conjugating the WHOLE
accumulated quaternion by that permutation every frame would achieve
this, but since conjugation distributes over quaternion composition,
it's mathematically equivalent (and far simpler) to apply the same
permutation directly to each individual increment's own axis vector
before building it -- once, in `integratePhoneGyroRotation()`, not
every frame. Applied to the 2 axis vectors that function already
builds: the combined pitch+roll tilt vector `(dPitchDeg, dRollDeg, 0)`
-> `(0, dPitchDeg, dRollDeg)`; the separate spin axis `(0,0,1)` ->
`(1,0,0)` (now `PHONE_GYRO_SPIN_LOCAL_AXIS`'s new value). The
alpha/gamma raw-property assignment from the 37th entry is left
untouched -- this fix is about which SLOT each role's value lands in,
a separate question from which raw sensor field feeds which role.

An isolated standalone quaternion-math script was used to sanity-check
the ALGEBRA of this permutation (confirming it reduces cleanly to a
single-axis rotation for each pure-motion case, and that applying it to
the round-37 code's own axis vectors matches the derived permuted
vectors by hand). That same isolated script, run against the CURRENT
(37th-entry) code, predicted an identity mapping (pitch keeps its own
axis fixed, etc.) that does NOT match this round's real-device report
of a 3-way cyclic permutation for that same code -- a real, unresolved
discrepancy between the isolated quaternion math (which has no
visibility into anything beyond `phoneGyroQuat` itself) and the actual
rendered result on a real phone. `computePhoneCombinedQuat()` and
`applyPhoneModelTransform()` were both read directly to rule out an
obvious extra transform (there isn't one -- `phoneModelWrapper.quaternion`
is a straight damped slerp toward the combined quaternion, no further
remap), so the source of that discrepancy is NOT understood this round.
Given that, this fix trusts the user's literal, real-device-tested
report as ground truth over the isolated simulation, rather than
withholding a fix until the simulation can be reconciled.

Not independently browser/device-verified this round (same standing
limitation as every prior round on this feature -- no physical device
available in this sandbox). If this is STILL wrong on the next real
test, do NOT reach for a 5th permutation guess -- the unexplained
gap between the isolated `phoneGyroQuat` math and the real rendered
result (noted above) is the more likely place a 5th round needs to
actually dig, e.g. instrumenting `phoneGyroQuat`'s live value on the
real device at a few known physical orientations and comparing directly
against what the isolated script predicts for those same inputs, rather
than reasoning about the code in isolation again.

2026-09-28  -  (39th)

Direct follow-up, same day: the 38th entry's permutation fix (also based
on axis-invariance testing) still didn't match the real device --
"bro what the fuck its still broken." The user then gave the single
most precise specification of this whole feature: physical axes 1/2/3
map onto model axes X/Y/Z, with 1(X)=left-right, 2(Y)=bottom(charging
port)-to-top, 3(Z)=back-to-face -- and stated the CURRENT bug precisely
as "123 to YZX," wanting "123 to XYZ."

ROOT CAUSE FINALLY FOUND: every round's own verification (this
session's 4th/5th/38th-round fixes included) tested the code's output
via AXIS INVARIANCE ("which world axis stays fixed under the
rotation"). But the actual ground truth the user reads is the Phone
Model Log's `Rot x/y/z`, an Euler('XYZ')-DECOMPOSED quaternion (see
`restartPhoneModelLogTimer()`). These are NOT the same measurement --
axis-invariance permutes cleanly under a change of basis; Euler-XYZ
decomposition does not, which is exactly why 2 different
algebraically-sound "permute the axis vector" fixes both still failed
once actually read off Rot x/y/z.

Verified by replicating three.js's own quaternion->Euler('XYZ')
extraction (matrix form, cross-checked against
Matrix4.makeRotationFromQuaternion + Euler.setFromRotationMatrix, not
reconstructed from memory of the formula alone) in a standalone script:
the 38th entry's shipped code reproduces "123->YZX" EXACTLY under this
measurement, confirming it as the correct methodology. A brute-force
search of all 6 (pitch-axis, roll-axis) placements against this same
Euler-XYZ measurement found exactly one clean, zero-cross-talk match:
the plain, UN-permuted structure -- combined tilt = pitch+roll on X/Y,
spin separate on local Z. This is literally the ORIGINAL slot structure
from when gyro integration first shipped, before any of the 4
subsequent permutation attempts.

Also reverted the 37th entry's "alpha/gamma crossed" raw-property
theory back to the W3C-spec-standard reading (`rr.beta`=pitch rate,
`rr.gamma`=roll rate, `rr.alpha`=spin rate) -- that theory was built
entirely on the same now-discredited axis-invariance measurement, so it
has no remaining support once the measurement itself is known to be
wrong. Kept gamma's existing inversion (matches the sign convention
already established for gamma in the old absolute-angle system).

Mid-fix, the user separately flagged "i even asked you to change the
object axes to rotate with the phone" -- checked `createFatAxesVisualization()`/
`ensureObjectAxesFor()` directly: the Object Axes gizmo IS correctly
parented onto `phoneModelWrapper` and draws its 3 lines along the
group's own local X/Y/Z (confirmed via source, not just assumed), so
that earlier fix is intact and not a contributing factor here.

Not independently browser/device-verified this round (same standing
limitation -- no physical device in this sandbox). This is the first
round grounded in a methodology that's actually been validated against
one real data point (the 38th entry's own reported bug), rather than
an assumption about which measurement the report corresponds to -- if
this is STILL wrong on the next real test, re-verify using the SAME
Euler-XYZ script methodology against the NEW reported numbers rather
than reverting to axis-invariance reasoning.

2026-09-28  -  (41st)

Direct follow-up, same day: the 40th entry's literal swap+invert fix
was STILL wrong -- reported as "REAL XYZ is translated to MODEL YZX"
(real X->model Y, real Y->model Z, real Z->model X, a clean 3-cycle).

Changed approach: rather than re-permuting `integratePhoneGyroRotation()`'s
own input axis vectors (tried in the 39th/40th rounds, both still wrong
despite being mathematically equivalent to a whole-quaternion
conjugation in isolation), applied the fix as a DIRECT CONJUGATION of
the fully-accumulated `phoneGyroQuat` at its read site in
`computePhoneCombinedQuat()`. New constant `PHONE_GYRO_OUTPUT_FIX_QUAT`
is the inverse of the reported 3-cycle (undoes X->Y->Z->X by relabeling
Y->X, Z->Y, X->Z), a 120deg rotation around (1,1,1)/sqrt(3); applied as
`Q' = P * Q * P^-1` via `premultiply()`/`multiply()`.

Caught a real sign/direction error before shipping this time: the
first candidate quaternion `(0.5,0.5,0.5,0.5)` numerically verified to
rotate ex->ey->ez->ex, the OPPOSITE of the intended ex->ez->ey->ex --
using it would have applied the 3-cycle a SECOND time instead of
undoing it. Caught via a standalone script checking where the
candidate quaternion actually sends each basis vector, not assumed
correct from the construction alone. Corrected to `(-0.5,-0.5,-0.5,0.5)`
(the inverse), re-verified to send ex->ez, ey->ex, ez->ey as intended.

Not independently browser/device-verified this round (same standing
limitation). This is the first round to apply the fix at a different
LAYER of the pipeline (post-hoc output conjugation) rather than the
input-slot layer that failed twice already -- if this is somehow still
wrong, that's meaningful new evidence that something between
`phoneGyroQuat` and the rendered result is not what this file's own
code comments describe (a plain damped slerp, no other transform), and
the next round should re-audit that pipeline directly rather than
trying a 4th input-slot permutation.

actually correlates with which physical axis (`beta`<->Y, `gamma`<->Z,

mechanism reads as Y, not X or Z respectively).

Solved both simultaneously in `integratePhoneGyroRotation()`: physical
X now routes `alpha` into the x-component (the one slot that reads
correctly), physical Z routes `gamma` into the y-component (which reads
as Z), physical Y routes `beta` into the separate mechanism (which

Not independently browser/device-verified this round (same standing
limitation). Beta's and alpha's signs in their NEW slots are UNVERIFIED
-- this codebase has repeatedly shown that moving a value between
mechanisms (combined vs. separate) can flip its effective sign in ways
that aren't predictable from the old mechanism's sign alone, so if X or
Y comes out backwards next test, that's a plausible, easy next fix
(negate that one role's raw value) rather than evidence the letter/slot
solve above is wrong.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-09-29  -  (45th)

Direct request: "I want to render the 3js screen space onto the
virtual screen within the app... In my phone glb file, the phone
screen face is in its own layer called 'Screen Face'... Make sure its
not recursive infinitely. Provide me a slider to control the levels of
recursion. Say make it 1-10."

New Virtual Screen feature: renders the app's own live 3D scene onto
the phone GLB's own 'Screen Face' mesh, found at load time by
`findPhoneScreenMesh()` (case/whitespace-tolerant name match, logs a
console warning if a loaded model has no such mesh -- confirmed only
`Iphone17MaxPro.glb` currently has one; P5 Project 1 and Pixel 9A do
not). Recursion is bounded BY CONSTRUCTION, not by a runtime guard:
`renderVirtualScreen()` runs a FIXED number of sequential off-screen
passes each frame (`cfg.screenRecursionLevels`, 1-10), ping-ponging
between 2 `THREE.WebGLRenderTarget`s -- pass 0 renders with the screen
mesh hidden entirely (the recursion's base case, guaranteeing no
screen-in-screen at the floor), each later pass textures the screen
with the PRIOR pass's own capture. The final pass's capture becomes
the screen's texture for the real, visible frame. Since no render call
ever reads a target it's currently writing to, and the loop always
terminates after exactly `levels` iterations, this cannot loop forever
regardless of the slider's value. The screen uses a dedicated, reused
`THREE.MeshBasicMaterial` (self-lit, matching a real display) rather
than the mesh's own PBR material while the feature is on;
`resetPhoneScreenMaterial()` restores the real glTF material when off
or when no model is loaded. `disposePhoneModelRaw()` is guarded so
switching phone models (or reloading the same one) swaps the screen
mesh back to its own original material BEFORE the generic per-model
material/texture disposal traversal runs -- without this, disposal
would have torn down the shared render target's own texture (still
referenced by the reusable screen material) the first time a model was
switched while Screen Render was on.

Follow-up direct request, same day: "provide a render resolution or
quality slider... place them in a new toggleable group called
'RECURSIVE RENDER'." Added `cfg.screenRenderResolution` (%, 10-200,
default 100) -- `ensureScreenRenderTargets()` now lazily recreates the
2 render targets (disposing the old pair) only when the resolution has
actually changed, scaling a 384x768 base capture size. Moved the 3
controls (On/Off, Recursion Levels, Render Resolution) out of PHONE
MODEL's own body into a NEW top-level dev-panel group, 'RECURSIVE
RENDER', made toggleable via devPanel.js's own
`makeDevGroupToggleable()` -- deliberately a TOP-LEVEL group (a sibling
of PHONE MODEL, not nested inside it) because that function's own
title lookup uses a direct-child selector
(`#tabTabContent > .dev-section > .dev-section-title[...]`), the exact
same restriction `findNestedGroupContent()` elsewhere in this file was
written to work around for group CONTENT lookups -- rather than
reimplement that restriction away a 2nd time for a toggle checkbox,
making this its own top-level group was the simpler, equally-correct
fix, matching how the template's own built-in Dev Panel/Debug groups
use the same mechanism.

Separately, 3 direct reports on Phone Model's existing Rotation Scale/
per-axis controls:
1. "allow me to go negative [on Rotation Scale]... -1 means rotate the
   other direction at the same scale" -- widened all 3 sliders'
   min from 0 to -3 (max unchanged at 3). No other code change needed:
   every consumer already just multiplies by the scale, so a negative
   value already flips direction correctly.
2. "I was wrong [that desktop didn't need all 3 axes] -- make all 3
   appliable, I will set it with the on/off." Desktop's Y axis
   (spin) was wired for the first time -- it previously only existed
   on mobile (see the 26th-region entries for that history). Desktop
   has no 3rd independent input channel the way a real gyroscope does
   (a 2D cursor position is only 2 DOF), so it reuses the SAME
   horizontal cursor offset that already, separately, drives the X/Z
   combined tilt -- through the same shared curve/range/fine-tune
   system every other axis uses -- composed as its own rotation around
   world UP on top of the tilt, mirroring the separate-mechanism
   pattern mobile's own spin axis already uses.
3. Direct follow-up, real isolated-testing report: "the current what
   is labeled as Z is Y, and vice versa" (desktop only -- confirmed via
   AskUserQuestion that this was specifically about desktop, and
   specifically an isolated one-checkbox-at-a-time test, the same
   methodology that previously resolved the identical bug class on
   mobile per the 10th-round entry above). Swapped which cfg field
   gates which contribution: the combined-tilt contribution (feeding
   the same axis-angle as X) is now gated by `phoneAxisYEnabled`/
   `phoneRotationScaleY`; the separate spin-around-UP contribution is
   now gated by `phoneAxisZEnabled`/`phoneRotationScaleZ` -- swapped
   from the initial, un-tested guess. X's own gating is unchanged (not
   reported as wrong). Mobile's own gating is untouched -- it lives in
   a completely separate function (`integratePhoneGyroRotation()`),
   already independently verified via the same methodology.

Live browser verification could not be completed this round despite
~20+ retries across 3 different local-server ports and both reused and
brand-new tabs -- every attempt hit `net::ERR_CONNECTION_RESET` on
`main.js` and/or `devPanel.js`, confirmed via the Python static
server's own request log to be serving every file with a real `200`
every single time, meaning the failure is in the sandbox's own
browser-proxy layer, not the code or the file server (the same
documented flaky-delivery issue this project's CLAUDE.md already
describes, just unusually persistent this round). Verified instead via
`node --check` (syntax) and a careful manual read-through of the full
diff, including re-deriving the axis-angle math by hand. Per direct
instruction ("from now on you dont need to attempt sandbox
verification. just skip that step"), browser-pane verification is
skipped entirely for this project going forward -- saved as a standing
memory.

`index.html`'s cache-buster bumped `?v=128` -> `?v=133` across this
round's several edits (each edit bumped it once; the final committed
value is what matters).

Direct report: "I dont see the Recursive Render image. I still see the

**CORRECTS the 45th entry's own claim that "only Iphone17MaxPro.glb
has a mesh named 'Screen Face'"** -- that was wrong, based on a false-
negative "no console warning" observation made during the same

alone. **Pixel 9A** (what the user is actually using) has NO mesh/node
named 'Screen Face' at all -- its screen is one PRIMITIVE inside a
combined 'Front' mesh (`Cube.015`), distinguished only by a MATERIAL

splits into separate `THREE.Mesh` objects per primitive on load (each
with its own single material), but each object's own `.name` still
comes from the shared parent node/mesh ("Front"/"Cube.015"), not the
material -- so a mesh-name-only search could never find it, matching
exactly what the user saw (the search silently found nothing every

45th entry's claim.

Fixed `findPhoneScreenMesh()` with a 2nd pass: if no mesh/node name
matches 'screen face' (pass 1, unchanged, still finds P5 Project 1),
fall back to matching `obj.material.name === 'screen face'` (pass 2,
new -- finds Pixel 9A's screen primitive directly by its own
material). CLAUDE.md's own matching gotcha corrected in place (not
just this changelog entry) since it's a living doc, not append-only.

2026-09-29  -  (48th)

Direct multi-part request: "Make the Object Model Selector a Item
Selector actually. Allow me to import glb models, If I import a glb
model with the same name as an existing import, provide a popup to
ask if i should overwrite. In all imported models, make it look for a
layer or mesh called Screen Face. Be flexible with capitalization. In
Recursive Render, provide a scale slider, a rotation slider, and an X
and y scale slider. Currently, I see the rendering looks off whenever
my actual browser size is different from the model mesh size... can
you add a 'To Scale' checkbox."

Raised via `AskUserQuestion` (import persistence: sync-to-git via the
existing Vercel API pattern, browser-local IndexedDB, or session-only)
-- answered "sync to git." Mid-build, found a real platform constraint
worth a 2nd `AskUserQuestion`: GitHub's simple Contents API (what
`save-settings.js` already uses) caps a single file at 1MB, but every
real phone GLB here is 1.8-4MB; Vercel's own serverless functions also
cap request bodies around 4.5MB. Offered building-now-with-the-ceiling-
accepted vs. adding chunked upload vs. holding off -- answered "build
it now, accept the ~4MB ceiling."

**New `api/upload-phone-model.js`** -- commits an imported .glb via
GitHub's Git Data API (create blob -> splice into a new tree -> create
commit -> move the branch ref), the standard mechanism for a file over
the Contents API's 1MB limit. Also maintains a new
`data/processed/SMARTPHONE MODELS/manifest.json` (a 2nd, separate
commit, via the simpler Contents API since it's small JSON) listing
every available model -- POST checks for a filename collision first
and returns 409 unless an explicit overwrite header is set, matching
the client's own confirm() popup. GET returns the manifest LIVE from
GitHub (same "don't trust the static file, it's stale until the next
deploy" reasoning `save-settings.js`'s own GET already documents).

**Client side (`main.js`)**: `PHONE_MODEL_OPTIONS` (a hardcoded const
array that needed manual upkeep every time the model folder's contents
changed -- a real, recurring maintenance burden visible in this file's
own git history) is now a mutable list populated at startup by
`loadPhoneModelManifest()` (live manifest -> static manifest.json ->
hardcoded 3-model fallback, in that order). The `<select>` dropdown is
replaced by a hand-built Item Selector (`renderPhoneModelItemSelector()`)
-- a clickable list reusing the SAME `.dp-list-picker`/
`.dp-list-picker-item` CSS this file's own preset pickers (Saved
Poses/Cameras/etc., `buildListPicker()`) already use for visual
consistency, but its own separate widget, not that function reused --
`buildListPicker()` is built around capturing/naming scalar dev-panel
STATE, with no file-import concept at all. `importPhoneModelFile()`
handles the full flow: collision check + confirm() popup, instant
local load via `URL.createObjectURL(file)` (GLTFLoader loads from a
blob: URL exactly like any other URL) so the import works for the
session even if the upload fails or the device is offline, then the
real upload, swapping the list entry's value from the temporary blob:
URL to the real permanent path once the commit actually succeeds.

**Screen Face detection for imported models**: no new code was needed
-- `findPhoneScreenMeshes()` (see the 47th entry) already operates
generically on whatever `phoneModelRaw` currently is, regardless of
whether it was loaded from the manifest or a fresh import, and was
already case/whitespace-tolerant.

**RECURSIVE RENDER texture-transform controls**: Texture Scale
(uniform), Texture Rotation, Texture X Scale, Texture Y Scale --
applied via `applyScreenTextureTransform()` to the render texture's
own UV transform (THREE.Texture's built-in repeat/rotation/center),
not the mesh geometry, with `center` set to (0.5,0.5) so
rotation/scale pivots around the middle of the mapped face. **"To
Scale"** checkbox fixes the reported distortion bug: the off-screen
capture always renders through the SAME camera used for the main view
(whose aspect ratio tracks the live browser window), but that capture
is written into a FIXED-aspect render target
(`SCREEN_RENDER_BASE_WIDTH`/`HEIGHT`) -- when on, X/Y Scale are
computed every frame from the live `camera.aspect` vs. the render
target's own fixed aspect instead of reading the sliders, and both
sliders are locked (`setSliderLocked()`, the same helper Camera Lock
Pan/Zoom/Rotate already uses) while on.

Not independently browser/device-verified (standing limitation). The
"To Scale" correction's own direction (`camAspect / targetAspect`) was
reasoned through carefully but not confirmed against a real window
resize -- if it makes the distortion WORSE instead of better, the fix
is trying the reciprocal at that one line, not re-deriving from
scratch (see `applyScreenTextureTransform()`'s own comment).

Noticed but deliberately left untouched: several new untracked files/
folders appeared directly on disk under `data/processed/SMARTPHONE
MODELS/` (`BLENDER/`, `RAW GLB/`, `Samsung Galaxy S2/`, `Samsung
Galaxy S26.glb`, `Zip/`, `blackberry/`, `iPhone 17 Max.glb`,
`samsung-galaxy-s26-black-low-poly/`) -- the user's own asset staging,
not committed or referenced by this entry's own work.

`index.html`'s cache-buster bumped `?v=134` -> `?v=135`.

2026-09-29  -  (49th)

Direct report: "fome some reason, for the recursion rendering, every
other rendered image is mirrored left to right. So the 1st image
rendered on the phone model is flipped, the 2nd one in that is not,
etc." Raised via `AskUserQuestion` whether this reproduces at
Recursion Levels=2 (the minimal case) or only compounds at 3+ --
answered "yes even at 2."

That answer is the key diagnostic: `applyScreenTextureTransform()`
runs with IDENTICAL `cfg` values on every single pass (nothing in it
varies by pass index), so it cannot itself be the source of a
difference BETWEEN generations -- ruling out a per-pass code bug in
that function. The one real structural difference between pass 0
(screen hidden -- a clean capture where the Screen Face mesh's own UV
is never sampled at all) and every pass from 1 onward (screen visible,
its geometry textured for the first time) is that any UV-orientation
defect ON THAT ONE MESH only ever enters the picture starting from
pass 1. A single, CONSTANT horizontal UV-mirror baked into the mesh
(a common glTF/Blender export quirk on a mirrored/duplicated part)
reproduces the exact reported pattern by itself: applying a mirror
once per recursion level is mathematically identical to two facing
mirrors -- 1 application reads flipped, 2 (one nested level deeper)
reads flipped-of-a-flip = back to normal, 3 flipped again, etc. Fixed
by negating `texture.repeat.x` in `applyScreenTextureTransform()`,
which cancels the mesh's own mirror at its source -- correcting every
recursion level uniformly rather than needing a per-level fix.

Not independently verified against the real mesh/device (standing
limitation) -- if this makes it worse instead of better, the mirror
theory is disproven and the fix is reverting the `-1 *` (see that
function's own comment), not flipping to the Y axis on a 2nd guess.

Same round, direct request: "i added new glb files and downsized the
others. please replace and add the new ones." The existing 3 phone
models (P5 Project 1, Pixel 9A, iPhone 17 Max Pro) are now 61-315KB
(down from 1.8-4MB); 5 new models added (Galaxy S2, Motorola Razr,
Nothing2, Samsung Galaxy S26, iPhone 17 Max), all comfortably under
any size limit discussed for the Item Selector's own import feature
(the 48th entry). `manifest.json` updated to list all 8. Left the
user's own raw asset-staging folders (`BLENDER/`, `RAW GLB/`, `Zip/`,
`blackberry/`, `Samsung Galaxy S2/`, `samsung-galaxy-s26-black-low-poly/`)
untouched -- only the finished `.glb` files at the top level were
asked to be added, matching this project's own raw-vs-processed
convention (§11).

`index.html`'s own cache-buster bump was missed in the same commit as
the code change (`012b682`) and had to be pushed as an immediate
follow-up commit (`6a6567f`) -- `?v=135` -> `?v=136`.

2026-09-29  -  (50th)

Direct report: "something is off with the scale. i cant see the phone
model right now" + a question about how the new Item Selector is
supposed to work ("how do i select an object? st click it? will it
change automatically? i think soemthign is wrong. it isnt working liek
before"). User's own follow-up clarification: "the model units may not
be the same, but i tink all my models are scaled the same" and "most
of my glb files were exported with mm.. the hand may be different."

Root-caused with a proper measurement, not a guess: wrote a small Node
script that parses each of the 8 GLBs' own raw JSON directly and walks
the REAL scene graph (full 4x4 matrix accumulation across every node,
including nested/multiple root nodes -- a first, naive attempt using
just a root node's own `scale` property in isolation was wrong for
models with more than one root, e.g. P5 Project 1's own 2 roots) to
compute each model's TRUE world-space bounding box, exactly matching
what `THREE.Box3().setFromObject()` would report once loaded. Result:
6 of 8 models cluster tightly (maxDim 0.0632-0.2289) -- confirming the
user's own stated belief that their models ARE consistently scaled.
P5 Project 1 (maxDim 2.0000, suspiciously round -- likely a
normalization side effect of whatever decimation/re-export step
"downsized" it) and Samsung Galaxy S26 (maxDim 5.2747) are real,
MEASURED outliers, ~13x and ~30x larger than the cluster respectively.
Since `cfg.phoneModelScale` (300) is ONE global value tuned for the
consistent cluster, and P5 Project 1 is the default/first model in
the manifest, a fresh load renders it at 2.0 * 300 = 600 world units
-- roughly 5-17x the hand's own ~115-unit scale, fully explaining
"can't see it" (not missing, just enormous, likely with the camera
effectively inside or past it).

Fixed with a new optional `scale` field on `manifest.json` entries
(threaded through `manifestModelsToOptions()`/the hardcoded fallback
array, defaulting to 1 when absent -- including for every imported
model via the Item Selector, which has no measured data of its own
yet) and a new `currentPhoneModelScaleMultiplier`, set in
`loadPhoneModel()` by looking up the just-selected option's own
`.scale`, applied on top of `cfg.phoneModelScale` in
`applyPhoneModelTransform()`. P5 Project 1 set to 0.0834 and Samsung
Galaxy S26 to 0.0316 -- both bring their effective size to ~0.167,
matching the cluster average almost exactly; the other 6 models are
completely untouched (scale defaults to 1). Separately noted, not
acted on: Samsung Galaxy S26's own root node also carries a `[-1,-1,-1]`
scale (a full point-inversion) -- a real, measured oddity in that
specific file, unrelated to its SIZE and not something this fix
touches; flagged in case it explains a future report about that one
model looking mirrored/inside-out.

On the Item Selector question: re-read `renderPhoneModelItemSelector()`
directly and confirmed no bug -- a single click on a row immediately
sets `cfg.phoneModelFile`, calls `loadPhoneModel()` if Phone Model is
currently enabled, and re-renders with the new row highlighted --
identical behavior to the old `<select>`'s own `wireSelect()` handler,
not a regression. The confusion was very likely the SAME scale bug:
even a successful selection change would have looked like "nothing
happened" if the newly-selected model was also oversized/invisible, or
if Phone Model's own master On/Off checkbox was off.

Not independently verified against the real device/browser (standing
limitation) -- this fix rests on a direct, ground-truth GLB measurement
rather than live rendering, the same methodology that resolved the
2 previous Virtual Screen bugs this session. `index.html`'s cache-
buster bumped `?v=136` -> `?v=137`.

2026-09-29  -  (51st)

Direct report, same day as the 50th entry's per-model scale fix: "Um, I
currently see none of the models. I'm not sure why. I'm pretty sure
it's with the scaling thing. Um, the hand is scaled correctly, so
could you just scale everything to the hand instead?" -- a request for
a 3rd approach after the 50th entry's per-model manifest correction
apparently didn't resolve things. Started building an automatic
scale-to-hand-length computation (measure each model's own bounding
box via THREE.Box3().setFromObject() at load time, target the hand's
own real reference length, `8 * cfg.handScale` -- see
computeBaseScale()'s own formula), but the user interrupted mid-build
with 2 direct corrections:

1. "in the Blender models themselves, I have all the phones centered
   to the world space origin. So if you place it in world space, it
   should still show up, right?" -- a genuine question, answered
   directly: yes, structurally, but at native scale the models measure
   far smaller than the hand's own reference length, so "shows up" in
   principle doesn't mean "visible at a normal camera distance."
2. "No, I don't want you to override my personal settings, but make
   by default scale to one and placed at world origin." -- REVERTED
   both the 50th entry's per-model manifest.json `scale` field AND the
   in-progress automatic computation entirely. Any per-model or
   automatic scale adjustment silently changes what a given
   cfg.phoneModelScale slider value renders as -- exactly the kind of
   "override" this instruction rules out. cfg.phoneModelScale is the
   ONLY thing controlling scale again, uniformly, for every model.

Then, direct follow-up diagnosis: "Okay, and then in that case, it's
just a model scaling issue. The hand is probably in meters. So just
scale up all my phones by 100, but set that as default." -- changed
the default to 100 (a single global value, not a per-model
correction). Immediately after, a further request: "for the hand and
phone scale sliders... make the minimum 0.5 and the maximum 5" --
directly conflicted with a default of 100 (a slider can't display a
default outside its own min/max without clamping), surfaced via
`AskUserQuestion` rather than silently picking one side. Final
resolution, direct: "I wanted you to scale it up to 100 but on the
sliders that will read as one... 100 is one now." Implemented as a
plain, ALWAYS-applied `PHONE_MODEL_SCALE_BASE = 100` constant
(uniform for every model, matching the earlier "don't override my
personal settings" instruction just as much as a flat 100 did) --
`cfg.phoneModelScale` is now a small fine-tune multiplier on TOP of
that fixed base, 0.5-5 range, default 1 (= actual scale 100). Hand
Scale's own slider range widened to match (0.5-5, was 0.1-3 -- its
own default of 1.55 already fits, unchanged).

Separately, while checking the LIVE git-tracked settings file to see
whether an old saved `sliderPhoneModelScale` value would collide badly
with the new semantics (it didn't -- the saved value, 1.01, happened
to already be close to the new neutral default), found a REAL,
separate bug: `selectPhoneModelFile: null` in that same file despite
real Sync round-trips, meaning the Item Selector's own click-to-select
rows were NEVER actually persisted -- they're plain `<div>`s, never
registered with devPanel.js's generic capture/restore pipeline the
old `<select>` participated in automatically (every `addRow()` call
auto-registers). Every fresh page load was silently resetting to
`PHONE_MODEL_OPTIONS[0]` regardless of what the user had selected --
a second, real contributor to "it isn't working like before," not
just the scale issue. Fixed with a hidden, Sync-participating text
control (`hiddenPhoneModelFile`) mirroring the current selection,
reacting to both user clicks and external restores (Reset/Undo/a
Sync-load) the same way this file's other custom widgets already do.

Not independently verified against the real device/browser (standing
limitation). `index.html`'s cache-buster bumped `?v=137` -> `?v=138`.

2026-09-29  -  (52nd)

Direct report: "sitll broke... its not the rightsettings. My last saved
seetings had the hand turned off and the phone on" -- duplicate HAND
MODEL/PHONE MODEL groups in the dev panel, and restored settings not
matching what was actually last Synced. First attempt (schema-version
bump to clear stale localStorage) did not resolve it -- confirmed via
direct DOM read on the real deployed site that this was never a
localStorage issue: `applySectionOrder()` (devPanel.js) was read
line-by-line and can only ever relocate a SINGLE existing DOM section
by its unique data-sid, never spawn a duplicate. Root-caused via a real
console error on the live site: `renderPhoneModelItemSelector()` threw
`Cannot read properties of null (reading 'value')` at
`document.getElementById('hiddenPhoneModelFile')`, because the
detached `container` div it lived in hadn't been appended to the real
DOM yet. That uncaught throw aborted `ensureDevPanelBuilt()` before
`devPanelBuilt` was ever set true, so the next caller
(`loadRemoteSettingsOnStartup()`'s own explicit call) re-ran the WHOLE
build from scratch -- producing exactly 2 real copies of HAND
MODEL/PHONE MODEL and nothing after them (RECURSIVE RENDER, Camera,
Lighting, Toon Shading, Background, Ground Plane, Finger Gizmos all
missing on both attempts), which also explains the wrong-restored-
settings report (2 independent partial builds each applying Sync'd
values, the second one's duplicate elements winning). Fixed by
querying within the row itself instead of a global
`document.getElementById` lookup. Verified live end-to-end on the real
Vercel deployment: 11 unique top-level groups, zero duplicates, no
console errors on a fresh incognito load.

Also found and fixed while verifying: `Iphone17MaxPro.glb`/
`P5_Project_1.glb`/`Nothing2.glb` all failed to load --
`THREE.GLTFLoader: No DRACOLoader instance provided` /
`EXT_meshopt_compression` errors -- neither decoder had ever been
wired into either GLTFLoader instance (hand or phone). Added a shared
`DRACOLoader` + `MeshoptDecoder` to both loaders. Separately, a
DISCLOSED-but-unfixed race from earlier in this project's history
(`rebuildField()` throwing `Cannot read properties of null (reading
'clone')` if a Field Layout slider restore fires before the hand's own
async model load resolves) was confirmed reproducing on every normal
page load via the real console -- fixed with a simple `if
(!modelRoot) return` guard (the model-load callback already calls
`rebuildField()` itself once loading finishes, so nothing is lost).

`index.html` cache-buster: `?v=138` -> `?v=143` across this entry's
fixes; `devpanel/devPanel.js` `?v=7` -> `?v=8` (makeDevGroupToggleable()
widened from a direct-child selector to a plain descendant selector,
per the 53rd entry below).

2026-09-29  -  (53rd)

A run of small, direct-request features/fixes on RECURSIVE RENDER and
PHONE MODEL, same session:

- Screen Emission Intensity slider (0-5x) -- direct report "When i use
  the Pixel 9A model, the screen is very dim." `phoneScreenRenderMaterial`
  is an unlit MeshBasicMaterial with no real `.emissive` property;
  multiplies `.color` instead for the same practical brightening effect.
- RECURSIVE RENDER moved from a top-level dev-panel group into a
  subgroup of PHONE MODEL > RESPONSIVE BEHAVIOUR - PHONE, per direct
  request. Its own title-bar On/Off toggle previously required a
  top-level group (`makeDevGroupToggleable()`'s own direct-child DOM
  selector) -- rather than lose that feature, generalized the function
  itself to work at any nesting depth (same fix shape
  `findNestedGroupContent()` already got for a different lookup).
  `HANDYSET_SETTINGS_SCHEMA_VERSION` bumped (2026-09-29b) since this is
  a real group-nesting change; the matching git-tracked settings entry
  relocated via a scripted surgery, verified with a zero-drift 427-key
  multiset diff.
- Texture X/Y Offset sliders added alongside the existing Scale/
  Rotation controls (`texture.offset`, -1 to 1).
- "Per-Model Settings" -- direct request: "make sure all slider
  settings within the phone model group saves per model." Every
  control under PHONE MODEL (Scale/Offset, Responsive Rotation, and
  Recursive Render's own settings) is now captured under the
  previously-selected model's own key right before a switch and
  restored right after, persisted via a 2nd hidden JSON control
  alongside `hiddenPhoneModelFile`. CORRECTED same day, direct
  feedback ("every phone model is oriented the same way in regards to
  world XYZ. so when rotating the recursie rendering, the rotations
  should all be the same"): Phone Model Rotation X/Y/Z removed from
  the per-model list, back to one shared value across every model.
  Also fixed while building this: the restore path only dispatched an
  'input' event, but `wireCheckbox()` listens for 'change' -- every
  checkbox in the per-model list visually updated on a model switch
  but silently never actually changed its underlying `cfg` value.
  Fixed by dispatching both events.
- Root-caused a suspected "per-model settings git-sync bug" (direct
  question: "so its got to be a big git settings syncing issue") --
  confirmed via a REAL Sync click (not just calling
  `saveDevPanelSettings()` programmatically, which only writes
  localStorage; the remote POST is wired as a separate click listener
  on the actual Sync button) that the mechanism works correctly. The
  real explanation: only P5_Project_1 had ever been individually tuned
  under the old single-shared-value system, so it alone had real
  per-model data after the migration; every other model was just
  sitting at generic defaults, which is not a bug.
- `frustumCulled` fix -- direct report: "iphone max pro 3d model
  screen is invisible. im lookin through it. its not a rotaiton
  issue." Confirmed live (setting `frustumCulled=false` on the mesh
  made the screen render immediately) as the SAME bug class already
  fixed for the hand's own SkinnedMesh: a mesh's default bounding
  sphere is computed from raw local geometry and doesn't account for
  the whole model's Offset/Scale/Rotation transforms shifting it in
  world space, so three.js incorrectly concludes it's outside the
  camera frustum and skips rendering it -- exactly "looking through
  it." Applied to every phone screen mesh, not just this one model.
- Nothing2.glb's Screen Face mesh wasn't being found even after the
  Meshopt fix made the model load -- confirmed live via
  `window.__debug.phoneModelRaw` that the LOADED mesh name is
  "Screen_Face" (underscore) though the raw GLB JSON's own node name
  is "Screen Face" (space) -- something in the load pipeline
  sanitizes separators. `findPhoneScreenMeshes()`'s name matching now
  normalizes underscores/dots to spaces before comparing, extending
  the existing case-insensitive matching.
- All-caps-vs-title-case scale slider tuning: for all "*Scale"-labeled
  sliders (Pose/Model/Texture/Hand Scale, Phone Rotation Scale X/Y/Z),
  step widened from 0.05 towards finer-grained values per direct
  request ("for alll scale sliders allow me to go in any increment.
  dont round it to .05") -- in progress, interrupted by the mirror
  investigation below; not yet completed/shipped this session.

`index.html` cache-buster: `?v=143` -> `?v=152` across this entry.

2026-09-29  -  (54th)

Recursive Render's "every other screen mirrored" investigation --
UNRESOLVED at the end of this session, disclosed honestly rather than
claimed fixed. 6+ distinct fix attempts across this single investigation,
each shipped, tested live by the user, and reported still broken:

1. A pre-existing blind constant `texture.repeat.x = -1 * ...` (from
   before this session) -- direct report "im still getting the issue
   o the render mirroring every other screen" proved it insufficient.
2. Replaced with 2 direct checkboxes (Mirror Alternating X/Y),
   alternating on ODD-numbered render-loop pass indices.
3. CORRECTED same day: parity was counted from the render loop's own
   pass index, not from the VIEWER's own depth perspective (depth 1 =
   the texture on the actual Screen Face mesh, computed LAST in the
   loop) -- direct clarification: "it should mirror the first render
   first. then the 3rd etc." Re-derived as `(passIndex % 2) !==
   (levels % 2)`.
4. `texture.updateMatrix()` added, theorizing a lazy matrix-recompute
   timing issue across multiple `renderer.render()` calls per frame --
   direct report "it didint work" after live testing.
5. Removed `material.needsUpdate`/`texture.needsUpdate` (both force an
   unnecessarily heavy shader-recompile/re-upload cycle on every one of
   up to 10 passes per frame) -- direct report "and the last scale and
   mirror ting didnt work."
6. Direct instruction after 4 straight failures: "i want you to put
   the scale and mirror origin on the center of te SCREEN FACE mesh."
   Computed each model's REAL UV midpoint from its own geometry
   (Pixel 9A measured 0.498,0.500; not exactly the hardcoded 0.5,0.5)
   instead of assuming a centered UV island -- a theory that also
   explained the earlier "P5 looks fine, others don't" scale report.
   Direct report: "mirroring not fixed." Follow-up measurement directly
   DISPROVED this theory: Nothing2 (which the user confirmed "looks
   correct") measured UV midpoint (0.501, 0.500) -- essentially
   IDENTICAL off-centeredness to Pixel 9A's "wrong" result, ruling out
   UV centering as the differentiator.

Live isolation testing (AskUserQuestion, per this project's own
established pattern for a stuck bug) established real, hard facts:
- With Mirror X OFF entirely (zero manual mirroring code active), the
  ACTUAL observed sequence across 8 recursion depths was "XAXAXAXA" --
  a real, perfect alternation with NO contribution from this session's
  mirror-checkbox code at all, proving an inherent flip already baked
  into the recursive-capture pipeline before any of this session's
  code ever runs. The equivalent Y-axis baseline was "AAAAAAA" (clean,
  no inherent issue) -- X-axis only.
- Reverse-solving what the checkbox's OWN code must be contributing
  (comparing the Mirror-X-ON sequence "AAXXAAX" against that OFF
  baseline) produced "XAAXXAA" -- a repeating 4-depth pattern, NOT the
  clean 2-depth alternation directly proven correct via live console
  instrumentation earlier in this same investigation (attempt 3's own
  diagnostic log showed -1,1,-1,1,-1,1,-1,-1 exactly, every single
  frame). This is an unreconciled contradiction between "what the JS
  computes" (proven) and "what ends up on screen" (observed) -- a
  6th fix built on the wrong side of that contradiction was drafted,
  found to NOT correctly predict the user's own ON-sequence data
  before shipping, and deliberately reverted rather than shipped
  unverified.
- Also ruled out via direct measurement, NOT the differentiator between
  Nothing2 (works) and Pixel 9A/P5 (don't): UV aspect ratio, ancestor-
  chain geometry scale inversion (Pixel 9A has zero negative-scale
  components in its chain, same as Nothing2; Samsung Galaxy S26 DOES
  have a `[-1,-1,-1]` root inversion -- a real, separate,
  already-documented issue for that one model only, unrelated to this
  investigation), mesh flatness (both are genuinely flat), and
  geometry subdivision complexity (Pixel 9A: 76 triangles/80 vertices;
  Nothing2: 77/79 -- essentially identical).
- Direct report also established the base (non-recursive-compounding)
  issue isn't purely about mirroring either: with Mirror X OFF,
  baseline recursion on Pixel 9A/P5 already looks wrong; turning
  Mirror X ON makes MORE depths look correct (not fewer) on Pixel 9A
  specifically ("with it turned on, now 123 look goo and 4 does not"),
  the opposite of what a pure regression would look like.

Left in place, deliberately: a temporary diagnostic
(`window.__mirrorDebug = true` in console) logging real per-pass
passIndex/levels/alternateParity/mirrorX -- proven reliable where
monkey-patching `THREE.WebGLRenderer.prototype.render` was NOT (the
renderer instance shadows the prototype method; patches placed there
are silently never called, confirmed live -- do not rely on
prototype-patching for renderer instrumentation on this project again).
Next planned step, not yet started: check whether the Screen Face
mesh's own raw UV winding direction (not just its center/aspect) is
itself mirrored at the geometry level, independent of anything in the
rendering code.

`index.html` cache-buster: `?v=152` -> `?v=156` across this entry (the
6th attempt's UV-center code IS live/shipped, since it's a real,
independently-justified improvement even though it didn't resolve the
reported symptom; the drafted-then-reverted 7th attempt never shipped
and left no trace in the deployed code).

2026-09-29  -  (55th)

Recursive Render mirror investigation, continued from the 54th entry --
X-axis mirroring RESOLVED (verified exactly, not approximately) against
a full letter-sequence dataset; Y-axis and the X/Y interaction remain
open, with a concrete, evidence-based explanation for why.

7th attempt (shipped, per direct instruction "skip all that for now" /
"just fix it and push", despite a known unresolved contradiction):
added an UNCONDITIONAL per-pass X correction (`baseFlipX`, gated on
`alternateParity` alone, always active regardless of the checkbox) to
cancel the inherent `XAXAXAXA` baseline proven in the 54th entry's own
investigation. Disclosed honestly at the time, in both the code comment
and chat, that this formula did not correctly predict the user's own
ON-checkbox data when checked algebraically before shipping.

The user then provided the richest dataset of this entire investigation:
both X and Y mirror sequences, for all 4 checkbox combinations (nothing
/ X-only / Y-only / both), on 2 models (Pixel9A, Nothing2) -- 16 full
letter sequences in total, specifically requested and delivered as a
"once-and-for-all" test matrix.

Cross-checking this dataset directly disproved one assumption baked
into the code: `mirrorX`'s own JS formula has zero reference to
`cfg.screenMirrorAlternatingY`, yet the OBSERVED X sequence changes
depending on whether Y is also checked (confirmed on both models via
direct comparison of the "X checked/Y not" vs "both checked" columns).
Y's own sequence, by contrast, is byte-identical regardless of X's
checkbox state on both models -- consistent with the code, which never
references X inside the Y computation.

Solved algebraically for the true per-pass contribution needed to
reproduce every observed "X checked" data point: not a flat per-pass
+-1 constant (what the 7th attempt shipped), but an alternation by
VIEWER DEPTH (`d = levels - passIndex`, i.e. how many nested "screen
inside a screen" generations deep a given pass's captured image sits),
via `offBaseline(d) * (-1)^d`. Verified this is an EXACT match --
every single position, not just a plausible fit -- against both
models' full 8-9-depth "X checked" sequences. Root cause: each
recursion pass's rendered image gets embedded inside the NEXT pass's
capture (the phone screen shows the previous pass's texture), so a
per-pass flip compounds as a PRODUCT across nesting depth rather than
applying once per depth as previously assumed -- this is also the
likely source of the period-4-looking patterns seen throughout this
whole investigation despite the underlying per-pass JS signal being
proven period-2 via console instrumentation as far back as the 54th
entry's own diagnostic.

Shipped: `checkboxFlipX` changed from a flat `cfg.screenMirrorAlternatingX
? -1 : 1` to a depth-parity term (`viewerDepth % 2 === 1`), composed
with the EXISTING unconditional `baseFlipX` unchanged (deliberately --
changing it would have invalidated the "off baseline" the depth-parity
fix was solved against). Y's own formula was left untouched: it does
NOT fit the same depth-parity model against the available data, and is
suspected to be contaminated by X's own always-on background
correction being active during every "Y only" test capture (X was
never actually "doing nothing" in those tests, due to the unconditional
correction) -- not yet reconciled, deliberately not guessed a 4th time
without better-isolated data.

Direct user insight, independently arrived at, matching the mechanism
above: since the phone's screen content is asymmetric, X-mirror and
Y-mirror correctness aren't independently judgeable properties of a
frame -- two mirrors composed (X and Y both on) is a 180-degree
rotation, not two independent flips, and for asymmetric content a
rotation just looks like a different wrong image, which a human eye
naturally attributes to whichever axis is being judged at the time.
This means the "both checked" case looking wrong on some models may
not be a further bug to chase at all, once X alone (Y off) is
confirmed clean -- consistent with, and a good independent check on,
the compounding-through-recursion mechanism solved above.

NOT yet independently browser-verified against the real deployed site
(per this project's own "skip sandbox verification, go straight from
node --check + review to push" convention) -- the exact-match
verification above is against the user's own reported letter
sequences, algebraically, not a fresh live screenshot check.

`index.html` cache-buster: `?v=156` -> `?v=158` across this entry
(`?v=157` shipped the 7th attempt above, `baseFlipX`; `?v=158` is the
depth-parity fix).

2026-09-29  -  On-demand rendering (skip per-frame render/composer.render
when Responsive Rotation is off)

Direct request: "when Responsive Rotation is not turned on, make sure
that the 3js isnt still rendering everyframe. In that case, only
rerender when a setting has been modified." `animate()` previously
called `composer.render()` unconditionally on every RAF frame. Added a
`needsRender` flag + `requestRender()` (main.js, exposed as
`window.requestRender`): the whole per-frame compute block AND
`composer.render()` itself are now skipped unless Responsive Rotation
is active (`cfg.trackingEnabled` with a hand present -- Palm Rotation/
Reactive Arm Length/Wrist Splay/Base Arm Rotation/Pose Tween/Phone
Model Responsive Rotation all key off `tiltMagnitude`/
`armBaseDistanceT`, which only changes while this is on) or something
discrete flagged `needsRender`. `requestAnimationFrame`/
`controls.update()`/`syncCameraPanelFromLive()` still run every frame
regardless (cheap, and needed so OrbitControls damping's own 'change'
event -- the actual on-demand-render trigger for camera motion -- keeps
firing correctly).

Every discrete "something changed" path was hooked explicitly rather
than relying on one central mechanism, since this project has several
genuinely separate control-wiring systems: the 5 shared wire* functions
(wireSlider/wireCheckbox/wireColor/wireTextInput/wireSelect -- covers
the large majority of ~150 dev-panel controls project-wide), an
OrbitControls 'change' listener (drag/wheel/damping-glide), a window
resize handler, devPanel.js's `applyFullDevPanelState()` (Sync/Reset/
Undo/Redo/Named-State restore -- a safety net, since `applyControlValues()`
already dispatches real events for most of these), the 5 list-picker
Use buttons (apply a preset directly, bypassing wireSlider/wireCheckbox
entirely), the Set Hotkey button-trigger path (a hotkeyed button's own
click handler isn't necessarily one of the wire* functions), the Pause
toggle, an async phone-model load completing (covers Item Selector
clicks, a Sync-restored selection, and Import GLB uniformly, regardless
of which one triggered the load), and devPanel.js's own generic
curve-editor/range-bar control types (the 12 curve/range fields across
Reactive Arm Length/Wrist Splay/Base Arm Rotation/Pose Tween/Phone
Model Responsive Rotation/Recursive Render's new per-level scale curve
-- these carry their value via direct per-frame polling, never a
dispatched input/change event, so they needed their own explicit
`window.requestRender()` call inside their shared `commitPoints()`/
`commit()` functions).

Recursive Render (`cfg.screenRenderEnabled`) is deliberately NOT part
of the "always continuous" condition -- its render-to-texture pipeline
only ever reflects the current scene state, so with nothing else
changing frame-to-frame its own output wouldn't change either; it
still updates correctly on-demand via the same `needsRender` flag as
everything else.

NOT independently browser-verified (standing project convention) --
syntax-checked via `node --check` only, no live sandbox testing. The
two highest-risk spots worth a live spot-check: OrbitControls damping
actually continuing to glide smoothly for a few frames after a drag
release with Responsive Rotation off (rather than snapping to a stop),
and a curve-editor/range-bar drag actually producing a live-updating
graph while Responsive Rotation is off. `index.html` cache-buster:
`main.js` `?v=168` -> `?v=169`, `devPanel.js` `?v=11` -> `?v=12`.

2026-09-29  -  Stop the render loop entirely when static (not just skip
its work)

Direct follow-up on the same-day on-demand-rendering change above: that
version still called `requestAnimationFrame(animate)` unconditionally
every browser frame even when nothing was rendered -- it skipped the
expensive compute+render work but never stopped the RAF loop itself, so
the browser kept waking the tab up 60x/sec for nothing while idle.

Added `renderLoopRunning` + `startRenderLoop()`: `animate()` now only
re-schedules itself when Responsive Rotation is active or `needsRender`
is still set after that frame's work; otherwise the loop is allowed to
die. `requestRender()` is the single guarded restart point -- it flags
`needsRender` and, if the loop isn't currently running, calls
`startRenderLoop()`. OrbitControls' own pointer/wheel handlers dispatch
'change' independent of this loop (their internal handlers call
`update()` themselves as part of handling the input), so a drag or zoom
correctly wakes a fully-stopped loop; `controls.update()` inside
`animate()` then sustains the loop frame-to-frame through damping's own
decay until it settles and the loop stops again on its own. Split the
previous single `animate()` body into `animate()` (scheduling only) and
a new `renderOneFrame()` (the actual per-frame work, logic unchanged) for
clarity.

NOT independently browser-verified (standing project convention) --
verified via `node --check` and by tracing three.js's own OrbitControls
source for the pointer-handler-calls-update()-synchronously behavior
this relies on. Worth a live spot-check: dragging the camera after the
scene has gone idle should still glide/decay smoothly via damping, not
snap to a stop. `index.html` cache-buster: `main.js` `?v=169` -> `?v=170`.
