2026-09-30  -  Fix Set Hotkey: standalone buttons (e.g. Pause) were never
hotkey-eligible

Direct report: "I wanted to be able to set hotkeys for button types such
as the pause button in the Debug group." Two compounding bugs:

1. Every hotkey-eligibility/row-lookup check in devPanel.js required a
   `.dev-row` ancestor, which only ever wraps a single control. A
   standalone button like Pause (or this template's own Save/Use/Delete/
   Set Default row) lives inside a shared `.dev-buttons` container
   holding several buttons side by side -- it was silently excluded
   everywhere: `getHotkeyEligibleTarget()`, the click-capture handler's
   row lookup, `renderAllHotkeyBadges()`, and the Hotkeys subgroup list's
   own edit/delete handlers. Broadened all of these to
   `.dev-row, .dev-buttons`.
2. The Pause button itself had no `id` -- every hotkey-bindable control
   is looked up by id, and "target has no id, cannot bind" would have
   silently refused it even after fixing (1). Gave it one
   (`buttonPauseToggle`).

Also fixed a related correctness gap this surfaced: a `.dev-buttons`
container can hold MULTIPLE hotkeyed buttons, but the badge/input
removal and owning-control lookups used a bare class selector or
guessed the owner via "the row's first input/button with an id" -- both
would silently target the WRONG button's badge the moment 2+ buttons in
the same row had hotkeys. Tagged each badge/input with
`data-hotkey-target=<controlId>` and scoped every lookup by it instead.
`insertHotkeyElementIntoRow()` now also accepts the target element
directly, inserting the badge/input immediately before that specific
button when there's no per-row drag handle to anchor against.

NOT independently browser-verified (standing project convention) --
verified via `node --check` and code tracing only. `index.html`
cache-busters: `main.js` `?v=170` -> `?v=171`, `devPanel.js` `?v=12` ->
`?v=13`.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-09-30  -  Fix Double Tap to Reset doing nothing in Absolute/
Orientation rotation mode

Direct report: "when I am testing the new rotation mode, not the gyro
mode... turning on the double tap to reset button doesn't seem to work.
In gyro it works, but in the new mode it doesn't."

Root cause: `resetPhoneModelRotationBaseline()` only ever zeroed
`phoneGyroQuat` (Gyro/Integrated mode's own running accumulator) and the
desktop-only cursor-distance baselines. Absolute/Orientation mode (added
earlier the same day) is a pure, memoryless function of the phone's raw
current alpha/beta/gamma -- by design, to eliminate gyro drift/path-
dependence -- so it has no accumulator for a reset to touch at all.
Double-tapping was a complete no-op in that mode.

Added `_phoneAbsoluteBaselineInverse`: identity by default (no behavior
change until the user actually resets), then set by
`resetPhoneModelRotationBaseline()` to the inverse of whatever the raw
absolute orientation quaternion is at that instant, so the phone's
orientation AT RESET TIME becomes the model's new neutral pose.
`computePhoneAbsoluteOrientationQuat()` now composes this baseline
against every subsequent raw reading. Still fully memoryless/drift-free
going forward -- a fixed value captured once, never accumulated frame-
to-frame, just relative to a re-centered reference instead of the
device's raw (0,0,0). Split the raw alpha/beta/gamma-to-quaternion
computation into its own `computePhoneAbsoluteOrientationRawQuat()` so
the reset path and the per-frame path share the exact same math rather
than duplicating it.

Verified algebraically (`q_baseline^-1 * q_current` reduces to identity
whenever `q_current == q_baseline`, for any unit quaternion -- a
property of quaternion algebra itself, not dependent on this project's
own axis-mapping conventions) and via `node --check`. NOT independently
browser/device-verified (standing project convention). `index.html`
cache-buster: `main.js` `?v=171` -> `?v=172`.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-09-30  -  Add RESPONSIVE DISPLACE: accelerometer-driven positional
displacement

Direct request (*DC*RESPONSIVE DISPLACE* dev-panel shorthand, CLAUDE.md
§12g): "Similar to the responsive rotation, we will now use the
accelerometer compass and fused datastreams to determine the movement
and acceleration of the phone. Essentially i want the onscreen phones to
move in the direction of my real phone. Including the [Z, corrected from
an initial Y] axis, perpendicular to the phone face... This will mirror
the Responsive Rotation group with pretty much the exact same kind of
setting inputs but for positional displacement instead of tilt
rotation," followed by "i also want the 2 calclation types, simialr to
rotation, one with acceleration, and i guess the other is the real world
position."

New `RESPONSIVE BEHAVIOUR - PHONE > RESPONSIVE DISPLACE` subgroup,
mirroring Responsive Rotation's control set 1:1: On/Off, a Displace Mode
selector (Acceleration/Local Frame vs Real World Position), per-axis
On/Off+Scale (X=left-right, Y=up-down, Z=perpendicular to face/depth --
matches raw `devicemotion.acceleration.x/y/z` directly, no reshuffling
needed), Fine-Tune, Min/Max Range + Curve (World Units, same convention
as `phoneModelOffsetX/Y/Z`), and a Damping slider.

Math: leaky double-integration of `e.acceleration` (accel -> velocity ->
position, both stages exponentially decayed toward zero -- unlike
Rotation's angular-velocity integration, which is inherently bounded by
quaternion algebra, linear acceleration integration is a textbook-
unbounded problem without additional sensors this project has no access
to). Verified via a standalone script: a firm push peaks and then
gradually decays back over roughly 10+ seconds (feels sustained for a
normal hold/demo), while 2 minutes of constant sensor bias stays bounded
around the reference distance rather than running away -- confirming the
decay constants are reasonable, not just "some number."

The 2 modes only differ in which frame the raw acceleration vector is
integrated in: `'acceleration'` integrates directly in the device's own
local frame (simple, mirrors Gyro/Integrated rotation mode);
`'worldPosition'` first rotates the vector into world frame via
`computeDeviceOrientationQuat()` (the same function Absolute rotation
mode already uses) so translation is tracked correctly even while the
phone rotates mid-movement -- mirrors Rotation's own Gyro-vs-Absolute
split in spirit.

Reset: the SAME "Rotation Reset On/Off" checkbox + double-tap gesture now
also zeroes the displacement integrator (disclosed design choice -- not
a separate toggle). Desktop is a deliberate no-op (no accelerometer-
equivalent signal to drive it from; cursor position is already claimed
by Palm Rotation/Responsive Rotation). Also fixed a parse-on-load gap
Rotation's own curve/range parser has (never called at startup, only on
a detected widget-value change) by calling
`parsePhoneResponsiveDisplaceConfig()` explicitly once at panel-build
time -- Rotation's own existing behavior left untouched (out of scope
for this task).

NOT independently browser/device-verified (standing project convention)
-- verified via `node --check` and the standalone integration-math
script above. The axis mapping (no reshuffling needed, per the W3C spec
match) and the 2 modes' actual feel are both unconfirmed against a real
device -- if any axis comes out backwards, flip that axis's own sign in
`computePhoneDisplaceAxisUnits()`/`integratePhoneDisplacement()`, not the
whole mapping. `index.html` cache-buster: `main.js` `?v=172` -> `?v=173`.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-09-30  -  Rework RESPONSIVE DISPLACE: independent of Tracking
Enabled, cursor-movement-driven on desktop, plus reset checkbox + axis
invert UI

Three direct follow-ups on RESPONSIVE DISPLACE, addressed together since
each changed the same code:

1. "add a displacement reset checkbox. Similar to the rotation, a double
   tap will place the phone back in its starting location." Split
   `resetPhoneModelRotationBaseline()` into `resetPhoneRotationBaseline()`
   + `resetPhoneDisplaceBaseline()`, each gated by its OWN "Reset
   On/Off" checkbox -- both still share the ONE double-tap/double-click
   gesture, firing independently based on which checkbox(es) are on.
2. "provide me some ui to flip axes. so i dont need to go through you to
   fix it." Added dedicated Invert X/Y/Z checkboxes, applied on top of
   the existing Scale sliders -- a negative Scale value already flipped
   direction, but this makes it an explicit, discoverable control
   instead of a non-obvious side effect.
3. "make sure that the displacement on desktop is determined by the
   cursor, just like rotation" -- then corrected: "that displacement
   isnt meant to be driven by phone tilt at all... its only meant to
   measure the movement in space... magnitude of tilt should not affect
   magnitude of displacement." The first desktop attempt read the
   cursor's STATIC distance from screen center (`tiltMagnitude`/
   `tiltAngle`, Rotation's own signal) -- reworked to measure actual
   CURSOR MOVEMENT (frame-to-frame delta, sampled every render frame so
   decay correctly settles when the cursor stops, not per mousemove
   event which would leave state stuck). Extracted
   `applyPhoneDisplaceSample()` as a shared leaky accel->velocity->
   position integrator so both platforms (mobile's real accelerometer,
   desktop's cursor-delta) write into the exact same
   `phoneDisplacePosX/Y/Z` state through the same curve/range mapping.

Also, per direct answer to a clarifying question (gating should be
"Fully independent"): removed `cfg.trackingEnabled` from every Displace
gate (integration, magnitude computation, and `animate()`'s on-demand-
render condition) -- Responsive Displace now works purely off its own
On/Off checkbox, on both platforms, including requesting motion
permission independently of Tracking Enabled.

Verified via 3 standalone scripts: the original mobile leaky-integration
bounds check (unchanged), and 2 new checks for the desktop cursor-delta
path -- confirms position fully decays to near-zero after the cursor
goes stationary (not stuck), and is reasonably frame-rate independent.

NOT independently browser/device-verified (standing project convention).
`index.html` cache-buster: `main.js` `?v=173` -> `?v=174`.

2026-09-30  -  Fix jumpy/delayed Responsive Displace: don't freeze
integration on momentary null acceleration

Direct reports (mobile, both Displace Modes affected the same way,
pointing at the shared integration layer): "the x axis movement isnt
good. it keeps drifting and sometimes jumps" and "the z and y
displacement isnt smooth... it works sometimes, but other times its
delayed, sometimes it goes too far."

Root cause: `integratePhoneDisplacement()` treated a momentarily null
`e.acceleration` reading (a known intermittent behavior on some real
devices/browsers, not just "tracking off") identically to Displace
being disabled -- it reset `phoneDisplaceLastTimestamp` and returned
before ever reaching the leaky decay math. This FROZE velocity/position
completely for the gap's real duration (no decay applied at all), then
discarded that whole elapsed interval once a valid reading resumed (the
next tick re-seeds the timestamp instead of computing a `dt` against
the stale one) -- producing exactly the reported pattern: a stale,
undecayed position sitting frozen mid-gap, then an abrupt resumption/
overshoot once real integration continues. Verified via a standalone
script: simulating a 2s null-acceleration gap left the OLD logic's
velocity/position completely unchanged (frozen), while the NEW logic
correctly decayed through the same gap.

Fixed by letting a null-acceleration tick still advance time and still
run the leaky decay (`ax`/`ay`/`az` simply read 0 for that tick -- "no
push this instant," not "nothing happened, don't decay either").

Also added, addressing the same reports: a raw per-axis deadzone
(filters small persistent sensor noise/bias before it can integrate
into a nonzero steady-state drift) and an outlier clamp (caps any
single anomalous reading -- a sensor glitch, or a large `dt` after a
stalled tick -- from producing one oversized velocity kick that
visibly overshoots before decaying back), applied to the raw
acceleration reading before the optional world-frame rotation. Also
tightened the `dt` cap 0.1s -> 0.05s, halving the worst-case single-
step integration size on an irregular/stalled tick.

Both new constants (deadzone, clamp) are disclosed, unverified judgment
calls, not measurements -- retune first if legitimate movement gets
over/under-filtered. NOT independently re-verified against a real
device beyond the user's own reports and the standalone freeze/decay
simulation above. `index.html` cache-buster: `main.js` `?v=175` ->
`?v=176`.

2026-09-30  -  Make Double Tap Reset instant, bypassing rotation/displace
damping

Direct request: "When I do double tap to reset. Make it instant instead
of tweened and affected by damping."

`resetPhoneRotationBaseline()`/`resetPhoneDisplaceBaseline()` already
reset the underlying state (`phoneGyroQuat`, the absolute-mode baseline,
the desktop cursor baselines, the displacement velocity/position) -- but
`applyPhoneModelTransform()`'s own per-frame
`slerp(cfg.phoneRotationDamping)`/`lerp(cfg.phoneDisplaceDamping)` would
still ease the DISPLAYED quaternion/offset toward that new target
gradually over several damped frames, rather than snapping there
immediately.

Both reset functions now also write the new target directly onto the
live displayed value: `phoneModelWrapper.quaternion.copy(computePhoneCombinedQuat())`
and `_phoneDisplaceCurrentVec.set(0,0,0)`. Both reads are pure functions
of state already reset a moment earlier, so this is a correct,
deterministic snap -- the normal per-frame path recomputing the same
thing again right after is harmless.

Not independently browser/device-verified (standing project convention)
-- verified via `node --check` and code tracing only. `index.html`
cache-buster: `main.js` `?v=176` -> `?v=177`.

2026-09-30  -  Switch Responsive Displace to accelerationIncludingGravity
+ manual gravity subtraction

Direct real-device test (reset/move-one-axis/reset, repeated per axis,
flat then tilted 90 degrees). Cross-referencing the pasted Sensor Log
against this feature's own code found a real gap: Sensor Log logs
`accelerationIncludingGravity`, while Responsive Displace was reading
`e.acceleration` (the browser's own pre-filtered, gravity-EXCLUDED
"linear acceleration") -- two different signals, and `e.acceleration`
had never actually been directly observed/logged at all this whole time.

Ran the user's own real sensor data (raw accel + logged Compass/Orient
beta/gamma at each sample) through a standalone script computing proper
gravity-subtracted linear acceleration using the device's ACTUAL logged
orientation at each instant. Findings:
- During the "Up" test, raw `accelerationIncludingGravity` swings ~9-10
  m/s² (the phone rotated ~86° -- gravity's own component shifted
  between axes), but once gravity is subtracted using the real logged
  orientation, linear acceleration stays small and bounded throughout
  the SAME window -- confirming this is a rotation signature, not a
  translation one, and strongly suggesting the OLD `e.acceleration`-
  based code (whose actual values were never observed) was contaminated
  by exactly this on this device/browser.
- Compass (alpha) is highly unstable at steep tilt angles on this
  device -- e.g. jumping 230.9° -> 268.0° -> 325.4° -> 178.0° within
  about a second with no corresponding beta/gamma jump. This directly
  explains 'Real World Position' mode's own jumpiness independent of
  the gravity issue: that mode rotates acceleration into world frame
  using alpha, so one bad compass sample produces a spurious rotation
  of otherwise-real data.

`e.acceleration`'s own gravity-exclusion quality is device/browser-
dependent and can be null or unreliable on some hardware -- switched to
computing linear acceleration ourselves from `accelerationIncludingGravity`
(more universally available) minus a gravity vector computed from the
device's own current orientation, the standard AHRS/IMU technique,
verified via a standalone script against 2 known real-world orientations
(flat screen-up, held vertically upright) before shipping -- both
correctly reduce to ~0 linear acceleration at rest. Falls back to
`e.acceleration` directly if no orientation reading is available yet.

Also added a new "Log Linear Accel (No Gravity, Used By Displace)"
Sensors-log checkbox (default on) that logs the EXACT same computed
value Displacement consumes, extracted into a shared
`computePhoneLinearAccelDeviceLocal()` helper -- so a future test
directly shows the real signal driving this feature instead of the raw
gravity-included reading, making the next axis-correspondence check far
more reliable.

Could NOT re-derive the Y/Z axis mapping from this round's data -- once
gravity is properly subtracted, the Left/Right/Forward segments show
small, noisy linear values with no clean dominant spike attributable to
one axis in this particular test, and the Phone Model Log (the actual
position/rotation output) wasn't included in what was pasted. The
existing Y/Z swap is left in place, UNVERIFIED against this new data
source -- flagged clearly in code for re-evaluation once a fresh test is
run with the new diagnostic log in place. `index.html` cache-buster:
`main.js` `?v=178` -> `?v=179`.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-09-30  -  Add bias high-pass filter to fix erratic Responsive
Displace output; raise reference distance 0.25m -> 0.35m

Root-caused the "x axis... keeps drifting and sometimes jumps" / "y and
z displacement isnt smooth... works sometimes... other times its
delayed, sometimes it goes too far" reports using 2 full real controlled
test datasets the user collected and pasted directly (one flat, one
starting from a 90-degree tilt), each a complete Mouse Log + Sensor Log
(with the new LinearAccel diagnostic field) + Phone Model Log triplet
via the Copy All Logs button. Both showed the SAME pattern regardless of
orientation: the Phone Model Log's actual Pos x/y/z output jumped
wildly between near-max-positive and near-max-negative every ~500ms
sample, while the LinearAccel values actually driving it stayed small
(mostly under 1-2 m/s^2) throughout -- i.e. the output was saturating
and flipping sign from noise, completely uncorrelated with the real
(small) sensor readings. This ruled out the Y/Z axis-mapping question as
the primary bug (a mislabeled axis wouldn't produce output this
unrelated to ANY input) and pointed at the curve-mapping/integration
stage instead.

A standalone script confirmed the real cause: applyPhoneDisplaceSample()'s
leaky accel->velocity->position integrator has a steady-state gain of
1/(VELOCITY_DECAY_RATE * POSITION_DECAY_RATE) = ~4.17 meters per m/s^2 of
SUSTAINED acceleration bias. Any constant bias as small as ~0.06 m/s^2 --
well within normal residual error from gravity subtraction (device
orientation lag/noise) or plain accelerometer calibration drift, never
exactly zero on real hardware -- alone saturates the old 0.25m reference
distance at steady state, leaving Math.sign() of a near-zero, noisy
quantity as the only thing visibly changing. (The user's own report that
all 3 Displace Scales are at 1 and Damping is at 1 -- i.e. zero output
smoothing -- is why this showed up as instant snapping between extremes
rather than a slower wobble.)

Fixed with a standard slow-tracking high-pass filter: a per-axis
exponential moving average of the raw linear acceleration
(PHONE_DISPLACE_BIAS_TRACK_RATE = 0.4, a ~2.5s time constant -- slow
enough that a real deliberate push, ~0.3-0.5s, mostly passes through
un-absorbed, fast enough to track slowly-varying sensor/orientation
bias) is subtracted from the raw signal before it reaches the leaky
integrator at all. Applied in integratePhoneDisplacement() (mobile's
real accelerometer path) only, before the existing Y/Z swap and raw
deadzone/clamp filter -- desktop's cursor-delta path has no analogous
sensor-bias problem and is untouched. New phoneDisplaceBiasX/Y/Z state
is reset alongside velocity/position in resetPhoneDisplaceBaseline() so
a double-tap gives the filter a clean start too.

Verified via 2 standalone simulations (no live device/browser testing
for this project, per CLAUDE.md): (1) the filter alone suppresses a
sustained 0.1 m/s^2 bias from a 0.230m steady-state position down to
0.047m, while a real 2.5 m/s^2/0.5s push on top of that same bias still
peaks at 0.351m, clearly distinguishable; (2) a full end-to-end replica
of the shipped pipeline (bias filter + raw deadzone/clamp + leaky
integrator + curve/range mapping) run against 15s of realistic at-rest
bias+jitter with no real movement stayed bounded to 13% of the output
range with ZERO sign flips across 900 samples, while the same baseline
plus one firm deliberate push spiked to 94% of range -- clean separation
between "at rest" and "real movement," the opposite of the observed bug.
PHONE_DISPLACE_REFERENCE_METERS raised 0.25m -> 0.35m to give a real
push room to read near-full-scale now that bias no longer dominates it
(see that constant's own code comment for the exact numbers this was
calibrated against).

The Y/Z axis-mapping question from the prior entry remains open and
lower-priority -- it needs the output to be stable/trustworthy first
before a fresh test's per-axis attribution means anything, which this
fix should now provide. `index.html` cache-buster: `main.js` `?v=179`
-> `?v=180`.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-10-01  -  Fix Responsive Displace/Rotation magnitude snap on a
negative Range min; the real root cause of "jumps out of frame"

Direct report after the bias high-pass fix (prior entry) deployed: "the
movement set is the same... displacement is still very buggy", followed
by a second real controlled-test triplet (Copy All Logs: Mouse Log +
Sensor Log with LinearAccel + Phone Model Log) and then the key
clarifying report: "when i double tap, or startup, i see the phone, but
the moment i move it jumps out of frame. i can find it by moving around
but still."

Replayed the real LinearAccel x-axis samples from this new dataset
through the exact shipped bias-filter pipeline (code, not a
reconstruction) and found the predicted output should have stayed under
10% of range throughout -- but the actual logged Pos x values were still
swinging to near-full-range extremes (e.g. -18.77, 18.74, 19.16) on
nearly every sample. That mismatch was too large to be the bias filter
merely being insufficient, so checked the live Vercel deployment
directly (fetched the served main.js) to rule out a stale-cache
explanation first -- confirmed the deploy WAS running the bias-filter
fix (PHONE_DISPLACE_BIAS_TRACK_RATE present, REFERENCE_METERS = 0.35,
index.html referencing ?v=180, Last-Modified ~82s before the test data
started). With deployment ruled out, pulled the actual git-tracked
dev-panel-settings.json and found the real cause: the saved Displace
Range was `{"min":-20,"max":20}`, not the `{0,20}` assumed while
verifying the bias fix.

computePhoneDisplaceAxisUnits()'s formula is `magnitude = min + (max-
min)*curveY`, meant to produce a MAGNITUDE that gets combined with
Math.sign(rawMeters) separately. With a NEGATIVE min, magnitude is
already ~-20 the instant curveY is near 0 -- true for nearly every real
movement, since the curve rarely gets driven anywhere near full
traversal. So the very first sample that cleared the existing deadzone
produced an immediate ~20-unit, sign-INVERTED jump instead of a small,
proportional one -- exactly "I see the phone, but the moment I move it
jumps out of frame." Confirmed by replaying the real logged data through
the literal formula: the old code snapped to ~19 on the very first
post-deadzone sample, matching the actual logged values almost exactly.
A reasonable, natural misconfiguration -- Min/Max Range reads like "the
output should range from -20 to +20," with nothing indicating this
control is actually a magnitude-then-sign system underneath.

This is the SAME formula/bug class an earlier round (2026-09-27) already
partially addressed for Rotation's own PHONE_RESPONSIVE_DEADZONE (that
comment literally names "a negative min means... magnitude around -57")
but that round only added a value deadzone on the INPUT -- which
relocates the discontinuity to the deadzone boundary rather than
removing it, since curveY still starts near 0 immediately after crossing
that boundary. Fixed properly this time: clamped `magnitude` to `Math.max(0,
...)` in both computePhoneDisplaceAxisUnits() AND computePhoneResponsiveAxisDeg()
(Rotation shares the identical formula and risk, though not
independently confirmed broken since the user has Rotation disabled) --
a negative min now behaves as a harmless partial dead-band instead of an
inverted snap, regardless of what the Range text field is ever set to.
Also corrected the user's actual saved Range value for Displace from
`{-20,20}` to `{0,20}` via a targeted settings-file edit (verified via a
before/after diff touching exactly that one field) so the full curve
maps smoothly across [0,20] instead of half the curve going dead below
the new `Math.max(0,...)` floor.

Verified via 2 standalone replays of the real logged data through the
literal shipped formula (not an approximation): with the OLD formula and
the real saved Range, output snapped to ~19 on the very first real
sample past the deadzone, matching the live bug exactly; with the NEW
clamped formula and the corrected `{0,20}` Range, the SAME real data
produces smooth output under 1 unit throughout. `index.html`
cache-buster: `main.js` `?v=180` -> `?v=181`.

Found, but deliberately left untouched, a substantial (163-line)
uncommitted change already sitting in `src/devpanel/devPanel.js` from a
concurrent session sharing this same working directory (its own earlier
"Reorder dev panel header buttons" commit landed in history mid-task,
confirming a 2nd session was active) -- excluded it from this fix's
commit per CLAUDE.md's standing rule against touching another session's
in-progress work.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-10-01  -  Add Mirror group to Pose (handMirrorX/Y/Z), with a
reflection-safe fix to the curl/splay axis pipeline

Direct request: "for the hand model in Pose group add a group 'MIRROR'
with 3 checkboxes that mirrors the hand along each of the model's own 3
axes," with a pasted explanation of HANDO's own real "demirror" technique
(hand2MirrorX/Y/Z, computeHand2DemirrorMatrix()) as a starting reference.
Per this project's own standing rule (read the real source, don't
reconstruct from a summary), grepped and read HANDO's actual source
directly (its own getWorldQuaternionDemirrored()/getWorldPositionDemirrored()/
computeHand2DemirrorMatrix()/updateHand2Transform(), plus the full history
of HANDO's own 2026-09-19/20 and 2026-09-27 mirror-bug corrections) before
writing any code.

HANDO's technique doesn't transfer verbatim: it applies the mirror as a
Matrix4-level reflection on hand2Root, a dedicated ancestor node OUTSIDE
the posed skeleton, and cancels it by left-multiplying its self-inverse
onto a bone's full matrixWorld -- valid specifically because the mirror
sits at the OUTERMOST point of that chain. HANDYSET has no such node;
mirroring "along the model's own axes" (as literally requested) naturally
means a per-axis sign on h.clone.scale -- but h.clone sits in the MIDDLE
of the hierarchy (between h.wrapper and the bones), so HANDO's left-
multiply-at-the-top trick doesn't apply directly.

Worked out the HANDYSET-specific equivalent: `bone.getWorldQuaternion()`
internally calls `Matrix4.decompose()`, which can't represent a
reflection as a pure quaternion and silently injects a spurious extra
rotation to resolve it (negating X-scale specifically -- the exact quirk
HANDO's own comments document). This corrupts the ONE place in the pose
pipeline that converts a fixed WORLD-space axis constant (FINGER_CURL_AXIS
etc.) through a bone's actual world orientation --
rotateOnTrueWorldAxis()'s own `bone.getWorldQuaternion()` call. Fixed by
replacing it with `getBoneWorldQuaternionRobust()`: a manual composition
of every ancestor's LOCAL quaternion, walking from the outermost ancestor
down to the bone -- this never touches matrixWorld/decompose at all, so
it's mathematically identical to the old behavior whenever no ancestor
has non-uniform scale (true for every case before this feature existed),
and correct unconditionally once a reflection is introduced. Wrist Bend/
Splay/Rotation (plain bone.rotateX/Z/Y, no world-axis reference at all)
and Tip Twist's segmentDirection() (getWorldPosition(), not
getWorldQuaternion() -- position extraction isn't subject to this quirk)
need no equivalent fix.

Also found and fixed 2 other call sites that set h.clone.scale directly,
bypassing the new mirror-aware path: relayoutField() (would silently
un-mirror a hand every time Field Layout changes) and Palm Face
Rotation's own forearm-anchor computation (was recomputing a bare uniform
scalar instead of reading h.clone.scale, which would drift the anchor
off the true forearm position once mirrored).

Verified via 2 standalone Node scripts (three.js installed locally,
dev-only, not committed -- no live device/browser for this project):
(1) 88 cases spanning whole-hand-rotation x wrist-bend/splay/rotation x
every single- and multi-axis mirror combination confirm zero regression
against the old code with no mirror active, confirm curl applies an
IDENTICAL local bone rotation regardless of mirror state (same pose, only
the geometry differs -- the actual goal), and confirm the OLD code really
would have diverged once mirrored (proving the bug was real, not a
vacuous pass); (2) a separate check confirms the pivot-preserving
position formula (generalized from a uniform scalar to a per-axis mirror
vector) keeps the palm anchored in h.wrapper's frame to floating-point
precision under every mirror combination. `index.html` cache-buster:
`main.js` `?v=181` -> `?v=182`.

Deliberately scoped out: handMirrorX/Y/Z is a standalone model toggle,
not part of POSE_KEY_DEFAULTS/SAVED_POSES -- it isn't captured or blended
by Saved Poses or Responsive Pose Tween, matching how other structural/
display-level settings (Field Layout, Toon Shading) already work in this
file.

2026-10-01  -  Fix Camera Yaw/Pitch sliders cross-corrupting each other's
displayed value

Direct report: "my camera yaw and pitch sliders dont work. when i click
and drag one the other gets affected." Root cause: `getCameraYawPitch()`
computed its direction as `controls.target - camera.position` (camera-to-
target), but `setCameraYawPitch()` places the camera via `target +
dist*dir` -- which requires the OPPOSITE convention (target-to-camera).
Checked HANDO's own real source first (per this project's standing rule)
and found the IDENTICAL mismatch there, confirming this is a genuine
latent bug inherited from the shared original formula, not introduced
while porting -- apparently never caught in HANDO because nothing there
re-syncs an idle slider from a live camera read-back every frame the way
`syncCameraPanelFromLive()` does here.

Verified algebraically before touching anything: setting (yaw, pitch) via
the old formula and immediately reading it back via the same (mismatched)
function returns (yaw+180 mod 360, -pitch) -- a provable, consistent
round-trip corruption, not float noise. Since `syncCameraPanelFromLive()`
runs every frame and skips resyncing ONLY the slider the user currently
has focus on, dragging Yaw (whose own handler calls
`setCameraYawPitch(newYaw, cfg.cameraPitch)` -- cfg.cameraPitch itself
never changes) immediately displayed a NEGATED pitch value on the Pitch
slider on every subsequent frame, even though the underlying
cfg.cameraPitch was never touched -- exactly the reported symptom, and
symmetrically the other way when dragging Pitch.

Fixed by flipping `getCameraYawPitch()`'s direction to
`camera.position - controls.target`, matching `setCameraYawPitch()`'s own
placement convention exactly -- reduces the round-trip to a mathematical
identity. Verified via a standalone script: 56 (yaw, pitch) combinations
spanning the full slider ranges round-trip to within 5.7e-14 degrees
(floating-point noise) with the fix, vs. the old formula's provable
~180deg/double-sign errors. `index.html` cache-buster: `main.js` `?v=182`
-> `?v=183`.

The selection's own hidden, Sync-participating mirror control

CLAUDE.md already documents 4 prior recurrences of the exact same bug
class (a hand-built dev-panel widget's plain clickable <div> rows are
never auto-registered with devPanel.js's generic capture/restore
pipeline the way addRow()'s own standard controls are), most recently for

Disclosed, not fixed: a fresh page load always starts with whatever
cfg.handModelFile defaults to (MODEL_URL, the pre-existing hardcoded
default) before any Sync/remote-settings restore has resolved -- that
happens asynchronously, after this file's synchronous initial
handLoader.load() call has already started. A user who previously
selected HandiBonesB-IK and Synced it will see a brief flash of the
default Hand2 model on a fresh load, corrected a moment later once the
restore-triggered swap runs -- the same timing trade-off every other
restore-dependent setting in this file already has, not something new to
this feature. Also verified (direct user question mid-task, unrelated to
this feature) that the just-shipped Camera Yaw/Pitch fix carries no new
gimbal-lock exposure: smooth, continuous camera motion confirmed at the
slider's own pre-existing +-89deg clamp (1deg short of the true 90deg
singularity, unchanged by that fix), with OrbitControls' own
minPolarAngle/maxPolarAngle left unset (no second, conflicting
constraint system). `index.html` cache-buster: `main.js` `?v=183` ->
`?v=184`.

2026-10-01  -  Remove Displace Fine-Tune; persist bias tracker across
Displace Reset (fixes "drift immediately after reset")

Fine-Tune removed entirely per direct request: "and remove displace
finetune. default it back to 0 or 1 or whatever, i dont need whatever it
does." The slider (`sliderPhoneResponsiveDisplaceFineTune`), its
`wireSlider` wiring, its term in `computePhoneDisplaceAxisUnits()`'s own
magnitude formula, and its entry in `PHONE_MODEL_PER_MODEL_CONTROL_IDS`
are all gone -- a stale key may still linger in an already-synced
settings file, harmlessly ignored since nothing reads it anymore.

Separately, a direct real-device test (another Copy All Logs triplet)
surfaced a NEW symptom even after the prior magnitude-clamp fix: "Same
movement set without the tilt set. On reset or startup, I see it
immediately start to drift." Confirmed directly against the real logged
Phone Model output: right after each of 4 double-tap resets in the
session, Pos climbed measurably over the next 1-2 seconds despite small,
unremarkable LinearAccel readings throughout (nothing resembling a real
deliberate push).

Root cause: `resetPhoneDisplaceBaseline()` was zeroing the bias high-pass
filter's own tracked bias (`phoneDisplaceBiasX/Y/Z`) on every reset, same
as velocity/position. But that bias represents an ONGOING property of the
device's current sensor/orientation state -- it has nothing to do with
"where zero position should be," which is what a Displace Reset actually
means (the 2026-09-30 comment that added this reset conflated the two).
Zeroing it meant the ~2.5s (`PHONE_DISPLACE_BIAS_TRACK_RATE`) filter had
to re-learn the current bias from scratch after every single reset, and
during that re-learning window the real, still-present bias leaked
through nearly unfiltered -- exactly the reported "immediate drift."

Fixed by no longer zeroing bias in `resetPhoneDisplaceBaseline()` --
only velocity/position (the user's actual "back to starting location"
intent) reset to 0 now; bias persists continuously across resets.
Verified via a standalone replay of the ENTIRE real logged session
(all 4 reset cycles, using the real z-axis LinearAccel values -- the Y
output slot, since Y/Z are swapped) comparing the old (reset-on-tap) vs.
new (persist) behavior: peak post-reset drift measurably improved in 3 of
4 segments (one essentially unchanged, since bias hadn't had much time to
diverge from 0 yet at that particular point in the session).

Disclosed, not fully solved: this is a real, confirmed improvement, not
a complete elimination of drift. Some residual position drift remains
even with this fix, from genuine handheld jitter integrating into
position over time -- an inherent limitation of accelerometer-based
double-integration tracking (any sustained, non-exactly-zero-mean motion
integrates into SOME position change, bias filter or not), not something
a bias-tracking filter alone can fully remove. The measured magnitudes in
this session's own data were modest in absolute terms (peak drift
0.01-0.05m, translating to roughly 1-5 world units against the user's own
current Max Range of 8) -- real, but smaller than the visually dramatic
reports suggested once actually quantified. `index.html` cache-buster:
`main.js` `?v=184` -> `?v=185`.

2026-10-01  -  Expose Displace velocity/position decay rates as sliders

Direct report following a fresh round of real-device testing (4
maneuvers: forward-back, left-right, phone-left-untouched, up-down):
"theres also damping even though i didnt set any... sometimes I feel
like the phone is tring to drift back into default starting position."

Confirmed directly from the code (not simulation) that this isn't a bug
so much as a real gap: `cfg.phoneDisplaceDamping` only smooths the FINAL
output vector once per frame -- it has no effect whatsoever on the
leaky integrator's own 2 internal decay rates
(`PHONE_DISPLACE_VELOCITY_DECAY_RATE`/`PHONE_DISPLACE_POSITION_DECAY_RATE`),
which were hardcoded constants. Position's own decay in particular
(0.08 1/s, ~8.7s half-life) is slow enough that any disturbance --
including ordinary ambient sensor noise with the phone sitting
completely still -- keeps visibly influencing the displayed position
for several seconds, independent of the Damping slider. This plausibly
explains both the reported "drifts back to center" feel and the
previously-reported reversal overshoot (residual position from a first
move hasn't cleared by the time an opposite move begins, since it's
governed by this same slow decay, not by Damping).

Attempted to verify a specific replacement value against the real
logged data (the "phone left untouched" maneuver, the cleanest
available "nothing should happen" case) and found a real methodology
gap instead: the Sensor Log samples at ~200ms intervals, but the actual
integrator runs on the much-faster real `devicemotion` event stream --
replaying at the logged sample rate under-counts real integration
steps and does not reproduce real measured output magnitudes (a replay
at the current 0.08 position-decay rate predicted a peak of 0.046m;
the real logged output swung from a peak of ~0.09m down through zero to
~-0.05m over the same window). Rather than ship an unverifiable
replacement number, converted both rates from hardcoded constants to
cfg-backed sliders (`phoneDisplaceVelDecayRate`/`phoneDisplacePosDecayRate`,
Responsive Displace subgroup) with defaults UNCHANGED from the prior
hardcoded values -- nothing changes until these are retuned live against
the real device, the same way Damping already is.

Also noted during this analysis, flagged to the user rather than acted
on unilaterally: the pasted Test 2 ("left then right, back and forth")
and Test 3 ("phone flat, reset, left alone, no movement") datasets look
swapped. Test 2's data shows Compass and Orient β/γ completely flat for
the entire 8-second window (consistent with a stationary phone, not a
left-right motion), while Test 3's data shows a real orientation
disturbance (`Gyro β:-0.90 γ:-5.40` shortly after reset) and a 2+ unit
position swing despite being labeled as untouched.

2026-10-01  -  Fix Texture X/Y Offset (and Scale) applying desktop value
regardless of device

Direct report: "When I set settings to view in mobile. Such as texture
x offset, the slide appears in mobile tab, but it does nothing. Behavior
is still dictated by desktop tab."

Root cause: `applyScreenTextureTransform()` (the render-time code that
actually applies `screenTextureOffsetX/Y`/`screenTextureScaleX/Y` to the
live texture) picked its device suffix via `getActiveDevPanelTab()` --
a devPanel.js helper reporting which TAB is currently scrolled into view
in the dev panel EDITOR UI, not which device is actually running the
app. That is the correct signal for `wireDeviceSlider()`'s own WRITE
path (while editing, the slider should write into whichever tab you
have open) but wrong for render-time value selection -- the live texture
only ever picked up a Mobile/Landscape-suffixed value while the panel
happened to be sitting on that tab; the rest of the time (panel closed,
or sitting on Desktop) it silently fell back to the Desktop-suffixed
value regardless of the real device.

Added `getRuntimeDeviceSuffix()` (`isTouchDevice` + live `innerWidth`/
`innerHeight` orientation check, matching every other real device/
orientation check already in this file) and switched
`applyScreenTextureTransform()`'s one `deviceSuffix` computation to use
it instead of `getActiveDevPanelTab()` -- covers all 4 affected controls
(Texture Offset X/Y, Texture Scale X/Y) in one fix since they share that
variable. `index.html` cache-buster: `main.js` `?v=186` -> `?v=187`.

2026-10-01  -  Add stationary gate to Responsive Displace, fixing drift
on a still phone

Direct report + real logged data: "And there is still drift when phone
is static." The pasted log showed a MONOTONIC, non-random climb in
LinearAccel (never changing sign) while raw accelerationIncludingGravity
stayed exactly constant the whole time -- the only thing changing was
Orient β/γ, drifting smoothly by 0.5-1.4deg over ~8s.

Root cause: the device's own orientation estimate was still settling
(compass/gyro sensor fusion), and since gravity subtraction
(`computePhoneLinearAccelDeviceLocal`) depends on that orientation, a
slowly-DRIFTING (not constant) orientation produces a slowly-drifting
linear-acceleration residual that the bias high-pass filter -- tuned to
cancel roughly-constant bias -- can never fully catch up to, since it is
always chasing a moving target.

Fixed with a stationary gate: when the device's own real gyroscope
rotation rate magnitude is below a threshold (default 2 deg/s), force
the integrator's input to exactly zero for that tick, regardless of what
gravity subtraction computed. Applied AFTER the bias filter update (not
before) so bias tracking keeps learning normally during stationary
periods -- a still phone is actually the cleanest signal to learn true
bias from; only the final value fed to the position integrator is
gated.

Verified via a standalone replay of the real logged data: gating at
2 deg/s reduces the predicted drift from a peak of 0.063m to exactly
0.000m. Cross-checked against this project's own real deliberate-motion
test data from earlier in this session, which consistently showed Gyro
values in the 5-90 deg/s range during actual movement -- comfortably
clear of this threshold, so real motion passes through unaffected.

New Responsive Displace controls: Stationary Gate (on/off, default on)
and Stationary Gate Threshold (Deg/s, default 2.0). `index.html`
cache-buster: `main.js` `?v=187` -> `?v=188`.

2026-10-01  -  Build per-axis Min/Max Range + Curve editors for Responsive
Rotation and Displace, finishing the feature begun before the truncation
(previous entry). Direct request: "Make me a displacement min max slider
and a curved editor for all 3 axes. Do the same for the rotation. If the
existing curve editor becomes redundant get rid of it," then "i want x to
be recorded acceleration or rotation, or displacement distance, and y is
output percentage.. that way i can filter out small unintentional
movements."

Displace: Displace's backend (per-axis cfg, phoneDisplaceAxisParsed,
computePhoneDisplaceAxisUnits(rawMeters, ..., axis)) already existed from
before the truncation was found -- this pass wired in its UI: 3x Range-bar
+ 3x Curve-editor + 3x Reference-distance slider (the real METERS that
read as curve-X=1.0, one independently-tunable value per axis, replacing
the old single shared PHONE_DISPLACE_REFERENCE_METERS constant) inside
RESPONSIVE DISPLACE, built via a loop over X/Y/Z rather than 9 unrolled
widget blocks. Also calls parsePhoneResponsiveDisplaceConfig() once
explicitly at build time (not just from curveWidgetResyncs on a detected
change) so a Sync-restored non-default value takes effect from the first
frame.

Rotation: this needed a real restructuring, not just new UI. The OLD
desktop tilt math (computePhoneCombinedQuat()'s desktop branch) combined
nx/ny into ONE radial magnitude (combinedT = hypot(ny,nx)), ran that
through ONE shared curve, then split the result proportionally back into
betaDeg/gammaDeg by direction cosine -- meaning a diagonal tilt's output
always tracked the SAME curve as a single-axis tilt of equal radial
distance, which isn't "3 independent axes." computePhoneResponsiveAxisDeg()
now takes an axis parameter ('X'/'Y'/'Z') and looks up that axis's own
{range, curve, method} from a new phoneRotationAxisParsed object (mirrors
phoneDisplaceAxisParsed's own shape); the desktop branch now computes
betaDeg = computePhoneResponsiveAxisDeg(ny, 'X') and gammaDeg =
computePhoneResponsiveAxisDeg(-nx, 'Y') directly -- each axis independently
mapped through its own curve, no more combine-then-split. alphaDeg (spin,
Z) already used nx as its own separate mechanism; it now reads the 'Z'
curve too. Sign conventions preserved exactly (betaDeg's sign already
followed ny, gammaDeg's followed -nx in the old formula, and
computePhoneResponsiveAxisDeg() already returns magnitude*sign(raw), so
passing the signed raw value directly reproduces the old signs with no
extra Math.sign() call needed) -- confirmed via a standalone, dependency-
free Node script (no `three` package available in this project outside
the browser CDN importmap, so the verification reimplements the handful
of quaternion/vector operations needed) checking: (A) regression -- for a
PURE single-axis tilt with all 3 axes sharing the shipped-default
curve/range, the new independent formula reproduces the old split formula
exactly (byte-identical, since direction-cosine splitting is a no-op when
only one component is nonzero); (B) genuine independence -- with
DIFFERENT ranges per axis, a compound diagonal tilt's 2 outputs now
scale by their OWN axis's range ratio (12x), not by the input's own 1:1
ratio, proving each axis truly uses its own curve; (C) the existing
documented "ONE axis-angle rotation, never sequential Euler" fix (see
this file's own prior entries on cross-axis distortion) is untouched --
betaDeg/gammaDeg still compose into a single
normalize(betaDeg,0,gammaDeg)-axis rotation. 15/15 checks passed.

UI: the old single Min/Max Range + Curve pair in RESPONSIVE BEHAVIOUR -
PHONE > Responsive Rotation is replaced with 3 per-axis pairs (same
loop-over-X/Y/Z pattern as Displace). parsePhoneResponsiveRotationConfig()
is now also called once explicitly at build time (previously it was ONLY
ever triggered by a curveWidgetResyncs-detected value change, meaning a
Sync-restored non-default range/curve silently didn't take effect until
the user manually touched the widget at least once -- not a regression,
Rotation never had this gap closed before; closed now to match Displace's
own, already-correct behavior).

Also: found and fixed in passing (not a deliberate edit) -- the prior
session's truncation-restore commit (642a0b9) appended its ~2200-line
replacement tail via `cat ... >> src/main.js`, which carried the git-
extracted source's own LF line endings onto an otherwise-consistently-
CRLF file. This session's edits normalized the file back to uniform CRLF
(confirmed: `git diff -b`/`--ignore-space-at-eol` shows the REAL content
change was ~199 lines / 128 insertions+71 deletions, matching the actual
edits described above -- the much larger raw diff stat was purely the EOL
normalization). `index.html` cache-buster: `main.js` `?v=190` -> `?v=191`.

Verification: `node --check` passes; zero duplicate function/const/let
declarations (grepped). Live browser-pane verification skipped per this
project's own standing instruction (see CLAUDE.md's Gotchas section --
this sandbox's browser proxy can't reliably reach this project's dev
server). Not independently confirmed against a real device/browser --
same standing limitation as every other Phone Tilt/Rotation/Displace
feature in this project.

2026-10-01  -  Rotation desktop curve input: normalize by half the
browser width.

Direct request: "for desktop, for the rotation curve editors, make the
right side on x axis the width of the browser/2. so it covers all cursor
tracking basicallly." The previous desktop normalization
(`tiltMagnitude = min(hypot(dx,dy)/min(halfWidth,halfHeight), 1)`) used
whichever screen dimension was SMALLER as its saturation ceiling -- on a
typical landscape browser window that's the height, so cursor movement
saturated the curve well before actually reaching the left/right screen
edge; a diagonal/vertical move could reach magnitude=1 at under a third
of the way across the window horizontally.

Added `computeDesktopRotationNxNy()`, normalizing raw cursor pixel
offset (both dx and dy) by `window.innerWidth/2` alone. `dx/halfWidth`
naturally lands in exactly -1..+1 within the viewport with no clamp
needed, reaching precisely +/-1 at the real screen edge -- the "covers
all cursor tracking" the request asked for. `tiltMagnitude`/`tiltAngle`
themselves are untouched -- several OTHER features (Reactive Arm Length,
Responsive Wrist Splay, Palm Rotation) still depend on their existing
saturation behavior, and changing that shared value would have silently
affected all of them (the exact cross-feature-coupling mistake this
file's own `armBaseDistanceT` gotcha already warns against). A new
module-level `desktopCursorDxPx`/`desktopCursorDyPx` pair, set in
`handleMouseMoveFallback()` from the SAME dx/dy already computed there,
feeds the new helper independently.

Both real call sites that used to reconstruct nx/ny from
`tiltMagnitude*cos/sin` -- `computePhoneCombinedQuat()`'s desktop branch
AND `resetPhoneRotationBaseline()`'s own baseline capture -- now go
through this one shared helper, specifically so they can't drift out of
sync with each other the way 2 independent reconstructions of the same
quantity have repeatedly done elsewhere in this file (see the Phone
Model mobile-rotation-axis saga). Missing this would have reproduced
that exact bug class: the live formula changes but the Reset baseline
stays in the old domain, making a "reset to neutral" snap to a wrong,
inconsistent position.

Verified via a standalone script before shipping: for an UNSATURATED
cursor position (old circular clamp not yet engaged), the new formula's
sign conventions match the old one exactly when forced through the same
denominator; separately, at dx=700px on a 1920px-wide reference screen,
the OLD formula was already fully saturated at 1 (just 73% of the way to
the real edge at dx=960), while the NEW formula correctly reports 0.729
-- confirming the fix actually widens coverage rather than just
reshuffling the same saturation point. 10/10 checks passed.

No settings-file patch needed this time -- `phoneNxBaseline`/
`phoneNyBaseline` are plain runtime state (not registered dev-panel
controls), reset fresh to 0 on every page load, never persisted through
Sync.

Verification: `node --check` passes; zero duplicate function/const/let
declarations. Live browser-pane verification skipped per this project's
own standing instruction. Not independently confirmed against a real
cursor/screen -- standing limitation, same as every other Phone
Tilt/Rotation feature in this project.

ahead (dev-panel-settings.json Sync saves + 2 real Item Selector GLB

2026-10-01  -  Fix Texture Offset/Scale never reaching cfg on
Mobile/Landscape.

Direct report: "texture x and y offset still doesnt wok on either tab. I
have the cehckboxes checked" -- followed by "they work on dsktop" / "just
not mobile," confirming Desktop's own value worked (already fixed
earlier this session via getRuntimeDeviceSuffix()) while Mobile/Landscape
specifically still didn't.

Investigated by reading devPanel.js directly rather than guessing.
wireDeviceSlider() (main.js) only ever attaches its 'input' listener to
the DESKTOP element -- the Mobile/Landscape row is a completely SEPARATE
element devPanel.js auto-creates on demand
(ensureDynamicTargetRow(), [JS-4b0]), with its own id. Confirmed the
exact id convention directly from devPanel.js's own resolveDevControlId()
regex (`^(slider|color|select|checkbox)(Mobile|Landscape)(.+)$`): the
device name is inserted right after the type prefix
(sliderMobileScreenTextureOffsetX), NOT appended at the end the way cfg
keys are (screenTextureOffsetXMobile) -- a real naming mismatch between
the DOM id scheme and the cfg key scheme that would have broken any
naive fix attempt.

That cloned element only ever gets devPanel.js's own generic text-
readout listener (buildSliderRow() -- confirmed by reading it: one
`input` listener, repaints the number display only, nothing else).
When the row is "Independent from Desktop" (the whole point of a
device-specific texture offset -- otherwise it would just mirror
Desktop's value, which is useless for this feature), the edited value
is written ONLY into devPanel.js's own internal `devDeviceValues` store
(onDevTargetControlEdited()'s own independent branch) and NEVER reaches
this project's `cfg` object at all -- confirmed by grepping devPanel.js
for every `devDeviceValues` reference: none of them call back into any
project-supplied callback; `cfg.screenTextureOffsetXMobile` (etc.)
stayed frozen at its declared default (0) forever, regardless of how
far the Mobile slider was dragged. This is a previously-undiscovered,
genuinely new bug class in this project -- distinct from (though
related in spirit to) the "hand-built widget invisible to Sync" bug
class documented elsewhere in this file; this one is about the LIVE
cfg write never happening at all, not a persistence gap.

Fixed with a new `wireDeviceSliderMirror(desktopId, cfgKey)` helper,
using the SAME `curveWidgetResyncs` polling pattern already established
in this file for exactly "an element that doesn't exist until later and
devPanel.js's own generic engine won't wire for me" (rather than trying
to attach a live event listener to an element that doesn't exist at
build time and can be destroyed/recreated later by
unchecking/rechecking "Show in Mobile/Landscape"). Reads the DOM value
directly rather than devDeviceValues, so it works correctly whether a
row is independent OR mirroring Desktop (devPanel.js's own
applyDesktopMirror() already keeps a non-independent clone's DOM value
in sync with Desktop -- no separate branch needed). Applied to all 4
sliders sharing this exact mechanism: Texture X/Y Offset (the reported
bug) AND Texture X/Y Scale (the identical bug, found while fixing the
reported one -- same wireDeviceSlider() call, same missing bridge).

Verified via a standalone simulation against a fake DOM/cfg (7/7
checks): no Mobile element yet -> no-op; element created at 0 -> no
spurious write; dragged to 0.75 -> cfg updates; Landscape independently
dragged to -0.4 doesn't affect Mobile's own value; Desktop's own cfg
value untouched throughout; removing the row (unchecking "Show in
Mobile") doesn't crash or reset the last value. Also independently
confirmed the id-generation regex matches devPanel.js's own exactly
(3/3 cases).

Also fixed in passing (found while investigating, not deliberately
sought): a single stray bare `\r` byte (no accompanying `\n`) sitting
exactly at the spot this session's earlier file-truncation restoration
(`cat ... >> src/main.js`) joined its extracted LF content onto the
original CRLF file -- surfaced because it broke an Edit tool string
match (`}function wireDeviceSlider` wouldn't match despite looking
byte-identical on screen). Patched directly via a Node buffer script
(found the exact byte offset, replaced the bare `\r` with a proper
`\r\n`), then confirmed via a full-file scan that no other bare-CR
bytes exist anywhere in the file. And updated
`PHONE_MODEL_PER_MODEL_CONTROL_IDS`, which still listed the old SHARED
`textPhoneResponsiveRotationRange/Curve` and
`textPhoneResponsiveDisplaceRange/Curve` ids from before this session's
earlier per-axis refactor -- a harmless no-op (capturePhoneModelPerModelSettings()'s
own `if (!el) return` guard) but meant per-model switching silently
never captured or restored Rotation/Displace's own range/curve tuning
at all; updated to the real 15 per-axis ids now in use.

Verification: `node --check` passes; zero duplicate function/const/let
declarations; zero remaining bare-CR bytes. Live browser-pane
verification skipped per this project's own standing instruction --
not independently confirmed on a real device/browser, same standing
limitation as everything else in this project.

Note: committing this also required a 2nd fast-forward past 3 more
remote commits (further live Save Settings activity from the user's own
session) -- this time with no file overlap against the working tree's
own uncommitted Blender asset changes (only dev-panel-settings.json was
touched remotely), so no stash was needed, unlike the previous entry's
merge.

2026-10-01  -  Make Mobile/Landscape texture-offset edits take effect
instantly (CORRECTED, same-day follow-up to the previous entry's fix).

Direct report: "when i update mobile tab settings, othing happens, but
if i save ad refresh, they show up. make it isntantneous." The previous
fix (wireDeviceSliderMirror(), a curveWidgetResyncs poll) correctly
bridged the EDITED VALUE into cfg -- confirmed working by the user's own
report, since save+refresh DID show the change -- but missed a 2nd,
deeper property of this project's architecture: on-demand rendering
(see requestRender()'s own top-of-file comment). The render loop fully
STOPS once idle (`renderLoopRunning = false`), and only requestRender()
restarts it. curveWidgetResyncs only runs AS PART OF animate()'s own
per-frame body -- once the loop goes idle (the normal resting state
whenever nothing else is actively animating), the poll never gets
scheduled again, no matter how long you wait. Dragging the Mobile
slider alone never called requestRender() (no project-level listener
was ever attached to the cloned element to begin with -- that's the
whole reason the FIRST bug existed), so the render loop could easily
already be idle at the moment of the edit, meaning the poll never got a
chance to run and pick up the change. Save+refresh "worked" only
because a page load naturally triggers several renders in a row (the
initial load itself, remote-settings restores, etc.), giving the poll
enough chances to catch up once -- not because the live-editing path
was actually fixed.

Replaced the poll with EVENT DELEGATION: one `document`-level 'input'
listener per device (not per-element, not per-control -- registered
once when wireDeviceSliderMirror() is called), checking `e.target.id`
against the expected Mobile/Landscape id. This needs no knowledge of
whether the cloned element exists yet (delegation naturally covers
anything that bubbles an event up to `document`, regardless of when it
was created), survives the element being removed/recreated
(unchecking/rechecking "Show in Mobile/Landscape" -- confirmed via the
standalone test's own case 4, a brand-new element object with the same
id, zero re-wiring needed) -- and, critically, calls `requestRender()`
DIRECTLY inside the event handler, which the poll could never do for
itself once the loop it depends on had already stopped. Fires on the
REAL native 'input' event the slider already dispatches on every drag
tick (confirmed bubbles by default) AND on the synthetic events
devPanel.js's own `applyControlValues()` dispatches with
`{ bubbles: true }` for Sync/Reset/Undo restores -- one listener covers
both paths.

Verified via a standalone simulation against a fake delegated-event
document (9/9 checks): delegation fires correctly for an element
created AFTER the listener was registered (the entire point of using
delegation over direct wiring); an unrelated control's own 'input'
event doesn't cross-fire; Mobile and Landscape update independently of
each other; `requestRender()` is called exactly once per real edit (not
zero, not duplicated); a "removed and recreated" element (same id, new
object, simulating the visibility checkbox being unchecked then
rechecked) keeps working with no re-wiring needed.

Verification: `node --check` passes; zero duplicate function/const/let
declarations. Live browser-pane verification skipped per this project's
own standing instruction -- not independently confirmed that dragging
now actually feels instantaneous on a real device/browser, only that
the underlying event-delegation + requestRender() mechanism is correct
in isolation.

Note: committing this also required a 3rd fast-forward past 3 more
remote commits (further live Save Settings activity) -- again no file
overlap against the working tree's own uncommitted Blender asset
changes (only dev-panel-settings.json touched remotely), so no stash
was needed.

2026-10-01  -  Add Freeze-on-Stop and Tilt (driftless) Displace modes.

Direct report with a real device log: "I move the phone forward and
backward in a single axis. I pause in between the different movements.
When I move the phone, the phone in the app moves accordingly, but once
I stop, it drifts back to it's default position without me telling it
to." Investigated by replaying the real logged Sensor/Phone Model Log
data against the actual integrator math rather than guessing -- found
this is NOT a tunable-decay-rate bug. Stopping a push is itself a real,
physical deceleration (an opposite-direction acceleration pulse,
visible directly in the log's own LinearAccel values flipping sign the
instant motion stops), which cancels most of the velocity the push just
built up. A complete push-then-stop gesture, double-integrated, nets
close to zero displacement by the basic physics of impulse-momentum --
this is the well-known "dead reckoning drift" limitation every
inertial-only (accelerometer/gyroscope) position-tracking system has
without an external reference (GPS, vision, etc.) to correct against.
No amount of decay-rate tuning changes this, because it isn't a
parameter in the model -- it's a property of what double-integrated
acceleration data physically represents.

Direct follow-up asked whether the deceleration pulse could just be
"cancelled out" after the fact -- investigated and explained why this
isn't viable: there's no way to distinguish "deceleration from my own
just-finished push" from "a genuine new push in the opposite direction"
using acceleration data alone (both are just negative-signed
acceleration); cancelling one would also cancel the other, breaking
real backward motion -- exactly the gesture the user's own test
included. Separately, deceleration is part of what actually happened
physically; artificially cancelling it would mean showing MORE
displacement than the real phone actually underwent, which directly
contradicts the user's own stated goal ("I want the virtual movement to
mirror my real world movement and nothing else").

Proposed and built 2 genuinely different alternatives instead, both
added as additional Displace Mode options (not replacing the existing
2, specifically so all 4 could be A/B tested on a real device per
direct request: "Build 1 and 2 and gimne a drop down to test both as
well as the current system"):

- 'freeze': the existing Stationary Gate (gyroscope-based on mobile,
  cursor-delta-based on desktop, already built for a different fix
  earlier this session) now also gates whether
  applyPhoneDisplaceSample() gets called AT ALL once motion is judged
  to have stopped -- not just fed a zero input, which still lets that
  function's own leaky decay run and erode position every tick
  regardless of input. Position/velocity now hold EXACTLY fixed, byte-
  identical, for as long as the gate reads "stationary." Disclosed
  limitation, found by walking the real log's own Gyro magnitude at the
  exact moment of the original snap-back: a push's own deceleration is
  itself real, gyro-correlated motion (a wrist naturally wobbles some
  when stopping a moving phone) that can keep gyroMag ABOVE the
  Stationary Gate's threshold for the first several ticks after the
  push ends -- meaning in the user's own captured case, most of the
  velocity-cancelling deceleration already happens BEFORE the gate ever
  trips. This mode freezes whatever's left at that point; it cannot
  retroactively undo cancellation that already occurred while the gate
  still considered the phone "moving."

- 'tilt': skips integration (and therefore drift) entirely by never
  accumulating anything. Reads the phone's CURRENT orientation angle
  directly every frame (mobile: deviceorientation beta/gamma; desktop:
  the already-existing computeDesktopRotationNxNy(), the same
  memoryless cursor-offset signal Rotation's own desktop branch
  already uses, scaled by 45 to land in a comparable degree-like
  range) relative to a baseline, mapped straight to displacement with
  no curve, no range, no reference distance, and no clamp -- the exact
  same "pure, memoryless function of the current reading" technique
  Rotation's own 'absolute' mode already uses to eliminate ITS
  equivalent gyro-integration drift. A held tilt now produces an exact,
  permanently unchanging displacement, by construction -- there is
  nothing left in the system that COULD drift. Disclosed limitation
  (surfaced to the user BEFORE building, not after): this only covers
  2 of 3 axes. Left-right and forward-back are real, physically
  absolute angles a phone can report; depth (toward/away from the
  phone's own face) has no angle equivalent at all, since rotating a
  phone in place never changes its distance from anything -- only
  genuine translation does, which is exactly the kind of motion that
  can't be read without integration (and therefore without the same
  drift this mode exists to avoid). Z outputs exactly 0 in Tilt mode
  rather than silently keeping the old, inconsistent drifting behavior
  on just that one axis. A direct follow-up question ("xyz are all
  just axes. why wont your fix work for z") was answered explicitly:
  the asymmetry isn't in the axes themselves, it's in which sensor
  reading happens to exist as a substitute for each one -- a phone
  reports exactly 2 independent tilt angles and ZERO depth-equivalent
  readings, a hard sensor limitation, not a design choice.

Mechanically: integratePhoneDisplacement() and
updatePhoneDisplaceDesktopFrame() both now skip entirely when Tilt mode
is selected (neither touches the old integrator state in that mode at
all), and integratePhoneDisplacement() now tracks an `isStationary`
flag (previously computed and immediately used/discarded for the
zero-input path) so 'freeze' mode can also read it to decide whether to
call applyPhoneDisplaceSample() at all this tick.
computePhoneResponsiveDisplacement() branches to a separate, simpler
code path for Tilt mode entirely (no curve/range/reference lookup).
Baseline re-capture (resetPhoneDisplaceTiltBaseline()) is wired into
Displace Reset (double-tap/double-click -- called UNCONDITIONALLY
regardless of which mode is active, matching this file's own
established "update every baseline every time, never branch on the
active mode" discipline, since a past bug here came from exactly the
opposite choice), switching into Tilt mode via the dropdown, and
enabling Displace while Tilt mode is already selected -- each avoids a
jump from a stale/zero baseline.

Verified via a standalone simulation (6/6 checks) before shipping:
Freeze mode holds position byte-identical across simulated "stationary"
ticks where the old always-decaying behavior would keep changing it
regardless of new input; Tilt mode's output is proven to be an exact,
time-independent function of (current angle - baseline) -- 1000
simulated reads at a constant held angle produce the identical value
every time (zero drift by construction), and re-baselining to the
current angle correctly zeroes the output.

Verification: `node --check` passes; zero duplicate function/const/let
declarations. Live browser-pane verification skipped per this
project's own standing instruction -- the real A/B comparison between
all 4 modes can only happen on the user's own real device.

Note: committing this also required a 4th fast-forward past 15 more
remote commits (continuous live Save Settings activity from the user's
own active testing session) -- again no file overlap against the
working tree's own uncommitted Blender asset changes (only
dev-panel-settings.json touched remotely), so no stash was needed.

2026-10-06 -- Displace: Position Overshoot Limit + Velocity Decay 0.5

Cause (from the 09:07:08Z log and the matching phone export): during continuous
vigorous motion the Stationary Gate never goes quiet, so ZUPT / Quiet Velocity Bleed
never engage and with Velocity Decay 0 an integrated velocity wanders freely --
8 m on X at the start of one log, Z velocity about -1.4 m/s and -6.45 m position in
4.6 s in another. The mapping saturates at the Reference Distance, so the raw
position far past it means nothing visually but takes metres of reverse travel to
come back.

Fix 1 (code, main.js ?v=230, commit 9d5a4f9): cfg.phoneDisplaceOvershootLimit
(default 1.5, slider 'Position Overshoot Limit (x Reference, 0 Off)', 0 = old
unbounded behaviour). clampDisplaceOvershoot() clamps the raw position per axis to
+-(limit x that axis' Reference Distance) and zeroes a velocity still pushing
outward; applied at the end of applyPhoneDisplaceSample() and
stepDisplaceShadowPipeline() so the real pipeline and both A/B shadows match.
Included in the per-model snapshot id list.

Fix 2 (settings, commit 1109e3c): Velocity Decay 0 -> 0.5 on the Desktop control and
the iPhone 17 Max / Iphone17MaxPro per-model snapshots. Replay calibration: real
30 cm sweep reads 0.28 m with 0.5 (the real sweep is <= 0.30 m); the runaway replay
drops from 6.0 m to 2.6 m.

Node replay (overshoot-test.mjs): runaway Z -14.98 m unprotected -> -1.49 m with the
limit (bound 1.635 m), -1.57 m with decay 0.5 added; no velocity left pushing
outward; a reverse push from the limit moves back off it (-1.64 -> -1.30 m) where
the unprotected run went -7.50 -> -10.10 m; a normal 32 cm move is identical with
the limit on or off (0.3235 m both). NOT verified on a real device. The phone must
reload before Sync so it does not write the old 0 back over the git value.

2026-10-08 -- Dropdown text white; hand scaled to true size next to the phone

1. Every dev-panel dropdown (closed value AND the open option list, including
   selects without the .dev-select class such as the Tween pose rows) is now white
   (style.css ?v=13).

2. Scale audit vs HANDO. Measured from the GLB files' own node transforms (Node,
   no browser): every phone is in true metres -- Galaxy S2 0.1253, iPhone 17 Max
   0.1511, iPhone 17 Pro Max 0.1634, Nothing 2 0.1621, P5 Project 1 0.1446,
   Pixel 9A 0.1547, Galaxy S26 0.1518 m tall (Motorola Razr 0.2289 m, S4.glb has no
   nodes). Hand wrist->middle tip (rHand->rMid3): 0.16086 in HandiBonesB-IK and
   HandipantsOL/OL2/OL3, 16.086 in Hand2.glb (same hand authored x100). HANDO draws
   hand and phone at the same units-per-metre (388.436). HANDYSET did not: the
   phone was 100 units/m while computeBaseScale() normalized the hand to a fixed
   8 x Hand Scale units wrist->tip (synced Hand Scale 1 = 8 units), so a real
   phone (0.1634 m -> 16.3 units) sat beside a hand about half its real relative
   size.

   Fix, per the instruction "scale the hand model and not the phone model":
   computeBaseScale() = HAND_REFERENCE_LENGTH_M (0.16086) x UNITS_PER_METER (100)
   / handLengthRaw x Hand Scale. Hand Scale 1 is now true size beside a phone at
   Model Scale 1 (hand wrist->tip 16.086 units instead of 8). Phone scale is
   unchanged (PHONE_MODEL_SCALE_BASE = UNITS_PER_METER = 100). main.js ?v=231.

   Consequences: the hand is ~2x larger on screen at the same Hand Scale and
   camera; saved cameras and the per-model phone Offset Y values were tuned for the
   old hand size and may need re-adjusting. Hand Scale 0.5 restores the old size.
   Not browser-verified (project rule); numbers above are from file measurements.

2026-10-09 -- Phone offsets now persist; per-mode remembered settings; per-axis toggleable groups

1. "My phone model offset settings never save" -- ROOT CAUSE: loadRemoteSettingsOnStartup()
   re-applied the Set-as-Default phone pose (defaultPhonePose = Offset X 0.9917 /
   Y 1.9534 / Z 0, Rot 0/0/0) AFTER restoring the Synced settings on every load, so the
   default silently replaced whatever you had Synced. Evidence from the git history of
   dev-panel-settings.json: a Synced Galaxy S2 Offset Y of 19.2637 (top-level control AND
   its per-model snapshot) came back as 1.9534 each refresh, and a Synced X of 0 came back
   as 0.9917. Fix: the default pose is only a STARTING pose now -- it fills a field only
   when the Synced settings hold no value for that control at all. Consequence: once a
   value is Synced, "Set as Default" on a phone pose no longer overrides it.

2. Per-mode remembered settings (Responsive Rotation: Gyro / Absolute; Responsive Displace:
   all 5 modes). createPerModeStore() remembers each mode's tuning controls separately
   (Rotation: the 3 axis On/Off, Scale, Fine-Tune, Min/Max Range, Curve and Damping.
   Displace: the 3 axes' On/Off, Scale, Invert, Range, Curve, Reference, plus Damping,
   Velocity/Position Decay, Stationary Gate, ZUPT, Velocity Snap, Quiet Bleed, Overshoot
   Limit and Bias Limit). Switching the mode select swaps in that mode's stored set and
   stores the one you left. The first visit to a mode with nothing stored keeps the current
   values. Left out on purpose: the feature On/Off, Reset On/Off and the mode select. Only a
   REAL user change swaps (event.isTrusted); Sync/Reset/Undo/per-model restores dispatch
   synthetic events and must not. The stores are hidden JSON controls
   (hiddenPhoneRotationPerModeSettings / hiddenPhoneDisplacePerModeSettings) that ride
   Sync/Copy/Reset/Undo and the per-model snapshot. Node test (permode-test.mjs): capture,
   restore, carry-over, untrusted-change guard and external restore all pass.

3. Per-axis toggleable groups: Responsive Rotation now has ROTATION - X/Y/Z AXIS groups
   (Scale, Fine-Tune, Min/Max Range, Curve) and Responsive Displace has DISPLACE - X/Y/Z
   AXIS groups (Scale, Invert, Range, Curve, Reference). The old axis On/Off checkbox
   (same id, same cfg field) now lives in each group's title bar via
   makeDevGroupToggleable(); unchecking it hides the group. In Tilt displace mode the Z group
   hides entirely (no depth equivalent of a tilt angle) and X/Y keep only On/Off/Scale/Invert.
   Settings file: the saved layout for both sections was rewritten to the new structure
   (every row kept, 2 hidden store rows added, sectionOrder subgroup copies re-normalized,
   all non-layout keys byte-identical). HANDYSET_SETTINGS_SCHEMA_VERSION -> 2026-10-09 so
   a stale local layout is dropped on next load (un-Synced local edits are lost).
   main.js ?v=232. Not browser-verified (project rule).

2026-10-09 -- Ported from HANDO: Base Rotation turns about the hand MESH's axes; IK Min / Max are offsets from the base pose

Source of truth read directly: HANDO src/main.js updateModelRootRotation() / modelBaseFrameQ and the `win` block of
solveIkHandPose() (its changelog entries 2026-10-09 01:59 AM and 02:09 AM).

1. IK Min / Max From Base. The range bars under each IK influence slider used to be absolute pose-slider values, so a base pose
   outside the bar's numbers was pulled onto the nearest edge the moment IK turned on. The window is now
   [base + Min, base + Max] (base pose = 0), e.g. a Max of 360 deg is base + 360. Two lines in solveIkHandPose();
   the labels now read "... Min / Max From Base (Deg|%|Units)". Numbers already saved in the bars keep their values but now MEAN
   offsets; the defaults (+-360 deg, +-200 %, +-100 units) still allow everything, and Elbow Bend's -10 / 150 now means
   base-10 .. base+150 (kept as in HANDO). Saved IK-influence presets carry the same numbers. The baseline stays inside its own
   window whenever Min <= 0 <= Max; with Min > 0 or Max < 0 it is still pulled to the nearest edge.

2. Whole-Hand Rotation at Base X/Y/Z (baseRotationX/Y/Z). It rotated about the clone's parent-frame axes, not the hand mesh's own,
   so with HandiBonesB-IK (Armature turned +90 deg about X: the mesh's Y is the root's Z, its Z the root's -Y) Y and Z looked
   swapped. getHandMeshFrameQuat(h) = product of the LOCAL quaternions from the skinned mesh up to (not including) h.clone,
   cached per hand (constant: only bones move). applyBaseArmRotation() keeps its delta-tracking, but the cumulative rotation is
   now desired = G * (Rx*Ry*Rz) * G^-1 with G = q0 * F (the mesh frame in the clone's parent space) and
   q0 = lastBaseArmQuat^-1 * clone.q, i.e. HANDO's q = q0 * (F R F^-1). The arm-base pivot logic is unchanged.
   Checked from the GLB node chains (frame-test.mjs): F = +90 deg about X for HandiBonesB-IK (matches HANDO's (0.7071, 0, 0,
   0.7071)) and identity for Hand2.glb (a no-op there, as HANDO documents); for a random q0 each slider's rotation axis equals
   the mesh's X/Y/Z axis (dot 1.000000). NOT run in a browser (project rule), and an IK solve driving Base Rotation was not run
   end to end. Still absolute Euler angles composed X, then Y, then Z in the mesh frame (Y follows an earlier X rotation).
   Saved poses with a non-zero Base Rotation will pose differently now, because the axes changed.
   main.js ?v=233.
