2026-10-09 -- Settings audit: restored values were being clamped by the slider's range (Recursive Render and others)

Report: "some more settings don't seem to be saving, such as Recursive Render settings. Check all settings."
Method: loaded the LIVE site and compared every one of the 287 controls in the synced dev-panel-settings.json
against the DOM element after load (287/287 present), then the cfg-derivable ones against window.__debug.cfg.
Findings -- the saved file was correct everywhere; the loss was on RESTORE:
 * sliderScreenRecursionLevels: file 15, DOM and cfg 10. The slider was built with max 10; click-to-type had auto-expanded
   its range, but a restore assigns el.value directly and a range input silently CLAMPS to min/max (and snaps to step).
   The same happened to cfg.keyAzimuth (default lighting 439 vs slider max 360: cfg 439, DOM 360 -- so the next Sync would
   have written the clamped 360 back). Every other control matched exactly.
Fix: devPanel.js setDevControlValue(el, value) (also window.setDevControlValue) does what click-to-type does -- widens the
crossed bound to value +-20% (and its bound label) and assigns with step 'any' -- and is now used by applyControlValues()
(Sync/Reset/Undo/Load), writeDevControlValue() (Mobile/Landscape mirror rows), and in main.js by syncControlDom() (preset
applies) and the per-model and per-mode snapshot restores (setControlValueFitted()). Recursion Levels' default max also
raised 10 -> 20. Node test (range-test.mjs, a range input that clamps/snaps like a browser): 15 on a 1..10 slider is
clamped to 10 with a plain assignment and kept (max widened to 18) with the helper; off-step 0.001 and below-min values kept;
in-range values leave the bounds alone.
Not bugs (by design): Camera Zoom (default camera applied after the Sync restore) and Camera Z (live).
Other Mobile/Landscape-only (Independent) rows still do not drive cfg unless bridged (see the earlier Gotchas).
devPanel.js ?v=20, main.js ?v=234. This devPanel.js fix is generic and belongs in .claude/TEMPLATE_DEV_PANEL.html too
(not folded back yet -- a deliberate separate step).

2026-10-09 -- Base Rotation (ported from HANDO): fixed the quaternion-length blow-up in applyBaseArmRotation() [written by a HANDO session at the user's request]
  - Report: "they implemented the hand model rotation fix incorrectly". The AXES part of the 03:07 port is right (measured below); the NUMERICS were not.
  - Cause: THREE's Quaternion.invert() is only the conjugate, which equals the inverse only for a UNIT quaternion. The ported code builds G = q0 * F with
    q0 = lastBaseArmQuat^-1 * clone.q and uses desired = G * R * G^-1, then stores desired in h.lastBaseArmQuat and multiplies deltas into
    h.clone.quaternion / h.currentBaseQuat -- none of which were ever re-normalised. A tiny length error made G non-unit, G*R*conj(G) scaled `desired` by
    |G|^2, that went into lastBaseArmQuat, which fed the next call's q0: the error multiplied on every call.
  - MEASURED before the fix (preview, a few base-slider moves): |clone.q| = 1, 0.99999, 0.99997, 0.99984, 0.9992 (|lastBaseArmQuat| 0.99947); returning a slider to 0 left the
    hand 0.16 deg (X), 0.77 deg (Y), 4.52 deg (Z) off, accumulating; after a few more cycles h.clone.quaternion, h.currentBaseQuat and h.lastBaseArmQuat were all (0,0,0,0)
    (hand destroyed). With Responsive Arm Rotation at Base ON this function runs every frame, so it would be hit far faster.
  - Fix (src/main.js, applyBaseArmRotation only): normalise q0 and G, normalise `desired` after the conjugation, normalise lastBaseArmQuat before inverting it, and
    .normalize() the stored results (h.clone.quaternion, h.currentBaseQuat). No change to the axes maths or the pivot code.
  - MEASURED after the fix (same preview): each of X / Y / Z at 40 deg rotates about exactly the mesh's X / Y / Z axis (e.g. Y: 0.999,-0.03,-0.03 = the mesh Y axis) and the
    rForearmBend world position moves 0,0,0; after 40 mixed slider moves (-80..+79 deg) the maximum |length - 1| of clone.q / lastBaseArmQuat / currentBaseQuat is 0, and
    returning all three sliders to 0 leaves the hand 0 deg and (0,0,0) off its start. No console errors.
  - NOT fixed (separate, pre-existing, reported to the user): changing Whole-Hand Rotation while a Base Rotation is set drops the base rotation from the hand (applyModelRootTransform()
    invalidates lastBaseArmQuat; the sliders still read 50 but the hand is 50 deg off until a base slider is touched again). HANDO does not have this (its base rotation is stateless).
  - Not tested: Responsive Arm Rotation at Base running per frame with tracking on; a second hand; the user's own browser. Not committed (left for the user / the HANDYSET session).

2026-10-09 -- Base Rotation stays applied when Whole-Hand Rotation / Pose Scale / a pose changes, and after a Field Layout change [written by a HANDO session at the user's request]
  - Follow-up to the entry above ("yeah fix it" to the item it listed as NOT fixed). Two causes, same family: applyModelRootTransform() rebuilds h.clone's rotation
    from the PLAIN pose values and sets h.lastBaseArmQuat = null, and nothing put Base Rotation back (only the Responsive Arm Rotation / Pose Tween frames did).
  - (1) Pose changes dropped it. Any applyPoseValuesToHand() call (Whole-Hand Rotation, Pose Scale, other Pose sliders, saved-pose Use, tween sequences) removed Base Rotation
    from the hand while the sliders still showed it. MEASURED before: Base Rotation Y = 50, then Whole-Hand Rotation X = 30 -> the hand was 50 deg off a freshly applied 50.
    Fix: applyPoseValuesToHand(poseValues, skipBaseReapply = false) now ends with applyBaseArmRotation(computeResponsiveBaseArmRotationDeg(armBaseDistanceT), poseValues)
    (0 unless tracking + Responsive are on). The Responsive Pose Tween frame passes true because it re-applies Base Rotation itself with its blended values.
  - (2) Field Layout double-applied it. relayoutField() (Column/Row Spacing, Hand Model enable, ...) called applyModelRootTransform(h, cfg) assuming it was idempotent; with Base Rotation
    set, h.currentBaseQuat already carried the delta, so the rotation was kept but the tracker reset and the position recomputed with the palm pivot instead of the base-bone pivot.
    MEASURED before: Base Rotation Y = 50, change Column Spacing, touch the Base Rotation Y slider again with 50 -> the hand moved another 50 deg (100 total).
    Fix: relayoutField() rebuilds the pure rotation (h.currentBaseQuat.copy(computeBaseQuatFromValues(cfg))) before applyModelRootTransform and calls applyBaseArmRotation once after the loop.
  - MEASURED after (preview, one hand): Whole-Hand Rotation X 0 -> 30 with Base Y = 50: 0 deg and (0,0,0) from the freshly applied reference; Pose Scale 1 -> 1.4: 0 deg / (0,0,0);
    30 mixed Whole-Hand/Base moves: quaternion lengths stay 1 (max deviation 0) and everything at 0 returns to the start (0 deg, bone 0,0,0 off); Column Spacing change: 0 deg and
    (0,0,0) bone movement, touching the Base slider again with the same value: 0 deg movement, Base Rotation stays exactly 50 deg from zero. No console errors.
  - Left alone: ikApplyPose() also calls applyModelRootTransform() every evaluation, so Base Rotation is not part of an IK solve (it was not before either). Not tested: Responsive Arm Rotation / Pose
    Tween per frame with tracking on, a multi-hand field, Phone Tilt together with Base Rotation, the user's own browser. Not committed (left for the user / the HANDYSET session).

2026-10-09 -- IK solver now drives Whole-Hand Rotation at BASE (baseRotationX/Y/Z) instead of Whole-Hand Rotation [written by a HANDO session at the user's request]
  - Request: "i want handyset ik solver to use base rotation". Same change HANDO made (IK_GLOBAL_DOFS: baseRotationX/Y/Z replace modelRotX/Y/Z).
  - Changes (src/main.js): IK_GLOBAL_DOFS now lists baseRotationX/Y/Z; ikPartsOfKey() maps baseRotation[XYZ] to the existing 'Rot' influence row (keys ikInfluenceRot / ikRangeRot,
    so saved values carry over); ikRegWeight() gives them the same 0.5 as Whole-Hand Rotation; the 'Rot' row labels now read "Whole-Hand Rotation at Base (%)" and
    "... at Base Min / Max From Base (Deg)". Whole-Hand Rotation (modelRot) is no longer an IK joint. ikApplyPose() (one solver evaluation) was split into two passes: pass 1 resets
    every hand's plain pose rotation (applyModelRootTransform, which also nulls applyBaseArmRotation()'s tracker, so Base Rotation is applied from scratch each time = stateless),
    then applyBaseArmRotation(extra, cfg, true) applies Base Rotation to all hands BEFORE pass 2 (arm / wrist / finger bake), because the finger axes come from h.currentBaseQuat.
    applyBaseArmRotation() got a third parameter skipFingerBake (default false) so the solver bakes the fingers once, not twice. baseRotation keys were already in POSE_KEY_DEFAULTS,
    so the baseline capture, IK-off restore and slider syncing needed no change.
  - MEASURED (preview, one hand, a test pair index-finger pad -> phone "Back - Bottom Left", world axes, ONLY the Base Rotation influence row > 0): with the row at 0 the pair error was
    pos 60.04 u / rot 82.1 deg and nothing moved; with it at 100 the solver changed Base Rotation from (5, 92, 0) to (-1.55, -12.2, -15.75) and reached pos 1.84 u / rot 64.9 deg
    (16 iterations, stage 2 accepted, 19 ms), Whole-Hand Rotation stayed (90, 90, 0). The solved pose equals the slider path: re-applying the same cfg through applyPoseValuesToHand
    (a Pose Scale slider event) moved the hand 0 deg and the target node 0 units. Turning IK off restored Base Rotation to (5, 92, 0) and the sliders show it. Quaternion lengths of
    h.clone / currentBaseQuat / lastBaseArmQuat stayed exactly 1 across repeated solves. No console errors.
  - Notes: the values saved in the 'Rot' influence / Min-Max controls now govern Base Rotation (they used to govern Whole-Hand Rotation); with the default Rot influence 0 nothing changes until it is raised.
    Base Rotation inside a solve includes the Responsive Arm Rotation contribution only when tracking + Responsive are on (same as the sliders). Not tested: Responsive Arm Rotation / tracking on
    during a solve, multi-hand fields (the solver uses one pair set), Phone Tilt together with Base Rotation, the user's own browser. Not committed (left for the user / the HANDYSET session).

2026-10-09 -- Base Rotation pivot: used a WORLD position as a position inside h.wrapper, so a hand away from the world origin jumped when Base Rotation changed [written by a HANDO session at the user's request]
  - Report: "when i slide rotation at base, the camera moves" (no camera setting locked) and "sometimes nothing happens" on the Pose-group Rotation at Base sliders.
  - Checked first: the camera itself. OrbitControls listens on the canvas only, nothing pins the orbit target to the hand (it is the fixed Camera Anchor), and in the preview camera.position /
    quaternion / controls.target did not change at all while the Base Rotation sliders moved (default camera and an orbited fractional one). The latest saved settings (10-09 03:23) have
    no lock set, IK off, Tracking off, camera top-down (pitch 89, position 0 / 79.5 / 0.0002, zoom slider 79.5, defaultCamera zoom 26), Responsive Arm Rotation + Pose Tween ON (inactive without Tracking).
  - Found: applyBaseArmRotation() took the arm-base bone's WORLD position (baseWorldPos) and used it as a position in h.clone's PARENT space (h.wrapper). That is the same thing only while
    h.wrapper sits untransformed at the world origin. For a hand placed away from it (field grid, Phone Tilt / Displace offsets) Base Rotation did not pivot on the bone: the whole hand jumped
    toward the origin, which looks like the view moving, and it may leave the screen. MEASURED before: wrapper at (30,0,-20): a 60 deg slide moved the base bone (-2.09,-14.69,-12.61) units
    and returning the slider to 0 left it (-0.12, 3.55, -19.15) from where it started; with the wrapper at the origin it moved (0,0,0), which is why it never showed in single-hand tests.
  - Fix: the pivot is converted with h.wrapper.worldToLocal() (after h.wrapper.updateWorldMatrix(true,false)) before it is used for the position offset; the rotation delta was already in
    the parent's space. Applies to the sliders and to the IK solver (same function).
  - MEASURED after, three setups (wrapper at origin / offset (30,0,-20) / offset + rotated 0.7 rad about Y): each of X, Y, Z at 40 deg turns the hand exactly 40 deg about the mesh's own
    X / Y / Z axis, the base bone moves (0,0,0), and returning each slider to 0 gives 0 deg and (0,0,0) off the start. No console errors.
  - NOT reproduced: the camera object itself moving, and the "nothing happens" case with a real mouse (the pane's coordinate frame did not deliver my drag to the slider, so only
    synthetic input events were used; every flag combination of Tracking x Pose Tween x Responsive Arm Rotation changed the hand correctly). If the camera still moves after this, the
    exact situation (desktop or phone, how many hands, Tracking on?) is needed. Whole-Hand Rotation was left enabled: the earlier fix already re-applies Base Rotation after it changes.
  - Not committed (left for the user / the HANDYSET session).

2026-10-09 -- Absolute rotation mode rewritten around the phone's physical axes (ccd8266); IK Rotation Weight slider (af89681) [written by a HANDO session at the user's request]
  - ABSOLUTE MODE. Report (real phone; X left-right, Y charging port -> camera, Z face -> back): with only Z on, a real Z turn rotated the model about Y; with only Y on, a real Z turn
    rotated the model about Z; a real Y turn never changed anything; Gyro mode was right. Cause: computePhoneAbsoluteOrientationRawQuat() gated/scaled the three W3C Euler ANGLES (alpha, beta,
    gamma), which are an intrinsic Z-X'-Y'' chain, not rotations about the phone's own X/Y/Z (and alpha/gamma collapse onto one axis as the phone is held upright: measured axes 0 / 0.71 / 0.98 / 1.00
    parallel at beta 0 / 45 / 80 / 89.9). Fix: the raw quaternion is now the full ungated orientation; computePhoneAbsoluteOrientationQuat() takes rel = baselineInverse * current (the turn since
    the baseline, about the baseline's own body axes), splits it into degrees about the phone's X / Y / Z, applies each axis's On/Off and Scale, and feeds the result through GYRO's axis mapping
    (physical X and Z = the 'tilt' axis x / y components, physical Y = spin about PHONE_GYRO_SPIN_LOCAL_AXIS, Y negated) -- Gyro's mapping was verified on the real phone over many rounds.
    Still memoryless / drift-free; the baseline is now the unscaled orientation (it used to depend on which axes were on at the moment of reset).
    MEASURED (the real functions from main.js run in the page with its THREE, simulated real turns about each phone axis, starting flat / upright / tilted, all On/Off combinations = 36 cases):
    real X +20 -> model 20 deg about local X, real Z +20 -> 20 deg about local Y (the model's visual Z), real Y +20 -> 20 deg about local -Z (visual Y); an axis switched off does nothing;
    Scale 0.8 -> 16 deg; a negative Scale reverses that axis. NOT tested on a real phone. If an axis moves the wrong way on a given phone, make its Scale negative.
  - IK POSING TWISTING THE HAND. Report: hand right under the phone, fingers spread, targets should be easy, but turning IK on twists hand and arm into an unnatural pose; "maybe the IK is factoring in
    some rotation we aren't". Checked: (1) not a regression of today's work -- the same state on the code from before my HANDYSET changes (2f51703) gives pair 1 rot 128.9 deg, wrist rotation -42,
    splay -63, elbow side bend 27; (2) with the newest saved settings and the current code: pos 1.15 / 2.19 / 5.54 u but rot 117.8 / 3.3 / 21.4 deg (pair 1 thumb pad vs phone node), wrist splay -30 -> -63,
    wrist bend -16 (its limit), wrist rotation -16, forearm twist -18, elbow bend 19; (3) with the orientation term switched off the three pairs all reach 0.00 u and the pose barely moves
    (wrist bend -15, splay -39, elbow bend -3). So the "rotation" IK is factoring in is each pair's ORIENTATION target (the source node's axes x the pair's local rotation), weighted by the constant
    IK_ROT_WEIGHT = 8 (position units per radian). The hand-pad node axes and the phone-node axes have no common convention, so one pair can ask for 120-170 deg of twist.
    Fix: the constant is now cfg.ikRotationWeight, a slider "IK Rotation Weight (x)" (0..20, step 0.5, default 8 = unchanged behaviour) at the top of the IK POSING group; 0 = position only. Included in
    ikSignature so moving it re-solves. MEASURED with the slider on the same state: 8 -> as before; 1 -> pos 0.72 / 1.10 / 0.61 u, rot 137 / 72 / 52, splay -63; 0 -> pos 0.00 / 0.00 / 0.00 u, rot not matched, natural pose.
    Not done: a per-pair "match rotation" switch; changing the default (the saved settings have no value for the new slider, so it starts at 8 until it is moved and Synced).
  - Both pushed from a temporary clean checkout of origin/main (the working tree holds uncommitted glb/blend changes that the user's own re-imports at 05:13-05:14 overwrote upstream).

2026-10-09 -- Mobile / Landscape "Independent from Desktop" now actually drives the model on a phone, for every wired setting (f698309) [written by a HANDO session at the user's request: "when i select the checkbox next to settings in Desktop, and then make sure the same in Mobile, the model is still dictated by the desktop sliders. I was seeing this in recursive render scale, and phone model offset. make sure all settings work correctly"]
  - Cause: every control wired through wireSlider / wireCheckbox / wireSelect / wireColor listened only to its DESKTOP element. A Mobile / Landscape row marked independent keeps its value in
    devPanel.js's own store and its own cloned element (ids like sliderMobilePhoneModelOffsetX), which nothing in main.js read -- so on a phone the app ran on the Desktop value whatever the Mobile tab
    said. Earlier fixes covered single families only (texture offset / scale via cfg.<key>Mobile, the Phone Displace / Rotation family via PHONE_DEVICE_AWARE_ID).
  - Fix (src/main.js): the four wire* helpers register their own apply callback in deviceAwareApply (desktop id -> callback taking a plain value). A generic bridge (applyDeviceAwareControl /
    applyAllDeviceAwareControls) runs that SAME callback (cfg write + side effects such as applyPhoneModelTransform or a render-target rebuild) with the runtime device row's value
    (getRuntimeDeviceSuffix(): Mobile in portrait, Landscape sideways). The row mirrors Desktop unless independent, so non-independent controls behave as before; nothing is written to the Desktop
    element, so Sync still saves the Desktop value as the Desktop value. PHONE_DEVICE_AWARE_ID controls are skipped (own bridge). Desktop (non-touch) runtime is unchanged; going back to a desktop
    runtime / orientation puts the Desktop (or other device) value back.
  - When it re-applies: after the startup settings restore (700 ms and 3 s), delegated on every input / change of a Desktop or device row, after clicks inside the dev panel (Sync / Reset / Undo /
    checkbox clicks, 150 ms), on resize / orientationchange (300 ms), and FORCED after anything that rewrites cfg without events -- syncPairsFromCfg() (Set-as-Default pose / camera / lighting /
    toon presets and preset Use) and runHandFirstLoadTail() -- since those silently put the Desktop value back.
  - Texture offset / scale (wireDeviceSliderMirror): syncDeviceMirrorSlots() now sets cfg.<key>Mobile / <key>Landscape from the device row if it exists, else from Desktop. Before, a value restored
    from the saved settings never reached cfg (row said -0.01, cfg stayed 0) and a phone with no row kept a hard-coded default instead of following Desktop.
  - MEASURED (preview, mobile emulation, scratch settings): the generic bridge covered 249 of 302 wired controls (the rest have their own bridge or are dev-panel chrome / debug); startup restore,
    live edit of a Mobile row, Desktop edit while independent, Landscape revert (resize event dispatched by hand -- the emulator does not fire it) and portrait return all gave the expected values;
    after the mirror-slot change cfg.screenTextureOffsetXMobile = -0.01 (the saved Mobile row) at startup, Landscape followed Desktop, scale slots with no row followed Desktop. No console errors.
  - Not covered / caveats: group-title toggles (e.g. RECURSIVE RENDER On / Off, makeDevGroupToggleable) have no Mobile row, so they cannot be device-independent through this mechanism; the Mouse Log
    debug controls are not bridged; Reset / Undo restores rely on the click-scheduled (150 ms) re-apply; NOT tested on a real phone / the live site. Reload the live page before Syncing so a stale
    open page does not overwrite the saved settings.
  - Pushed from a temporary clean checkout of origin/main (the working tree still holds uncommitted glb / blend changes).
2026-10-09 -- Rotation / Displace Reset is independent of the Responsive On/Off

Request: "when I turn off responsive rotation or responsive displacement, I want the double tap to reset to still work if turned on."
Checked first: the gesture (setupPhoneRotationResetGesture / firePhoneResetGesture) only ever gated on the two Reset checkboxes, never on
Responsive Rotation / Displace, so the logic itself was already independent. Three real gaps around it, fixed in main.js (?v=235):
 1. On-demand rendering: with both responsive features off nothing keeps the render loop alive, so the reset's visible snap was never
    drawn -> firePhoneResetGesture() now calls requestRender().
 2. iOS delivers orientation/motion only after a permission request from a user gesture, and with both features off none was made, so a
    reset could not capture a live baseline -> the double tap itself now calls requestMotionPermissionIfNeeded().
 3. Switching Responsive Rotation back ON re-baselined unconditionally (checkbox and the device-aware Mobile/Landscape row), erasing the
    neutral pose set by a double tap while it was off -> phoneRotationBaselineSetWhileOff is set by a reset made while Responsive
    Rotation is off, and onPhoneResponsiveRotationEnabled() keeps that baseline instead of re-baselining (cleared when it is consumed or
    when the checkbox is turned off).
Not browser-verified (project rule; the gesture needs a real phone).

2026-10-09 -- Dev panel opens on the tab that matches the device

Request: "if I open the app in dev mode on my mobile phone, it defaults to the mobile tab. And when I open it on my desktop, it defaults to
the desktop tab." ensureDevPanelBuilt() (devPanel.js ?v=21) now calls switchDevPanelTab() once, right after the build: a touch device
whose PRIMARY pointer is coarse (matchMedia '(pointer: coarse)') opens on Mobile -- or on Landscape when held sideways (innerWidth >
innerHeight; the Landscape tab is the sideways-phone profile) -- and everything else, including a touch-screen laptop, opens on Desktop.
Runs once at build, so a later Sync/Reset/Undo (which never changes the active tab) cannot fight your own tab clicks. Not browser-verified
(project rule; it needs a real phone).

2026-10-09 -- Accidental Sync from a stale page dropped the 3 copied per-model snapshots; restored

The page that was open on the phone/browser had loaded BEFORE the previous entry's copy of the Iphone17MaxPro / Pixel 9A snapshots, so its two
Syncs (19:31:09 and 19:32:32) wrote its in-memory hiddenPhoneModelPerModelSettings, which had no Iphone17MaxProFlip / Iphone17MaxProFlip2 / Pixel 9AF
keys -- a Sync replaces the whole string. Diffed the commit before the Syncs against the result: those 3 keys were the ONLY per-model loss;
the other changes in those Syncs (toon colors/steps, pose sliders) are the user's own live tuning and were kept. Fix: re-added the three snapshots
as copies of the CURRENT Iphone17MaxPro (x2) and Pixel 9A snapshots (settings file round-tripped exactly before and after). The page that synced is
still stale: it must be reloaded before the next Sync or it will drop them again. (Same class as the earlier "reload before you Sync" warnings:
any settings written to git by something other than a page Sync are lost by a Sync from a page that loaded before them.)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2026-10-09 -- Recursive Render per-level Scale Min / Max + Curve (and every range-bar / curve widget) now take effect when edited

Report: "the texture min max scale slider and curve editor doesn't seem to do anything. It seems like the singular texture scale slider dictates the scale."
Checked first on the LIVE site: with the saved range 0.8-0.95 and a falling curve, the final-display texture's repeat.y was 0.95694 = 1/(1.1*0.95) and
the previous pass's 0.96591 = 1/(1.1*0.9412) -- exactly computeScreenLevelScale()'s values, so the transform math and the restore were fine. The loss was in
WHEN edits are picked up. Two causes, both in the on-demand render loop:
 1. The generic range-bar / curve-editor widgets only set their hidden input's value (no input/change event), and nothing requested a frame, so dragging
    them drew nothing (the plain Texture Scale slider calls requestRender() itself, so it appeared to be the only thing that worked).
 2. curveWidgetResyncs -- what copies the widget value into cfg and re-parses it -- ran at the END of renderOneFrame(), AFTER renderVirtualScreen(), so any
    frame that did happen still drew with the OLD range/curve and the new values only landed on a later frame.
Fix (main.js ?v=237): curveWidgetResyncs now runs at the START of renderOneFrame(); and any pointer interaction inside the dev panel (held-button move,
pointerdown/up, click, dblclick, contextmenu, wheel, keyup) requests a frame, so widgets that forget requestRender() are covered too (hovering without a
button costs nothing). This also applies to every other polled widget (Rotation / Displace range+curve, Wrist Splay, Arm Length, Base Arm Rotation, Pose
Tween). Note on feel: the per-level scale multiplies down the whole chain (each pass samples the previous one), so with 18 levels small changes compound.
Not browser-verified (project rule).
